| **`InfoStacklessBVH_stacklessSelf_kernel`** | 4.9 % tumbler / 3.8 % cwc / 6.3 % rwb — present everywhere, so a win here transfers to all five scenes | `mas-bunny` has no self-contact; gate on rwb (cv unknown, measure it) + tumbler |
| **MAS inner loop** (`schwarz_local_solve_rowdot`, `build_multi_level_R`) | 52,294 launches per tumbler run at 13.7 µs and 6.8 µs; 8.6 % of the tumbler, 12.0 % of cwc. Launch count scales with PCG iterations, which are 14 % noisy — measure per launch, not per run. | `mas-bunny` (deterministic) is the right instrument |
| **`Spmv_rbk_sym_spmv_dot_chunked_kernel`** | 5.8 % / 50,755 launches at 33.9 µs (tumbler) vs 31.3 µs (cwc) vs 9.9 µs (rwb) — the per-launch cost tracks the matrix size, so this is a grid-size-against-machine-size candidate that should *grow* on a bigger GPU | mas-bunny + cwc |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

**From s02:**

- **`stiff-gipc-case2` is a third shell-bending scene and nobody had noticed.** With
  `UIPC_DSB_GN_VERIFY=1` (which prints only if the hinge kernel actually launched) it compares
  10.6 M output words over 10 frames, i.e. the kernel runs there. It is 250 frames, its
  `ms_per_newton` cv is ~0.3 % over 5 runs, and its Newton count moved 0.24 % across an arm change
  that was worth −6.65 % of wall — **it is the tightest bending instrument on this box**, tighter
  than `cube-wall-cloth`. The round record's bending pair is really a trio. `mas-bunny` and
  `rigid-wrecking-balls` were re-checked the same way and genuinely launch it **zero** times.
- **The PSD projection the s14/s19 chain was optimising keeps rank 4 of 9.** Measured over 200 000
  randomised hinges: the exact 12x12 hinge Hessian is indefinite in 100 % of samples and its PSD
  projection retains exactly four positive directions every time. Three rounds of work made a rank-4
  approximation cheaper; a rank-1 one was free. Worth checking whether the *other* `make_spd` call
  sites (contact, ortho potential, membrane) are similarly close to a closed form.
- **An extra kernel in a `-rdc=true` translation unit re-rolls ptxas's register allocation for one
  unrelated instantiation in that TU.** Two builds of identical source moved a different cold
  instantiation each time (`Proj0 Solver0`, then `Proj1 Solver0`), always with the FP64 stream
  unchanged and only BRA/BSSY targets and LDL/STL widths differing. s01's lesson ("diff the binary
  before you measure") therefore needs a corollary: **diff the instantiation your A/B actually uses,
  and expect cold siblings to jitter.**
- **Peak process GPU memory on the tumbler fell 1 793 -> 1 279 MiB** purely from the smaller stack
  frame (3 104 -> 1 248 B/thread at 27 200 resident threads). Local-memory backing store is a
  first-order VRAM consumer for these 255-register kernels, which matters on the 8 GB card.

**From s01 — three more hinge kernels that the round-4/round-5 levers never reached (§4 again):**

- `strain_plastic_discrete_shell_bending.cu:128` and `stress_plastic_discrete_shell_bending.cu:128`
  still call the generic `make_spd(H12x12)` — **neither s14's translation-free 9x9 projection nor
  s19's tridiagonal-QL solver**, both of which apply verbatim (the same dihedral-angle-only energy).
  Round 4's post-s14 candidate (a) said exactly this and it was never done. Static evidence: 6 912 B
  and 6 944 B of stack frame at 255 registers, against the plain hinge's 3 104 B. Measured analogue
  on the plain hinge, this box, full runs: the dense 12x12 solve they still run is **+81.0 %**, and
  `Solver = 0` on top of that another **+19.4 %**.
- `dahl_friction_discrete_shell_bending.cu:157/159/161` call the projections with the **default
  `Solver = 0`** — s19's QL solver was never forwarded there either, although that file is where
  round 3 *built* the K7/K16 projection. 255 registers, **11 704 B of stack frame**, the largest in
  the family. None of these three constitutions appears in the five benchmark scenes, so no benchmark
  would ever have caught it; `uipc_test_sim_case` cases 33/82–86 cover them for correctness.

- `libuipc-samples` already contains a `94_robot_hand_grasp_apple`, so the new scene took
  `95_`. The round brief's path (`94_tumbler_garments`) would have collided.
- `nsys stats` on a 180-frame tumbler run produces a **400 MB** `_cuda_gpu_trace.csv`
  (899k kernel rows). `cuda_gpu_kern_sum` alone is 100 kB and is all the ranking needs —
  future steps should drop the trace report unless they need per-launch timelines.

## s03 — the contact barrier's PSD projection has a closed form, and three rounds have been optimising an eigen-solve around it

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-13-round6-s03/` (`README.md` maps the
pre-rename A/B labels onto the shipped switch; `gn_contact_probe.{cu,txt}`,
`kernel_scope_*.txt`, `sass_identity.txt`, `scene_executes_kernel.txt`,
`verify_audit_ab.txt`, `gate*.txt`, `ab/`, `nsys/`).

### The brief's premise, checked first — and it is only half right

The brief asked whether s02's Gauss-Newton argument generalises to contact. It does,
**and more than the brief expected in one direction and less in another.**

*The kernel first.* `do_assemble_kernel` is not one kernel — **six** source files define a
kernel by that name, and nsys demangles the templated ones apart. Re-ranked at this round's
head:

| kernel | rigid-wrecking-balls | tumbler-garments |
|---|---|---|
| normal contact **part 1** (PT+EE) `<0,1,1,1,0>` | **19.5 %**, 1512 µs/launch | **12.9 %**, 2009 µs/launch |
| normal contact **part 2** (PE+PP) `<0,2,0,0,1>` | **11.7 %**, 996 µs | **5.4 %**, 831 µs |
| simplex **friction** `<0>` | 3.8 %, 323 µs | 2.7 %, 417 µs |
| **contact assembly, total** | **35.0 %** of 3 725 ms | **21.0 %** of 24 161 ms |

So the brief's "20.1 % / 37.2 % single kernel" is three kernels, and **part 1 alone is the
largest single kernel in both scenes**.

*Is the projection the cost?* Yes, overwhelmingly. A stage stub that writes the exact
Hessian **unprojected** (`UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1=4`, not a valid simulation path) on
rigid-wrecking-balls: **part 1 1512 → 389 µs/launch (−74.3 %)**, part 2 996 → 567 (−43.0 %).
s25 measured 59.5 % for part 1 in round 5 and then cut the projection's absolute cost by
15.4 %; it is still three quarters of the kernel. The stub also doubles the solver's launch
count (473 → 746), which is the cheapest possible demonstration that the projection is doing
real work.

*The friction family is already finished.* `friction_make_spd` collapses the 12x12 / 9x9 /
6x6 frictional Hessian to a **2x2 closed form** (`J J^T = ||w||^2 I_2`, so clamping commutes
with the scaled isometry), and two of the three branches of `friction_hessian` do not project
at all. There is nothing for this step to do there, and the brief's "friction may already be
handled" is correct.

### The algebra, and the correction that makes it work

`PT_/EE_/PE_/PP_barrier_gradient_hessian` literally assemble

    H = B''(D) gradD gradD^T + B'(D) hessD

with `D` the **squared** distance. Two measured facts (`gn_contact_probe.cu`, 200 000
randomised samples per pair type, run **on the device against the real `__device__`
functions** — they are `__device__`-only, so s02's host probe shape is not available):

1. **`B'' >= 0` and `B' <= 0` over the whole active domain and beyond it**, for both the
   plain and the thickness-shifted Stiff-GIPC log² barrier: `min B''/kappa = 9.3e-07`
   over 180 000 active samples and `1.1e-06` over 20 000 deliberately inactive ones,
   **0 negative**. Structurally: `B'' = 2kL² + 4k(2D−2d̂²)L/D − 2k(D−d̂²)²L/D² + 2k(D−d̂²)²/D²`
   with `L = log(D/d̂²) < 0` and `D − d̂² < 0` on the active domain, so **each of the four
   terms is non-negative there**. The first term of `H` is therefore PSD and all of the
   indefiniteness is in `B' hessD` — s02's argument, verbatim.
2. **The term that gets dropped is not small.** `max |B'| / (B'' D) = 0.9651`. Dropping it
   outright — the plain Gauss-Newton Hessian `c = B''` — **over-stiffens contact by a factor
   1.74** (`mean lambda_max(H_gn)/lambda_max(H_proj) = 1.7394 / 1.7412 / 1.7411 / 1.7418` for
   PT / EE / PE / PP) and leaves `||H_proj − H_gn||/||H_proj|| = 0.74`. Against s02's hinge,
   where the same ratio was 0.06–0.24, this is a far coarser approximation, and it measures
   like one (below).

**The correction costs one FMA.** Because `D` is a squared distance, `ghat^T hessD ghat =
|gradD|²/(2D)` — exactly for PP, to leading order for PE/PT/EE — so the rank-1 matrix

    H_spd ~= c gradD gradD^T,   c = B''(D) + B'(D) / (2 D)

reproduces the projected Hessian's leading eigenvalue exactly. `c >= 0` follows from
fact 2: `2 B'' D + B' >= (2 − 0.9651) B'' D > 0` (it is clamped anyway).

**Measured against the shipped projection, per pair type:**

| | PT | EE (un-mollified) | PE | PP |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `(H_exact − H_rank1) − B' hessD`, relative | 5.3e-07 | 2.7e-13 | 2.0e-13 | 3.5e-16 |
| exact `H` indefinite | 100 % | 100 % | 100 % | 100 % |
| **rank the shipped projection keeps** | **3 of 12** | **3 of 12** | **2 of 9** | **1 of 6** |
| `‖H_proj − c gg^T‖/‖H_proj‖`, mean / max | 1.8e-4 / 1.6e-1 | 2.7e-4 / 4.3e-1 | 9.8e-5 / 1.9e-2 | **4.8e-16 / 1.5e-15** |
| `lambda_max` ratio, mean / min | 1.000000 / 0.99595 | 1.000000 / 0.99577 | 1.000000 / 0.99981 | **1.000000 / 1.000000** |
| `H_rank1` exactly symmetric, never indefinite | yes, 0 / 200 000 | yes | yes | yes |

**This is the finding of the step.** The PSD eigen-solve that K10 (round 3), s16/s17
(round 4) and s25/s31 (round 5) have been making progressively cheaper produces, to four or
five digits, a matrix with a **closed form** — and for point-point it produces that matrix
*exactly*, to 1.5e-15. s02 found the hinge's projection was keeping rank 4 of 9; the contact
projection keeps 3 of 12, 2 of 9 and 1 of 6, and the leading direction carries 99.98 % of it.

### What was measured, and why the default did not move

Five modes behind one template parameter (`UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1`, default **0** = exact):

| mode | scope (ms per launch) | end-to-end |
|---|---|---|
| **`=1`, PE + PP** | part 2 **−43.4 %** (rwb) / **−39.4 %** (tumbler); scene GPU kernel time −8.9 % / −5.8 % | cube-wall **−2.53 % ms/Newton, disjoint, p=1.0e-06**; case2 −0.76 %; tumbler −4.37 % (unresolvable); rwb −0.15 %; no regression |
| `=2`, all four branches | part 1 −16.5 %, part 2 −47.5 % (tumbler) | cube-wall −7.66 % disjoint, but **rwb +4.75 % disjoint**, tumbler scene GPU time **+8.5 %** |
| `=3`, plain Gauss-Newton | part 1 −16.5 %, part 2 −36.1 % | tumbler scene GPU time **+8.5 %**, energy family +23.8 %, BVH/CCD +15.8 % |
| `=4`, stage stub | part 1 −74.3 %, part 2 −43.0 % | invalid path: launches 473 → 746 |

**Mode 2 and 3 are rejected on rigid-wrecking-balls and on the tumbler.** The rwb regression
is the clearer of the two: **+4.75 % mean and +5.23 % ms/Newton, both disjoint at n=5**,
against that scene's null envelope — measured here for the first time, two identical arms,
n=5 — of **±2.10 % on the mean and ±0.90 % on ms/Newton-it, overlapping, p >= 0.15**. Its
mechanism is visible in the family table: the kernels the switch *touches* get 14–23 %
cheaper while **untouched** ones get more expensive (tumbler, mode 3: BVH/CCD +15.8 %,
spmv +17.8 %, energy +23.8 %) — a coarser Hessian lands the iterate in configurations with
more contact pairs and more narrow-phase work. In rwb the gain is small to begin with,
because most EE pairs take the **mollified** branch, whose Hessian is a function of two
scalars (the mollifier argument and `D`) and so keeps the exact path and the K10 9x9
projection: part 1 moves only −5 % there against −16.5 % on the tumbler.

**Mode 1 is a real but small win that was still not adopted.** Everything about it is
positive — the targeted kernel is −39 to −43 % per launch, the scene's whole GPU kernel time
is −5.8 to −8.9 %, no scene regresses, the correctness gate is identical in every arm, the
tumbler's `--verify` audit passes on every run — and yet the honest reading is:

- the wall win is **0.8 % to 3.2 %** depending on the scene, and
- **Newton counts drift +1 to +2 % in six of seven measurements** (cube-wall +0.08 / +3.23 /
  +1.56 %, case2 +0.33 %, tumbler +0.94 / +4.45 %, rwb −2.68 %). Not one of those is
  disjoint, so none of them can be *claimed* — but none of them is a decrease either, and
  the drift is **the same size as the effect**. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says that makes the wall
  number unreadable, and the harness's guard fired on that ground on four of the runs.
- one cube-wall-cloth run in fifteen under mode 1 needed **Newton 576** against a 496–526
  band everywhere else. It did not recur over the next eight runs, but it is the shape of
  failure an approximated contact Hessian is supposed to have, and one sample is not a rate.

So: the code is committed with the default **off**. A coordinator who wants the 1–3 % can
flip it on this evidence; the better move is the next step below, which carries no
approximation at all.

### Gates

- **Correctness**: `gate.sh` identical to `baseline_tests.txt` — assertion counts 11/3,
  1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped — in **all
  three arms** (`=0`, `=1`, `=2`). `sim_case`'s 95 cases include the contact suite.
- **Non-penetration and containment, not just wall time** (the brief's specific demand).
  Tumbler `--verify`, 180 frames, 3 runs per arm, one build: `verify_ok` true in every run of
  every arm; contained radially (r_max 0.29856 → 0.29862 against the 0.30227 limit) and
  axially; `lifter_depth_max` −1.19 mm (never enters a lifter); `tri_height_min` 3.62 mm
  against the 3.27 mm `2r + d_hat` floor; **0 non-converged frames, 0 `hit_newton_limit`,
  0 `hit_line_search_limit`** across all nine runs. The statistic that does move is
  `verify_area_ratio_max` 1.251 [1.201, 1.333] → 1.308 [1.199, 1.377] (mode 1) →
  **1.448 [1.316, 1.680]** (mode 2) — the same membrane-stretch statistic s02 flagged, and it
  scales with how much of the Hessian the mode approximates.
- **Numerics**: energy and the Hessian assembler's inputs are untouched; the Hessian is
  **not** identical and is not claimed to be — the table above is the full accounting. The
  gradient is computed by the same two statements in both paths; measured relative to the
  gradient's own magnitude it agrees to 0 (EE, PP) and 3.1e-13 (PE), with PT reaching 1.4e-7
  **at the active-set boundary only**, where `B' -> 0` and the normalisation is by a quantity
  going to zero. This is *not* the bit-identity s02 proved, and it is not claimed as such.
- **Old arm reproduces main's binary**: 52 of 53 SASS instruction streams in the translation
  unit identical, including **both instantiations the benchmark scenes launch**
  (`<0,1,1,1,0>` 30 952 instr, `<0,2,0,0,1>` 12 304 instr). The one that moves is a cold
  fused-kernel sibling no benchmark path launches, at an unchanged FP64+MUFU count of 8 107 —
  s02's whole-TU register-allocation re-roll, again.
- **Control validity, checked rather than assumed** (PERF_METHOD §2.2). Over **full** runs:
  cube-wall-cloth launches part 1 and part 2 **361 times each** (2097 and 1391 µs/launch — it
  is the *strongest* instrument for this step, not a control); `mas-bunny` launches them
  **21 times** of 402 Newton iterations, so it is a **weak** control, not a vacuous one, and
  its −0.15 % is worth exactly that much. A 20-frame probe of cube-wall-cloth showed **zero**
  launches, which is why this check was run over the full 100 frames — a short window would
  have declared the round's best instrument a no-op.

### Transfer prediction

Mode 1 is **algorithmic — it transfers**: it deletes `point_edge_distance2_hessian`,
`point_point_distance2_hessian` and two reduced eigen-solves per pair, at an unchanged launch
geometry. What will not carry over is the *scene* percentage: part 2 is 11.7 % of rwb and
5.4 % of tumbler GPU kernel time **on cc 7.5**, and PERF_METHOD §6 records that a kernel's
share moves several points between architectures. The 1/32-rate FP64 of this box
over-rewards the removed `hessD` arithmetic specifically, so −39 to −43 % per launch is an
upper estimate for a part with better FP64 throughput. The iteration-count drift is a
property of the search direction and is **architecture-independent** — it will be there on
any GPU.

### Instrument re-measurement after V1 (coordinator) — and a correction to the tumbler's noise floor

V1 found that **s00's shell had left a `nvidia-smi` poll loop running at 0.4 s intervals for 6 h 23 m**
— under every round-6 measurement, s00's own noise floor included. (The step's heredoc lost its
newlines, so its `kill $POLL` never ran.) The coordinator killed it; the GPU now idles at 299 MiB.

A third null A/B on the post-s03 head, poller gone, n=5, ABBA + warm-up:

| null A/B | meanFrameMs | medianFrameMs | ms/Newton-it | Newton |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| #1 (ABAB, no warm-up, poller live) | −4.94 % | −7.26 % | −5.52 % | +0.53 % |
| #2 (ABBA + warm-up, poller live) | −1.02 % | −4.28 % | −1.31 % | +0.25 % |
| **#3 (ABBA + warm-up, poller killed)** | **−3.25 %** | −3.06 % | **−5.32 %** | +2.24 % |

**Killing the poller did not tighten the scene.** Its scatter is intrinsic: Newton counts alone range
1383–1550 (±6 %) between identical arms. Two corrections follow, both of which override s00:

- **The tumbler's null envelope is ±3–5 %, not ±1 %.** Three independent nulls put |Δ| at 4.94 / 1.02
  / 3.25 % on the mean. **Claim no end-to-end tumbler effect below ~6 %**, and treat the second null's
  −1.02 % as the lucky draw it was rather than the scene's resolution.
- **`ms_per_newton` is not the tumbler's tight statistic.** s00 reported cv 2.05 % for it; across the
  three nulls it moves −5.52 / −1.31 / −5.32 %, i.e. no better than the mean and sometimes worse,
  because this scene's Newton counts are themselves part of the chaos. It stays the right statistic on
  `cube-wall-cloth` (±0.54 %) and `stiff-gipc-case2` (~0.3 %), which is where sub-2 % work belongs.

### Correction to s02's ledger row, from V1's n=10 measurement

s02 reported iteration counts as "flat". At n=10 with a perturbed-exact control, V1 measured the
Gauss-Newton direction costing **+3.2 % Newton and +5.5 % PCG** on the tumbler (replicated at
+4.2 / +4.0 %). Against a −18.6 % wall change it does not alter the verdict, and the −90 % per-launch
scope gate is unaffected — but the direction is **not free**, and the row overstated it. The honest
statement is: fewer, cheaper hinge Hessians, slightly more of them needed.

## Measurement doctrine after s11 (coordinator) — what this round has retired

Three instruments have now been disqualified for concurrency work, each by a measurement rather than
by argument. Recorded together because a later round will otherwise reach for them:

| instrument | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| `nsys stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` scene totals | **retired for scene totals** (s10) | a duration *sum*: it grows every time the round adds overlap. rwb sum/union went 1.060 (pre-K9) → 1.075 (s09) → **1.130** (s10). s10's winning arm reads **+0.9 %** here and −5.2 % by union. Still valid per kernel |
| GPU **busy union** from `cuda_gpu_trace` | **retired for ranking placements** (s11) | Spearman **rho = +0.10** against wall across five arms — it gave its *best* score to the *worst* arm. Per-arm sd 0.3–1.7 % against arms 4–9 pp apart, so this is not noise |
| repo `WB_TIMER=1` Timer tree | **unusable for any concurrency question** (s11) | `sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ wait_device(); })`, so every scope boundary synchronises the device — destroying the overlap and host run-ahead the arms differ in. Nothing at the call site says so |

**What remains for a step that changes where work is issued: end-to-end wall time, n large, through
`ab.py`.** Per-kernel `kern_sum` numbers stay valid for steps that change a kernel's own cost.

### Consequences for what the round already believes

- **s10's win stands and is confirmed at higher n**: s11 re-measured it at n=30 without a profiler at
  **−4.97 % ms/Newton (p=2.3e-21)**. What does *not* stand is s10's *ranking* of the placements — at
  n=8 it read `=2` as better and its trace called `=2` "does not work"; at n=30 modes 1, 2 and 4 are
  mutually indistinguishable on rwb (Welch p = 0.59–0.95). The placement does not matter on that scene.
- **s09's split conclusion is unaffected** — it compared union *and* wall and both agreed there.
- **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` no longer measures the split alone**, because modes 3/4 of the
  prepass only fire when the K9 split is taken. Pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms.

### And a 1-in-20 accident, caught live

s11's `stiff-gipc-case2` control — a scene that launches **zero** ABD G/H kernels — read **−0.46 %,
p=0.034, "DISJOINT" at n=4**, and reversed to +0.12 %, p=0.58 at n=6. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says a round of
twenty measurements manufactures one of these; this is the round's, on a scene where the effect is
known to be exactly zero.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **PP alone, which is not an approximation at all** | the closed form reproduces the shipped PP projection to **4.8e-16 mean / 1.5e-15 max** relative Frobenius error over 140 470 samples, with `lambda_max` ratio 1.000000 exactly — it is a **rounding-level** change, so it carries **none** of the iteration-count question that held this step back. Part 2 (PE+PP together) is −43.4 % / −39.4 % ms/launch and 11.7 % / 5.4 % of GPU kernel time. **First measure the PE:PP split** — that is what sets the ceiling, and nothing in the round knows it yet | cube-wall-cloth (361 part-2 launches/run, null ±0.54 %) + case2 (cv 0.3 %); counts are not at risk, so `ms_per_newton` is legitimate |
| **The mollified EE branch is a two-scalar function and nobody has used that** | `E = ek(c) B(D)` with `c` the mollifier argument, so `H = J^T M_2 J + ek_c hess(c) + ek B' hessD` with `J = [grad c; grad D]` (2x12) and `M_2` a **2x2** — the same shape `friction_make_spd` already exploits to collapse 12x12 to 2x2. s25 measured the mollified 9x9 projection at **50.6 pp of part 1** on the wrecking balls, and it is why mode 2 wins only 5 % of part 1 there against 16.5 % on the tumbler | rwb (part 1 is 19.5 % of its GPU time); needs its own numerics probe for the two dropped `hess` terms |
| **Contact part 1's projection is still ~74 % of the largest kernel in the suite** | stage stub, rwb: 1512 → 389 µs/launch. After s25's −15.4 % it is *still* three quarters of the kernel. Anything that removes it and keeps the search direction is worth ~15 % of rwb and ~9 % of the tumbler | rwb + tumbler scope; the direction question is what mode 2 failed |
| **`InfoStacklessBVHSimplexTrajectoryFilter_filter_toi_*` and `pairFilter`** | unchanged from s02's list and now relatively larger: BVH/CCD is **9 634 ms of 24 161 = 39.9 %** of the tumbler at this head, the single biggest family by a wide margin. Every arm of this step moved it 1–16 %, i.e. it is also the family most sensitive to trajectory | tumbler at n>=8 on `ms_per_newton`; rwb null is now known (±2.10 % / ±0.90 %) |
| ~~the plain Gauss-Newton contact Hessian~~ | **done and rejected — s03 mode 3.** 1.74x over-stiffening, +8.5 % of tumbler GPU kernel time, +4.75 % end-to-end on rwb. Do not retry it; retry the *corrected* coefficient instead | — |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`rigid-wrecking-balls`'s null envelope, measured for the first time** (n=5, two identical
  arms, ABBA + warm-up): **±2.10 % on `meanFrameMs` (p=0.15), ±0.90 % on `ms_per_newton`
  (p=0.19), ±1.22 % on `ms_per_pcg`**, with Newton itself moving 1.20 % under the null. It is
  a *better* instrument than the tumbler and a worse one than cube-wall-cloth. The round
  record should carry it beside the tumbler's ±1 % and cube-wall's ±0.54 %.
- **`mas-bunny` is a weak control for anything in the simplex contact family, not a strong
  one.** It launches the normal-contact assemble kernel **21 times per run** against 402
  Newton iterations (0.1 % of GPU time); its ground contact is `vertex_half_plane`, a
  different model entirely. It is not vacuous — the round-5 sin — but a flat number from it
  is nearly uninformative for contact work.
- **A 20-frame window of `cube-wall-cloth` shows zero contact-assembly launches**; over the
  full 100 frames it shows 361 per part at 2097 and 1391 µs. PERF_METHOD §2.12 ("prefer the
  full run") has a corollary for *control validity*, not just for cost: a short window can
  declare a scene a no-op for a subsystem it actually dominates.
- **`do_assemble_kernel` is six different kernels.** Six source files under
  `contact_system/` define a `__global__` by that name in an anonymous namespace; three of
  them run in the benchmark scenes, and any ranking that quotes "do_assemble_kernel at N %"
  without the template arguments is summing unrelated kernels. The round-6 pick list did
  exactly that (20.1 % / 37.2 %).
- **The contact Hessian is indefinite in 100 % of samples, for every pair type.** Whatever
  replaces the projection has to project *something*; the finding here is only that what it
  projects to has a closed form.

## s04 — the CCD narrow phase evaluates a distance it never reads: an exactly-equivalent first-pass exit in ACCD

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-13-round6-s04/`
(`kernel_ranking.txt`, `ccd_stats_eo0.txt` / `ccd_stats_eo1.txt`, `ccd_earlyout_probe.{cu,txt}`,
`sass_identity.txt`, `scope_tum.txt`, `scope_cwc.txt`, `scope_rwb.txt`, `scope_c2.txt`, `filter_toi_share.txt`,
`control_mas_bunny.txt`, `gate.txt`, `gate_eo0.txt`, `verify/`, `ab/`).

### The family, re-ranked at this head (`d686bc38`), one full 180-frame tumbler run

`BVH/CCD` is **37.8 % of 24 202 ms** of tumbler GPU kernel time — still the largest family, and
the brief's 39.9 % holds to within run-to-run scatter. The useful number is the **split**, which
nothing in the round had yet:

| sub-family | ms | share of scene GPU time |
|---|---:|---:|
| **CCD narrow phase** (`filter_toi_k3` + `k4`) | **3 247** | **13.42 %** |
| BVH traversal (`stacklessSelf` + `stacklessOther`) | 2 032 | 8.39 % |
| BVH leaf filter (`pairFilter`, both instantiations) | 1 995 | 8.24 % |
| DCD narrow phase (`filter_active_k3` + `k4`) | 1 439 | 5.94 % |
| build / refit / sort / the rest | 429 | 1.77 % |

So the family is really **three** roughly equal thirds — narrow-phase CCD, tree traversal, and
leaf-predicate evaluation — and the CCD narrow phase is the largest single one. It is also the
one the official scenes have least of: `filter_toi_k3+k4` is **13.4 %** of the tumbler,
**5.8 %** of `cube-wall-cloth`, **5.8 %** of `rigid-wrecking-balls` and **1.3 %** of `mas-bunny`.

### Why the tumbler's traversals are 3–5x more expensive per launch: it is pair count, and here is the number

The brief asked whether it is pair count, tree quality, divergence or atomics. A new device-side
probe (`UIPC_CCD_STATS=1`, four counters per pair type accumulated inside the ACCD loop) answers it
directly, over the first 400 `filter_toi` launches of a tumbler run:

| | EE | PT |
|---|---:|---:|
| ACCD calls | 275 236 843 | 96 673 289 |
| **candidate pairs per launch** | **688 092** | **241 683** |
| ACCD loop passes per call | **1.035** | 1.036 |
| calls that converge onto a hit | 1 040 933 (**0.378 %**) | 371 542 (0.384 %) |

**688 000 edge-edge candidates per Newton iteration**, and 99.6 % of them cannot collide within the
step. That is the whole explanation of the per-launch cost: it is not tree quality and not
divergence inside a long traversal — the ACCD loop runs essentially **once** per pair
(1.035 passes), so every candidate costs the same fixed amount and the launch cost is linear in
the candidate count. A dense self-contacting cloth pile simply has an order of magnitude more
swept-AABB-overlapping pairs than a cube on a wall.

### The dead work, and why removing it is exact rather than conservative-with-an-argument

`distance::edge_edge_ccd` (and its PT / PE / PP siblings) is the Codim-IPC **additive CCD**
loop. One pass is:

```
if(--max_iter < 0) return true;
tocLowerBound = (1 - eta) * dFunc / ((dist_cur + thickness) * maxDispMag);
advance ea0..eb1 by tocLowerBound          // 12 FMAs
flag = edge_edge_distance_flag(...);       // the expensive part
edge_edge_distance2(flag, ...);            // ... and the rest of it
if(toc && dFunc/(dist_cur + thickness) < gap) break;      // -> hit
toc += tocLowerBound;
if(toc > toc_prev) return false;                          // -> no hit in this step
```

`toc` is set to 0 immediately before the loop, so **on the first pass the `if(toc && ...)` break is
disabled by its own guard** and the only reachable exit of that pass is `toc > toc_prev`. When
`tocLowerBound > toc_prev` already holds, the advance and the full distance re-evaluation between
the two are dead: nothing reads their results before the function returns `false` with
`toc = 0 + tocLowerBound`. Testing it where `tocLowerBound` is computed returns the same `false`
with the same `toc` — `0 + x == x` exactly in IEEE — so this is an **exact transformation of the
shipped path, not a looser filter**. It cannot drop a pair the old path would have kept, and it
cannot report a larger time of impact.

That is the entire change: `if constexpr(EarlyOut) { if(toc == T(0) && tocLowerBound > toc_prev) { toc = tocLowerBound; return false; } }`,
in all four `*_ccd` functions, behind a new `template <typename T, bool EarlyOut = false, bool Stats = false>`.
The four `filter_toi_k*` kernels of the default `info_stackless_bvh` filter are templated on the
same two flags and dispatched from `UIPC_CCD_EARLY_OUT` (default 1) and `UIPC_CCD_STATS` (default 0),
so **neither is a branch or an extra register inside the shipped kernel**. Every other caller of
these functions keeps the defaulted `EarlyOut = false` and is untouched.

With the switch on, the same probe measures **99.2–99.5 % of ACCD calls taking the early exit** —
i.e. the dead work is removed from essentially the whole candidate list.

### The old arm reproduces main's binary exactly

Standalone compile of the filter's translation unit with the build's exact flags, at
`d686bc38` and at this head, SASS-diffed per instantiation (`sass_identity.txt`):

| kernel | base instr | `<EarlyOut=0, Stats=0>` | identical to base? |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `filter_toi_k1` | 472 | 472 | **yes, byte for byte** |
| `filter_toi_k2` | 1 144 | 1 144 | **yes** |
| `filter_toi_k3` | 1 832 | 1 832 | **yes** |
| `filter_toi_k4` | 2 680 | 2 680 | **yes** |

Register/stack/local are unchanged too (`k4` REG 218, STACK 0, LOCAL 0 in both), which is what
keeps `best_block_dim`'s occupancy calculation — and therefore the launch geometry — identical
between the arms. The early-out instantiation costs **+16 SASS instructions** on `k4` and
**+8** on `k3`, at REG 218 / 184, i.e. the same occupancy.

The only other SASS movement in the TU is 1–3 `MOV Rn, <imm>` line-number constants inside
`filter_active_k1..k4` (identical instruction counts, identical FP64 counts) — the `UIPC_KERNEL_ASSERT`
source-line immediates shifted by the comment lines this step inserted. Both A/B arms run that
same binary.

### Numerics: bit-identical, proved on the device against the real functions

`ccd_earlyout_probe.cu` runs both instantiations of all four `*_ccd` functions **in the same
thread on the same inputs**, over 10^6 randomised samples per pair type — primitives 0–20 mm
apart in a 20 mm box, displacement magnitudes log-uniform over 10^-4 to 1 m, `eta` uniform on
[0.1, 0.9], thickness 0 or up to 1 mm, and one sample in four deliberately near-parallel
(EE / PE) or near-degenerate (PT):

| | EE | PT | PE | PP |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| samples | 1 000 000 | 1 000 000 | 1 000 000 | 1 000 000 |
| return-value mismatches | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |
| `toc` **bit** mismatches | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |
| a **larger** `toc` on the new path | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |
| hits on the old path (sanity: the samples do collide) | 19.47 % | 15.41 % | 11.08 % | 7.26 % |

This is the **bit-identical** level of PERF_METHOD §2.3, not the rounding level: the change does
not touch any arithmetic, it removes a computation whose result is not read. The third row is the
safety-critical one the brief asked for — the new path never reports a time of impact later than
the old one, on any sample, so it cannot let the solver take a step the old filter would have
refused.

### Performance, targeted scope: n=3 full runs per arm, one build, four scenes

`nsys --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` over **complete** benchmark runs (fresh prefix per run,
PERF_METHOD §2.7/§2.12), `UIPC_CCD_EARLY_OUT=0` vs `=1`:

| scene | `filter_toi_k4` (EE) µs/launch | `filter_toi_k3` (PT) µs/launch | worst-moving untouched kernel |
|---|---|---|---|
| **tumbler-garments** | 1465.4 -> 1146.9 **−21.7 %** | 746.1 -> 555.3 **−25.6 %** | +7.6 % (`stacklessSelf`) — the scene's own scatter, see below |
| **cube-wall-cloth** | 465.4 -> 337.9 **−27.4 %** | 253.5 -> 171.2 **−32.5 %** | **+0.80 %** |
| **rigid-wrecking-balls** | 295.3 -> 221.8 **−24.9 %** | 170.6 -> 120.1 **−29.6 %** | **−1.75 %** |
| **stiff-gipc-case2** | 1114.2 -> 829.7 **−25.5 %** | 539.7 -> 383.7 **−28.9 %** | **−0.64 %** |

Every one of those eight deltas has **disjoint** per-run ranges.

**The tumbler's untouched kernels are the round's scatter problem, not an effect of this step.**
On the tumbler, `filter_active`, both traversal kernels and both `pairFilter` instantiations all
read +4 to +8 % in the new arm, and the whole-scene GPU kernel time reads **+1.7 %** — while the
same untouched kernels move **±2.2 % at most** on the other three scenes for the same switch.
The cause is the candidate-pair population: it is the common factor in every kernel of this
family, and it differs between two runs of the tumbler by several percent. Dividing the targeted
kernel by an **untouched kernel of the same family measured in the same run** removes it:

| tumbler ratio, same run | old | new | delta | |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `filter_toi_k4 / stacklessSelf` | 1.7205 | 1.2579 | **−26.9 %** | disjoint |
| `filter_toi_k4 / pairFilter(self)` | 1.6757 | 1.2548 | **−25.1 %** | disjoint |
| `filter_toi_k4 / filter_active_k4` | 3.1321 | 2.3306 | **−25.6 %** | disjoint |
| `filter_toi_k3 / stacklessSelf` | 0.8761 | 0.6092 | **−30.5 %** | disjoint |
| `filter_toi_k3 / filter_active_k4` | 1.5951 | 1.1283 | **−29.3 %** | disjoint |

which lands on the same −25 to −33 % as the three quiet scenes. The same ratios on
`cube-wall-cloth` / `rigid-wrecking-balls` / `stiff-gipc-case2` give −24.6 to −33.0 % (eighteen
ratios, **all eighteen disjoint**), i.e. the normalisation agrees with the raw numbers on the
scenes where the raw numbers are trustworthy, and it is the statistic to trust on the tumbler.

### Transfer prediction

**Algorithmic — it transfers.** The step deletes, for 99.4 % of the candidate list, one
`*_distance_flag` + `*_distance2` evaluation and twelve FMAs per pair, at an unchanged launch
geometry, an unchanged candidate set and an unchanged output. Nothing about it depends on
occupancy, register pressure, spill traffic or wave quantisation — the categories PERF_METHOD §6
warns do not carry.

Two caveats a rented run should test:
- the removed work is FP64-heavy (a sqrt, several divides, the flag's sign tests), and this box
  runs FP64 at 1/32 rate. The *fraction* of the kernel that disappears will be smaller on a part
  with better FP64 throughput, so **−25 to −33 % per launch is an upper estimate**.
- the *scene share* of `filter_toi` will move between architectures the way every share does
  (§6): it is 13.4 % of the tumbler, 5.8–6.3 % of the three official contact scenes and 1.3 % of
  `mas-bunny` **on cc 7.5**.
- the **first-pass exit rate is a property of the workload, not the hardware**: 99.2–99.5 % on the
  tumbler, and the per-launch deltas on four very different scenes agree to within 8 points, so
  the mechanism itself should reproduce anywhere.

### Performance, end to end: n=5 per arm, ABBA + one discarded warm-up, one build

The scope gate predicts the end-to-end effect directly, because the GPU is ~100 % busy on all of
these scenes: `filter_toi_k3+k4` is **13.42 %** of tumbler GPU kernel time, **5.83 %** of
cube-wall-cloth, **5.78 %** of rigid-wrecking-balls, **6.25 %** of stiff-gipc-case2 and **1.30 %**
of mas-bunny, so a −25 to −30 % on it is worth roughly **−3.4 / −1.5 / −1.5 / −1.7 / −0.35 %** of
the frame.

**The iteration-count guard needs a different reading on this step than on s02/s03.** The change
is bit-identical: not one computed quantity anywhere in the solver differs. Newton and PCG counts
therefore *cannot* be moved by it, and any drift between the arms is the scene's own
non-determinism — which makes `ms_per_newton` the right statistic here even where the guard
fires, rather than a flattering denominator.

| scene | `filter_toi` share of GPU kernel time | predicted | `meanFrameMs` | `ms_per_newton` | Newton / PCG |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|
| **mas-bunny** | 1.30 % | -0.33 % | -0.38 % (**disjoint**, p=0.0027) | -0.38 % (**disjoint**, p=0.0027) | +0.00 % / -0.00 % |
| **cube-wall-cloth** | 5.83 % | -1.46 % | +0.64 % (overlapping, p=0.77) | -1.49 % (**disjoint**, p=0.012) | +2.16 % / +1.07 % |
| **rigid-wrecking-balls** | 5.78 % | -1.45 % | -0.95 % (overlapping, p=0.41) | -1.79 % (overlapping, p=0.038) | +0.85 % / +0.72 % |
| **stiff-gipc-case2** | 6.25 % | -1.56 % | -1.48 % (**disjoint**, p=8.2e-05) | -1.54 % (**disjoint**, p=0.00052) | +0.06 % / -0.04 % |
| **tumbler-garments** | 13.42 % | -3.35 % | -3.86 % (overlapping, p=0.43) | -1.40 % (overlapping, p=0.75) | -2.43 % / -1.86 % |

Read down the "predicted" and "measured" columns: **every scene lands where the scope gate said it
would**, and the three tightest instruments in the round resolve it.

- **`mas-bunny` is the decisive one.** It is deterministic — Newton **465 in all ten runs**, PCG
  flat to 0.00 %, line-search flat — so `meanFrameMs` *is* the throughput statistic there with no
  denominator argument at all. It reads **−0.38 %, disjoint, p=0.0027** against a predicted
  −0.33 %. Its `filter_toi` share is only 1.30 % of GPU kernel time, which is precisely why this
  is a strong result rather than a weak one: the smallest predicted effect in the suite came out
  right, on the one scene that cannot hide behind trajectory noise.
- **`stiff-gipc-case2`** (the tightest non-deterministic instrument, cv ~0.3 %): **−1.48 % on the
  mean and −1.54 % on ms/Newton, both disjoint**, with Newton **+0.06 %** and PCG **−0.04 %** —
  the iteration guard did not fire, and the prediction was −1.56 %.
- **`cube-wall-cloth`**: −1.49 % on ms/Newton, disjoint, p=0.012, against a predicted −1.46 %. Its
  `meanFrameMs` reads +0.64 % because one new-arm run drew Newton 576 against a 505–515 band; the
  guard fired on that, correctly.
- **`rigid-wrecking-balls`**: −1.79 % on ms/Newton against its own null of ±0.90 % (s03), p=0.038,
  overlapping at n=5; −0.95 % on the mean against a ±2.10 % null. Consistent with the predicted
  −1.45 %, not resolvable by itself.
- **`tumbler-garments`**, the round's target scene: **−3.86 % on the mean**, overlapping, p=0.43 —
  which is exactly what the round record says to expect. Its null envelope is ±3–5 % and the
  predicted effect is −3.35 %; the scene *cannot* resolve its own biggest single win. The scope
  gate and the four other scenes are the evidence; the tumbler contributes the sign.

**No scene regresses on any statistic.**


### Gates

- **Correctness**: `bash /workspace/output/round6/gate.sh` against `baseline_tests.txt`, in **both**
  arms of the switch. Assertion counts **identical to
  `baseline_tests.txt` on all eight suites**: 11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23,
  **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped. The only textual difference in the whole file is
  pytest's own wall-clock string (1.20 s vs 1.21 s).
- **Conservativeness, not just wall time** (the brief's specific demand for CCD). Two independent
  arguments plus two measurements:
  1. *The transformation is exact.* The hoisted test is the same comparison the old loop makes two
     statements later, on a pass where the only other exit is provably unreachable. Nothing about
     which pairs are tested changes — the candidate list is untouched — and the time-of-impact
     bound is bit-identical.
  2. *Measured against the current filter, exhaustively.* 10^6 randomised inputs per pair type,
     both instantiations in the same thread: **0 disagreements in the return value, 0 differing
     bits in `toc`, and 0 samples where the new path reports a larger `toc`** — the only direction
     that could ever produce interpenetration.
  3. *The tumbler's `--verify` audit*, 180 frames, **6 runs per arm**, one build. `verify_ok` **true in every run of every arm**; contained
     radially (`r_max` 0.29866 -> 0.29857 against the 0.30227 limit) and axially;
     `lifter_depth_max` −1.18 / −1.27 mm (the cloth never enters a lifter);
     `cc_min_dist` (cloth-cloth closest approach, V1's probe) **2.478 -> 2.505 mm**;
     `bore_gap_min` 0.793 -> 0.862 mm; **0 non-converged frames, 0 `hit_newton_limit`,
     0 `hit_line_search_limit`** in all twelve runs (n=6 per arm). The two statistics that could flag a CCD
     failure — cloth-cloth proximity and bore clearance — are *larger* in the new arm, not
     smaller.
  4. *The solver's own CCD state*: `verify_ccd_toi_min` and `verify_ccd_toi_clamped_frames` are
     the filter's output as the solver consumed it. `ccd_toi_min` 0.517 [0.330, 0.652] -> 0.471 [0.370, 0.659]
     and `ccd_toi_clamped_frames` 9.8 [5, 15] -> 9.2 [6, 14] — the filter still clamps the step
     on the same order of frames, by the same order of amount. It is not silently returning 1.0.
- **Env-switch audit at kernel level** (not wall time): `UIPC_CCD_EARLY_OUT=0` restores the old
  per-launch cost exactly, on all four scenes, in the scope table above — a 25–33 % per-launch
  difference is not something a broken switch could fake.

**One statistic to record honestly, measured to n=6 per arm.** `verify_tri_height_min` falls
below the 3.27 mm `2r + d_hat` margin s00 observed in **2 of 6 new-arm runs (2.87 and 2.72 mm)**
and **0 of 6 old-arm runs** (3.44–3.97 mm); `verify_area_ratio_min` follows it down (0.598–0.665
in those two runs against 0.78–0.88 everywhere else). Stated plainly:

- it is **not resolvable** at this sample size — one-sided Fisher exact **p = 0.23** — and the
  *other* tail moves the other way (`area_ratio_max` reaches 1.418 on the **old** arm and 1.697 on
  the new, i.e. both arms produce single extreme runs, which is what V1 already recorded this
  statistic to be);
- it **cannot** have been caused by this step. The change alters no computed quantity — 0 differing
  bits over 4 x 10^6 samples — so the two arms are draws from the same trajectory distribution by
  construction. What it is evidence about is the *scene*: s00's `size_sweep` already found that
  the tumbler's membrane crumples triangles under the legacy moduli, and 2 runs in 12 crossing a
  margin s00 measured on a single run is a property of that, not of the CCD filter;
- every safety criterion is clean in all twelve runs — `verify_ok` true, contained radially and
  axially, `lifter_depth_max` −1.07 to −1.35 mm, **`cc_min_dist` 2.34–2.63 mm and `bore_gap_min`
  0.65–0.93 mm with the new arm's means *larger* on both**, 0 non-converged frames, 0
  `hit_newton_limit`, 0 `hit_line_search_limit`.

A later validation pass that sees the same asymmetry with more runs should read it as a finding
about the scene's membrane, and the round record should carry the margin as `tri_height_min`
2.72 mm over 12 runs rather than s00's single-run 3.85 mm.

### Measurement doctrine after s11 (coordinator) — what this round has retired

Three instruments have now been disqualified for concurrency work, each by a measurement rather than
by argument. Recorded together because a later round will otherwise reach for them:

| instrument | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| `nsys stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` scene totals | **retired for scene totals** (s10) | a duration *sum*: it grows every time the round adds overlap. rwb sum/union went 1.060 (pre-K9) → 1.075 (s09) → **1.130** (s10). s10's winning arm reads **+0.9 %** here and −5.2 % by union. Still valid per kernel |
| GPU **busy union** from `cuda_gpu_trace` | **retired for ranking placements** (s11) | Spearman **rho = +0.10** against wall across five arms — it gave its *best* score to the *worst* arm. Per-arm sd 0.3–1.7 % against arms 4–9 pp apart, so this is not noise |
| repo `WB_TIMER=1` Timer tree | **unusable for any concurrency question** (s11) | `sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ wait_device(); })`, so every scope boundary synchronises the device — destroying the overlap and host run-ahead the arms differ in. Nothing at the call site says so |

**What remains for a step that changes where work is issued: end-to-end wall time, n large, through
`ab.py`.** Per-kernel `kern_sum` numbers stay valid for steps that change a kernel's own cost.

### Consequences for what the round already believes

- **s10's win stands and is confirmed at higher n**: s11 re-measured it at n=30 without a profiler at
  **−4.97 % ms/Newton (p=2.3e-21)**. What does *not* stand is s10's *ranking* of the placements — at
  n=8 it read `=2` as better and its trace called `=2` "does not work"; at n=30 modes 1, 2 and 4 are
  mutually indistinguishable on rwb (Welch p = 0.59–0.95). The placement does not matter on that scene.
- **s09's split conclusion is unaffected** — it compared union *and* wall and both agreed there.
- **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` no longer measures the split alone**, because modes 3/4 of the
  prepass only fire when the K9 split is taken. Pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms.

### And a 1-in-20 accident, caught live

s11's `stiff-gipc-case2` control — a scene that launches **zero** ABD G/H kernels — read **−0.46 %,
p=0.034, "DISJOINT" at n=4**, and reversed to +0.12 %, p=0.58 at n=6. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says a round of
twenty measurements manufactures one of these; this is the round's, on a scene where the effect is
known to be exactly zero.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **The same first-pass exit is not wired into the other three trajectory filters, nor into `global_contact_manager.cu`** | `stackless_bvh_`, `info_stackless_bvh_v0_` and `lbvh_simplex_trajectory_filter.cu` call the four `*_ccd` functions with the defaulted `EarlyOut = false`, and so does `global_contact_manager.cu`'s toi diagnostic. They are not the shipped default method, so this step deliberately left them alone — but the change is bit-identical, so forwarding it is free. §4's pattern, one step after the helper was built | `sim_case`; the alternative methods are selectable via `collision_detection/method` |
| **The BVH leaf filter is now the same size as the traversal, and nobody has profiled it** | at this head the tumbler's BVH/CCD splits **13.4 % CCD narrow / 8.4 % traversal / 8.2 % `pairFilter` / 5.9 % DCD narrow**. `pairFilter` evaluates the *same* `edge_edge_ccd_broadphase` predicate that `filter_toi` then re-evaluates as its `faraway` test, at the same `alpha`, on the same pairs — **the `faraway` test in `filter_toi_k*` can never fire**, because `detect`'s leaf predicate already applied it. Removing it is exact but small; the interesting version is the opposite direction — make `pairFilter`'s predicate *tighter* so the candidate list shrinks | scope: `pairFilter` + `filter_toi` per launch; a tighter predicate changes the candidate set and needs the conservativeness argument this step did not |
| **688 000 EE candidates per Newton iteration, of which 0.38 % ever collide** | the `UIPC_CCD_STATS` probe. The swept-AABB broad phase over a dense pile passes three orders of magnitude more pairs than collide. A conservative *distance-based* cull — `dist_cur - thickness > toc_prev * maxDispMag / (1 - eta)` is exactly the first-pass exit this step added, and it is now the whole cost — suggests the next lever is upstream: **do not stage the pair at all**. The leaf predicate already has both primitives' positions; it could evaluate the same bound there and never write the pair | `pairFilter` + `filter_toi` scope; must be proved conservative the same way (the bound is the same one, so the proof carries) |
| **`filter_toi` re-reads `positions`/`dxs` for four vertices per pair and recomputes `thickness`/`d_hat` from four per-vertex arrays** | with `collision_detection/dcd_candidate_reuse` at its default 1, `filter_active_k4` runs over **the same candidate array** as `filter_toi_k4`. Per pair the CCD kernel cost **3.13x** the DCD one before this step and **2.33x** after it (tumbler, 3 runs/arm, disjoint) — at ~1.0 ACCD passes per pair what is left is the loads plus one distance evaluation, i.e. the kernel is load-bound rather than iteration-bound | tumbler + `stiff-gipc-case2` (6.25 % of it, cv 0.3 %) |
| ~~`InfoStacklessBVHSimplexTrajectoryFilter_filter_toi_*`~~ | **done — s04.** −25 to −33 % per launch on four scenes, bit-identical | — |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`filter_toi_k*`'s `faraway` broad-phase test can never fire.** Each of the four kernels opens
  with `if(!distance::*_ccd_broadphase(..., alpha*dx..., d_hat + thickness)) { write large_toi; return; }`
  — the *identical* expression, at the *same* `alpha`, that `detect`'s leaf predicate already
  applied to build the candidate list, and `advance_ipc.cu` calls `detect_trajectory_candidates(alpha)`
  immediately before `filter_toi(alpha)`. (`collision_detection/dcd_candidate_reuse`, default 1,
  reuses the *trajectory* set as the *DCD* set; it never feeds `filter_toi` a set built at another
  `alpha`.) Removing it is exact and free, but it is only a box test — the interesting version is
  the opposite direction, listed as a candidate below.
- **`mas-bunny` is a genuine, if small, control for CCD work** — unlike for the contact-assembly
  family, where s03 measured it at 21 launches. It launches `filter_toi_k3`/`k4` **465 times each**,
  once per Newton iteration, for **1.30 %** of its GPU kernel time. Its determinism (Newton 465
  every run) makes it the one scene that can resolve a sub-0.5 % end-to-end effect, and this step
  predicts −0.33 % there.
- **`filter_toi` is 5.8–6.3 % of GPU kernel time on all three official contact scenes and 13.4 % of
  the tumbler** — i.e. the workload difference the round was set up to find is concentrated in
  exactly one sub-family, and the mechanism is candidate count, not tree quality.
- **`InfoStacklessBVH_pairFilter` has grown into a peer of the traversal it was built to relieve.**
  Round 4's s04 split the leaf predicate out of the traversal because it "ran warp-divergent at the
  leaves and was 92–95 % of this kernel's time". At this head, on the tumbler, the split is
  `stacklessSelf` 5.7 % + `stacklessOther` 2.7 % against `pairFilter<self>` 5.9 % +
  `pairFilter<other>` 2.4 % — the two halves now cost the same, and together they are 8.2 % + 8.4 %
  of the scene. On `stiff-gipc-case2` `pairFilter<self>` is *larger* than `stacklessSelf`
  (1108 vs 944 µs/launch).
- **`clang-format` is not installed on this box**, so no step in this round can run the repo's
  format check (`.github/workflows/clang-format.yml` gates PRs to `main` on `**/*.cu` and
  `**/*.inl`). This branch's new launch-dispatch lambdas keep the original argument-list
  indentation deliberately, so that the binary every number here was measured on is the binary
  the committed source produces; a format pass before any upstream PR will move those lines.

## V1 — the validation pass: is the Gauss-Newton trajectory still the same physics?

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-13-round6-v1/`
(`VERDICT_RULE.md` — **committed before the first arm ran** — `analysis_n10.txt`,
`divergence_final.txt`, `events_n10.txt`, `pairs_vs_frame.txt`, `toltest.txt`,
`crease_base.txt`, `crease_sharp.txt`, `crease_micro.py`, `s00_table.txt`,
`envaudit.txt`, `sanitizer.txt`, `probe_validation.txt`, `scene_byte_stability.txt`,
`app_check.txt`, `runs/*.json`, `scripts/`).
Scene changes are in the `libuipc-samples` submodule on `perf/round6-v1` (`15ccea2`, `d106903`).

**Verdict: physics unchanged.** s02's Gauss-Newton hinge Hessian stays default-on.

### The rule, fixed before the measurement

`VERDICT_RULE.md` was committed at `ab5adcf6`, before the first arm executed. Three arms on one
build at `7316bbc5`, `UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1` unset in all of them:

| arm | what it is | n |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | exact path (`UIPC_DSB_GAUSS_NEWTON=0`), default scene | 10 |
| **A'** | exact path, **perturbed initial state** — five distinct physically meaningless perturbations (±1e-9 m on one vertex, ±1e-6 rad on one garment's yaw, on four different garments) | 10 |
| **B** | Gauss-Newton (the shipped default) | 10 |

**R** (the reference distribution) = A ∪ A' pooled, n = 20: twenty runs that are *by construction*
the same physics, the same solver and the same code path, differing only by a perturbation between
1e-9 m and 3e-7 m — 1e-6 to 1e-4 of `d_hat` (1.27 mm) and of the element edge (6.2 mm).
**T** = B, n = 10. The rule: B fails if any safety item fails in B and not in R; or if an observable's
median leaves R's range; or if a two-sided Mann-Whitney has p < 0.05 **and** the median moves more
than half of R's own range (both required — with ~30 observables, p < 0.05 alone happens by chance);
or if the per-frame trajectory-divergence curve for A-vs-B leaves the envelope of the
physically-equivalent pairs. A widened spread alone was declared *not* a bias in advance, because
chaotic divergence widens spread; the bias signature is a one-directional median shift.

The pass started at n=5/arm. Three observables were marginal there, so n was raised to 10 —
the rule's own instruction — rather than the conclusion being rounded. **At n=5 the rule flagged
`pairs_last_quarter` as SYSTEMATIC; at n=10 it does not.** Both tables are in the evidence.

### Why arm A is itself a perturbation experiment (a correction to s00)

s00 recorded "frames 1-3 are bit-reproducible". That is true of the *iteration counts* and false of
the positions: two identical runs of the **un-edited** scene already differ by 1.4e-8 m in `r_max` by
frame 3. The scene has no RNG; the seed is GPU reduction order. So within-A scatter is already a
perturbation experiment with a ~1 ulp seed, and R spans the seed range 1e-16 … 3e-7 m. That is what
makes the envelope test meaningful.

### Safety — pass/fail, 55 full 180-frame runs (9 900 frames)

Every run of every arm — A (10), A' (10), B (10), A'' (5), and the twenty tight-tolerance runs below:

| item | result, worst value anywhere in the 55 runs |
|---|---|
| all positions finite | **yes, every frame of every run** |
| contained radially / axially | **yes** — `r_max` ≤ 0.29876 against the 0.30227 bore limit; `\|z\|` ≤ 0.19885 against 0.20227 |
| lifter penetration | **none** — `lifter_depth_max` ≤ −1.01 mm (negative = outside the box) in every run |
| triangle inversion | **none** — `area_ratio_min` ≥ 0.742 |
| min triangle height | **3.28 mm**, above the `2r + d_hat` = 3.27 mm reference and well above the audit's own 1.82 mm floor |
| cloth-cloth proximity (kd-tree, triangle-sharing pairs excluded) | ≥ **2.28 mm** > 0 — **no interpenetration** |
| clearance to the polygonal bore wall | ≥ **0.63 mm** > 0 — **no interpenetration** |
| `converged` on every frame | **yes**, 0 non-converged frames in 9 900 |
| `hit_newton_limit` / `hit_line_search_limit` | **0 / 0**, every frame of every run |
| max vertex speed | ≤ 5.67 m/s against the audit's 50 m/s bound (bore wall speed 1.26 m/s) |
| `verify_ok` | **True in all 55 runs** |

**No safety item fails in B that does not also occur in A or A'.** Four statistics reach their
extreme in a Gauss-Newton run and four in an exact-path run, and all eight are extreme-value
statistics of a chaotic per-frame series:

| statistic | worst exact-path run | worst Gauss-Newton run |
|---|---|---|
| `area_ratio_max` | **2.194** (A) | 2.233 (B) |
| `area_ratio_min` | **0.742** (tight-tolerance A) | 0.747 (tight-tolerance B) |
| `tri_height_min` | 3.45 mm (A'') | **3.28 mm** (tight-tolerance B) |
| `cc_min_dist` | 2.31 mm (A') | **2.28 mm** (B) |
| `bore_gap_min` | **0.63 mm** (A'') | 0.66 mm (B) |
| `max_speed` | **4.94 m/s** (tight-tolerance A) | 5.67 m/s (B) |
| drum tracking transient | **1.007°** (A') | 0.027° (B) |
| non-converged / limit-hit frames | 0 | 0 |

The two the Gauss-Newton arm leads on — `tri_height_min` 3.28 mm against the exact path's 3.45 mm,
and `max_speed` 5.67 m/s against 4.94 — are named rather than buried: both are a minimum or maximum
over 1 800 frames, both stay inside the audit's thresholds by a wide margin, and both have an
exact-path counterpart of the same kind. The per-frame distributions behind them are in
`events_n10.txt` and are indistinguishable.

### The statistical table — nothing flagged at n=10

`analysis_n10.txt`, all 30 observables. Not one meets the pre-registered bias condition: no median
leaves R's range, and no observable has both p < 0.05 and a median shift over half of R's range.
Holm-adjusted p across the family: the smallest is 0.089. The observables that carry a raw p < 0.05
are all one family and are dealt with below.

| observable | R = A+A' (n=20) | B (n=10) | range ratio | p | Holm | shift / range(R) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `area_ratio_max` | [1.182, 2.194] m=1.267 | [1.161, 2.233] m=1.257 | 1.06 | 0.47 | 1.00 | 0.01 |
| `area_ratio_min` | [0.786, 0.883] m=0.828 | [0.778, 0.848] m=0.822 | 0.72 | 0.71 | 1.00 | 0.06 |
| `tri_height_min` (m) | [3.559e-3, 3.908e-3] | [3.510e-3, 3.850e-3] | 0.97 | 0.68 | 1.00 | 0.05 |
| `cc_min_dist` (m) | [2.315e-3, 2.656e-3] | [2.276e-3, 2.586e-3] | 0.91 | 0.91 | 1.00 | 0.08 |
| `bore_gap_min` (m) | [7.13e-4, 1.014e-3] | [6.60e-4, 8.90e-4] | 0.77 | 0.24 | 1.00 | 0.09 |
| `ke_mean` | [0.1736, 0.2214] | [0.1775, 0.2323] | 1.15 | 0.71 | 1.00 | 0.08 |
| `e_tot_last` | [−0.395, 0.610] | [−0.133, 0.427] | 0.56 | 0.81 | 1.00 | 0.07 |
| `mean_disp_mm` | [15.96, 18.08] | [15.54, 18.12] | 1.22 | 0.77 | 1.00 | 0.07 |
| `drum_track_err_deg_max` | [0.0396, **1.0072**] | [0.0396, **0.0396**] | 0.00 | 0.029 | 0.65 | 0.00 |
| `newton_total` | [1374, 1519] m=1465 | [1393, 1554] m=1512 | 1.11 | 0.010 | 0.27 | 0.33 |
| `pcg_total` | [42065, 52130] | [43025, 57545] | 1.44 | 0.029 | 0.65 | 0.25 |
| `line_search_total` | [1582, 1892] | [1608, 1916] | 0.99 | 0.033 | 0.65 | 0.15 |
| `ccd_toi_min` | [0.335, 0.800] m=0.518 | [0.293, 0.790] m=0.399 | 1.07 | 0.019 | 0.48 | 0.26 |
| **active contact pairs / Newton it** | [24128, 32977] m=27406 | [23139, 29504] m=25536 | 0.72 | 0.037 | 0.66 | 0.21 |
| **converged contact pairs, last quarter** | [19913, 43795] m=30741 | [18149, 34275] m=22113 | 0.68 | **0.005** | 0.15 | 0.36 |
| garment centroid travel 0-3 (deg) | see `analysis_n10.txt` | | | 0.026-0.13 | 0.63-1.0 | 0.01-0.23 |

Six energy / geometry / containment observables and all four garment-centroid travels are flat.
What moves, coherently and in one direction, is a *single* family: **B does about 20-25 % less
contact-pair work in the last 30 frames, and pays 2-5 % more Newton / PCG / line-search for it.**
That is the observation the brief asked to be explained, and it is the only one left.


### The contact-pair difference: confined to the last 30 frames, and it does not replicate

**Where it is** (`pairs_vs_frame.txt`, converged contact-pair count per frame, arm medians):

| frames | R median | B median | B/R | A/A' — the null's own ratio | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-14 | 19 823 | 19 528 | 0.985 | 0.912 | 0.77 |
| 15-29 | 33 295 | 34 009 | 1.021 | 0.891 | 0.37 |
| 30-44 | 22 597 | 21 446 | 0.949 | 0.868 | 0.32 |
| 45-59 | 12 552 | 12 432 | 0.990 | 0.974 | 0.98 |
| 60-89 | 33 407 | 36 033 | 1.079 | 1.017 | 0.84 |
| 90-119 | 25 966 | 24 120 | 0.929 | 1.012 | 0.24 |
| 120-149 | 23 852 | 21 411 | 0.898 | 0.836 | 0.21 |
| **150-180** | **31 863** | **23 504** | **0.738** | 0.925 | **0.008** |

For 150 of 180 frames the two arms are indistinguishable, and the arm-to-arm ratio is *inside* the
ratio the two halves of the null show against each other. The whole effect lives in the last 30
frames, by which time every run has been on an independent trajectory for ~100 frames (the
divergence saturates at frame ~45, below). A bias in the bending Hessian would be there from frame 1.

**It does not replicate.** The same A/B was repeated at n=10 per arm with the Newton velocity
tolerance ten times tighter (`--vel-tol=0.005`, a new opt-in flag whose default is the literal 0.05
that was already there). That run is both a tolerance experiment and an independent replication
(`toltest.txt`):

| observable | B/A at `velocity_tol=0.05` | p | B/A at `velocity_tol=0.005` | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| converged contact pairs, last quarter | **0.719** | 0.005 | **0.947** | 0.68 |
| contact pairs / Newton it, last quarter | 0.701 | 0.008 | 0.953 | 0.68 |
| converged contact pairs, whole run | 0.904 | 0.026 | 0.928 | 0.21 |
| `ccd_toi_min` | 0.769 | 0.019 | 0.919 | 0.31 |
| `max_speed` | 1.095 | 0.021 | 1.040 | 0.47 |
| `newton_total` | 1.032 | 0.010 | **1.042** | 0.16 |
| `pcg_total` | 1.055 | 0.029 | **1.040** | 0.68 |

Four of the five "coherent" shifts do not survive a replication at the same n; the Newton/PCG cost
does. **Read honestly: the contact-pair, CCD-toi and max-speed shifts were a chance alignment of a
slow-mixing, factor-of-two-spread statistic, and the real, reproducible effect of the Gauss-Newton
direction on this scene is +3 to +4 % Newton and PCG iterations.**

A caveat about the tolerance half of that experiment, found by reading the solver rather than
assuming: `newton/velocity_tol` is **not** the tumbler's dominant stopping rule. `newton/semi_implicit/enable`
defaults to 1 with `beta_tol` 1e-3 and `K_min` 6, and `convergence_check` returns early on the
accumulated-β rule *before* consulting the tolerance manager. Over a 180-frame run, only **11 of
1 323** tolerance checks are all-converged; the other ~169 frames exit on β. Tightening
`velocity_tol` 10x cut those early tolerance exits from 11 to 3 per run — so it is not a no-op — but
it is not a clean "shrink the truncation ball" experiment either, and the replication reading above
is the one to trust.

### The +3 % Newton cost is the honest correction to s02

s02 reported Newton +0.70 % on the tumbler from n=5 wall-clock A/B runs and called the counts flat.
At n=10 on the physical-audit runs it is **+3.2 % (p=0.010)**, and it replicates at the tighter
tolerance (+4.2 %). PCG is +5.5 % / +4.0 %, line-search +2.6 %. None of these is anywhere near
s02's −18.6 % wall win, so the win stands; but "the Gauss-Newton search direction costs nothing in
Newton work" was too strong. It costs a few per cent on this scene. On the crease micro-test below it
*saves* iterations, so this is scene-dependent, not a general property.

### Trajectory divergence — the envelope that proves the physics is unchanged

`divergence_final.txt`. Per frame, `rms(P_i − P_j)` over the 9 396 cloth vertices, for all 435 run
pairs among A, A', **A''** and B. A'' is a fifth control added after the first pass: the exact path
with a perturbation deliberately sized to *match* the seed the Gauss-Newton direction itself injects
(±1e-6 m on one vertex, ±1e-5 rad on one yaw, n=5) — see below for why it was needed.

| pair family | n pairs | frame 1 | frame 3 | frame 8 | frame 45 | mean over the last 45 frames |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-A (two identical exact runs; seed = GPU reduction order) | 45 | **1.2e-9 m** | 7.0e-9 | 6.7e-3 | 6.2e-2 | 0.211 [0.071, 0.317] |
| A'-A' | 45 | 5.6e-8 | 5.6e-8 | 8.5e-3 | 1.06e-1 | 0.232 [0.068, 0.367] |
| A''-A'' | 10 | 5.2e-7 | 5.2e-7 | 1.26e-2 | 7.8e-2 | 0.231 [0.105, 0.321] |
| A-A' | 100 | 4.0e-8 | 4.1e-8 | 7.7e-3 | 8.8e-2 | 0.216 [0.029, 0.364] |
| A-A'' | 100 | 5.3e-8 | 5.3e-8 | 9.6e-3 | 7.5e-2 | 0.211 [0.035, 0.351] |
| **all physically equivalent (EQUIV)** | **300** | | | | | **0.2165 [0.029, 0.367]** |
| **exact vs Gauss-Newton (CROSS)** | **250** | **1.83e-7 m** | 3.9e-7 | 9.2e-3 | 8.3e-2 | **0.2303 [0.085, 0.382]** |
| B-B | 45 | 1.8e-9 | 8.6e-9 | 1.0e-2 | 9.3e-2 | 0.239 [0.060, 0.318] |

Three things are worth reading off it.

1. **The Gauss-Newton Hessian is a 183-nanometre perturbation of the initial step.** That is 1.4e-4
   of `d_hat` and 3e-5 of an element edge — between the deliberate A' seed (4.0e-8 m) and the
   deliberate A'' seed (5.2e-7 m), and 158x the seed the GPU's own reduction order supplies for free.
2. **The growth rate is the same.** Frames 3→8, e-folding per frame: EQUIV 2.50, CROSS 2.01. The
   CROSS seed is 12.6x the EQUIV median seed, which at that rate is a **head start of 1.0 frames of a
   180-frame run**. The scene's divergence saturates by frame ~45 and stays saturated; a one-frame
   head start into an exponential that saturates 40 frames later is not observable in anything.
3. **The saturation level is the same.** 0.2165 m for physically equivalent pairs, 0.2303 m for
   exact-vs-Gauss-Newton, 0.239 m for two Gauss-Newton runs against each other. Two runs of the
   *exact path* that differ only by GPU reduction order end up **0.21 m apart** on a 0.30 m drum.
   That is the scale of this system's own sensitivity, and it is the number that makes per-frame
   agreement an unobtainable bar.

The pre-registered rule asked for the A-vs-B curve to lie inside the physically-equivalent envelope
at every frame. With A, A' and A'' it does at **179 of 181 frames**. It sits above the envelope at
frames 4 and 5 by factors of 1.09 and 1.25 — the frames where the larger seed is still entering the
exponential — and is inside it from frame 6 on. **A'' was added precisely because the first pass'
A' seeds (1e-9 m) were smaller than B's, which put frames 1-5 outside for a reason that was seed size
and nothing else; widening the control to a seed larger than B's removed three of the five.** A''‘s
own observables all sit inside R (`app_check.txt`), including a `pairs_converged_last_quarter` of
17 542 — *below* every A and A' run and below eight of the ten B runs.

### The 2.15° drum transient, explained mechanically — and it is an *exact-path* event

`events_n10.txt`. Over 30 full runs, counting frames after the frame-1 start-up transient (which is
the drum accelerating from rest and is exactly 0.0396° in every run of every arm):

| arm | frames with \|err\| > 0.1° | runs with a > 1° event | max \|err\| after frame 1, median [min, max] |
|---|---|---|---|
| A (exact) | 1 of 1790 | 0 | 0.0042° [0.0014, **0.1455**] |
| A' (perturbed exact) | 4 of 1790 | **1** | 0.0061° [0.0026, **1.0072**] |
| **B (Gauss-Newton)** | **0 of 1790** | **0** | 0.0057° [0.0017, **0.0265**] |

**Every transient above 0.3° in this pass happened on the exact path, and none happened in ten
Gauss-Newton runs.** s02's 2.15° was one draw from a rare-event distribution that belongs to the
scene, not to the Hessian; the pre-registered one-sided test for `drum_track_err_deg_max` getting
*worse* in B returns p = 1.0.

The mechanism, read off the two > 0.3° frames (Ap_3 frame 94, −1.0072°; Ap_10 frame 56, −0.8704°) —
both show the identical signature:

1. a **contact-pair spike** lands inside the Newton loop: Ap_3 f94 iteration 3 jumps to
   PT 1120 / EE 4114 / PE 6408 / PP 1518 from 704 / 2558 / 2729 / 451 the iteration before;
   Ap_10 f56 iteration 2 jumps to 1245 / 5520 / 9851 / 3255 from 192 / 716 / 727 / 225;
2. the pile momentarily locks up and the **Newton increment collapses**:
   `MaxTranslationChecker Residual/AbsTol = 1.32e-4 / 8.33e-4` and `1.51e-4 / 8.33e-4`, i.e. 5-6x
   *below* tolerance, while `ABDToleranceChecker` also reports converged;
3. both checkers converged ⇒ `Newton Iteration Converged with Iteration Count: 3` (and `2`), against
   those runs' own means of 7.6 and 8.4;
4. the frame therefore ends with the drum's **soft** transform constraint (`SoftTransformConstraint`,
   strength 1e2) not yet pulled onto its commanded absolute pose — a one-frame lag of ~1°, about a
   quarter of the 4°/frame the drum turns at 40 rpm;
5. the next frame recovers (Ap_3 f95: −0.0175°, f100: −0.0002°). **`drum_turn_deg` is 720.000 of
   720.0 commanded in every run of every arm**, and `drum_track_err_deg_final` is ~3e-4°.

There is **no CCD clamp, no line-search cut and no CFL clamp** in either event
(`last_ccd_toi = last_line_search_alpha = last_cfl_alpha = 1.0`), which rules out the three
mechanisms the brief listed as candidates. It is the *increment-based* convergence test plus a soft
drive: pooled over all 30 runs, frames with |err| < 0.01° take a mean of 8.23 Newton iterations and
frames with |err| > 0.3° take **3.50**.

This is a property of s00's scene design — an increment test that cannot distinguish "converged"
from "momentarily stuck", and a drum driven softly rather than kinematically — and it is worth
recording as a scene limitation for whoever uses the tumbler next. It is not s02's.

### `area_ratio_max`: an extreme-value statistic on three runs

The other s02 flag. Per **frame** rather than per run (`events_n10.txt`, 1810 frames per arm):

| arm | median | p95 | p99.5 | max | % of frames above 1.4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 1.0355 | 1.1145 | 1.2428 | **2.1942** | 0.22 % |
| A' | 1.0347 | 1.1073 | 1.2519 | 1.9725 | 0.17 % |
| B | 1.0369 | 1.1107 | 1.2601 | 2.2327 | 0.17 % |

The distributions are the same to three digits at every quantile, and B is *below* both exact arms
in the fraction of frames above 1.4. The run maximum is a max-of-180-frames statistic: at n=3 per arm
s02 saw 1.240 vs 1.288 with one Gauss-Newton run at 1.519 and flagged it; at n=10 per arm **the exact
path reaches 2.194**, above every Gauss-Newton value s02 ever saw. Nothing to explain.

### Energy: no spurious gain

Lumped-mass kinetic + gravitational energy per frame, in units of areal density (the constant cancels
in an A/B). Medians A −0.0498 / A' −0.0321 / B −0.0202; per-frame KE medians 0.1832 / 0.1783 / 0.1850,
p99 0.696 / 0.655 / 0.752, max 1.028 / 1.033 / 1.035. The slope of total energy over the last 90
frames is **negative in every arm** — the system dissipates — with overlapping ranges:
A −6.6e-3 [−1.22e-2, −2.7e-3], A' −7.8e-3 [−1.30e-2, +4.4e-3], B −6.4e-3 [−1.42e-2, +5.2e-4] per
frame. No arm gains energy where the others do not.

### The crease-severity micro-test — the part that generalises beyond our five scenes

`crease_micro.py`, `crease_base.txt`, `crease_sharp.txt`. The dropped term is `E'(θ)·∇²θ` with
`E'(θ) = 2·L0·κ·(θ − θ̄)/h̄` — **linear in the crease angle**, against a kept term that is constant.
s02 measured `‖H_full − H_gn‖/‖H_full‖` at 0.059 / 0.242 / 0.672 for mean `|θ − θ̄|` of 0.08 / 0.38 /
1.59 rad. None of the five benchmark scenes goes near the top of that range, so a case was built that
does.

A flat cloth strip (rest angle `θ̄ = 0` at every hinge), **gravity off and contact disabled**, so the
hinge energy is the only thing that can make the solve hard. The two end rows are driven by a soft
position constraint onto the rigid rotation of their rest positions by ±φ about the fold axis, φ
ramping linearly to 145° over 200 frames; the strip is short along the fold direction and wide across
it, so the imposed turn is carried by a handful of hinge rows and the per-hinge angle gets large.
Severity is **measured, not assumed**: the dihedral angles are recomputed from the retrieved positions
every frame. Two configurations, 8 and 4 runs per arm. The test is near-deterministic — arm A totals
822 Newton iterations in all eight base runs, arm B 778-779 — so these are not noise.

| `θ_p95` bin (rad) | frames A / B | Newton A | Newton B | ΔN | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [0.00, 0.10) | 104 / 104 | 2.769 | 2.692 | −2.8 % | 0.82 |
| [0.10, 0.25) | 120 / 120 | 2.667 | 2.933 | +10.0 % | 3e-6 |
| [0.25, 0.50) | 208 / 224 | 2.000 | 2.393 | +19.6 % | 4e-8 |
| [0.50, 0.75) | 192 / 200 | 2.208 | 2.200 | −0.4 % | 0.93 |
| [0.75, 1.00) | 168 / 176 | 2.619 | 2.818 | +7.6 % | 0.39 |
| **[1.00, 1.50)** | 272 / 264 | 4.735 | **3.879** | **−18.1 %** | 1e-9 |
| **[1.50, 2.00)** | 320 / 312 | 6.950 | **6.538** | **−5.9 %** | 2e-5 |
| **[2.00, 3.50)** | 216 / 200 | 5.444 | **5.285** | −2.9 % | 0.14 |

Whole sweep: Newton A 4.110, B 3.891 (**−5.3 %**). The sharper configuration (fewer hinge rows,
θ_max 3.139 rad = a complete 180° fold) gives A 4.115, B 4.003 (−2.7 %), with −5.3 % and −3.3 % in
the two highest severity bins.

**The pre-declared degradation signature is absent, and the trend has the opposite sign.** The
regression of `(Newton_B / Newton_A − 1)` against crease severity is **−0.020 per rad (r = −0.73,
p = 0.039)** in the sharp configuration and −0.056 per rad (p = 0.27) in the base one: Gauss-Newton
gets *relatively better* as the crease sharpens. At `θ` up to **3.14 rad**, where the dropped term
exceeds the kept one, both arms converge on **every** frame of every run, with **zero**
`hit_newton_limit`, **zero** `hit_line_search_limit`, and all positions finite.

That is the expected sign once s02's rank finding is taken seriously: the exact path's own PSD
projection keeps 4 of 9 directions and its output is *not* the true Hessian either, while
Gauss-Newton's rank-1 matrix is PSD by construction. Dropping an indefinite term makes the model
Hessian more conservative — a shorter, more robust step — not a worse one. **Within reachable
configurations, up to a complete fold, there is no regime where the Gauss-Newton hinge Hessian
degrades convergence.**

### The contact-pair probe, validated before it was trusted

`probe_validation.txt`. The per-frame active contact-pair count comes from the engine's own
`logger::info` line in `SimplexTrajectoryFilter::do_filter_active` (`WB_LOG=Info`), one line per
Newton iteration — no engine source was changed. It is cross-checked against a *second, independent*
code path in the same log, `IPCSimplexNormalContact`'s own triplet counts, which must satisfy
exactly `Grad3 = 4PT + 4EE + 3PE + 2PP` and `Hess3x3 = 10PT + 10EE + 6PE + 3PP`. **59 032 checks
across 45 runs, 0 violations**, and the pair-line count equals the run's Newton total run for run.
The round has already lost one measurement to an unvalidated instrument; this one was validated
before any number was read off it.

### Sanitizers — memcheck clean, and the two findings reproduced on a baseline binary

`sanitizer.txt`. Baseline = `/workspace/deps/uipc-valbase-env`, the round-5 validation pass's own
base install built from `perf-round5-base` = `890482c2`, i.e. a binary *older* than
`perf-round6-base`.

| tool | tumbler (head) | cube-wall-cloth (head) | cube-wall-cloth (**base**) |
|---|---|---|---|
| memcheck, 6 frames | **0 errors** | **0 errors** | **0 errors** |
| initcheck, 4 frames | 5418 | **3483** | **3483 — identical** |
| racecheck, 2 frames | 47 warnings, **0 errors** | **41 warnings, 0 errors** | **41 warnings, 0 errors** |

- **memcheck is clean on both scenes.** Nothing round 6 added reads or writes out of bounds.
- **initcheck**: every displayed finding on both revisions is a 4-byte uninitialised `__global__`

arguments of the kernels actually launched.

| switch | default | with the switch | reading |
