### Correctness gate

`gate.sh` on the head build with the V1 branch checked out, identical to `baseline_tests.txt`:
11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped.

### Verdict: physics unchanged

No safety item fails in B that does not also occur in A or A'. No physical observable's median leaves
the reference distribution's range. No observable meets the pre-registered systematic-shift condition.
The trajectory-divergence envelope contains the exact-vs-Gauss-Newton curve at 179 of 181 frames and
at saturation, and the two frames it does not are accounted for, quantitatively, by seed size. The one
coherent shift the first pass found does not replicate. The two statistics s02 flagged are both
extreme-value artefacts of n=3, and the more dramatic of them — the drum transient — happens **only on
the exact path** in 30 runs, with a mechanism that is the scene's increment-based convergence test
plus a soft drum drive.

**s02's Gauss-Newton hinge Hessian should stay default-on.** The `UIPC_DSB_GAUSS_NEWTON=0` rollback is
proved to work at kernel level and should stay.

Two honest corrections to the round record come out of this pass and are worth more than the verdict:

1. **The Gauss-Newton direction costs +3 to +4 % Newton and +4 to +5 % PCG on the tumbler**, measured
   at n=10 and replicated, not the "flat" of s02's n=5 wall A/B. Against −18.6 % of wall time it does
   not change the decision, and on the crease micro-test it *saves* iterations — but it is not free.
2. **s00's "frames 1-3 are bit-reproducible" is about iteration counts, not positions.** Two identical
   runs of the un-edited scene differ by 1.4e-8 m in `r_max` by frame 3 and end **0.21 m apart** on a
   0.30 m drum. Any future claim about this scene's trajectory has to be made against that envelope.

### Limits, stated plainly

- **N runs is N runs.** 10 per arm on the main comparison, 10 per arm on the replication, 5 on A''.
  A real effect smaller than about a third of the null range would not be visible here; the null range
  on the pile-compaction statistics is a **factor of two**, which is why this scene cannot answer a
  small question about them at any affordable n.
- **One scene.** The three-arm experiment is `tumbler-garments` only. `cube-wall-cloth` and
  `stiff-gipc-case2` also run the hinge kernel and were *not* given the same treatment; their s02
  observables (`cloth_min_y`) are the only cross-scene evidence and they were n=5.
- **One GPU.** Everything is cc 7.5. The iteration-count difference is a property of the search
  direction and is architecture-independent; nothing else here is a throughput claim.
- **Short sanitizer runs.** 6 / 4 / 2 frames. Sanitized runs are 100-1000x slower, so these windows do
  not cover the dense-pile steady state that starts around frame 15, and `uipc_test_sim_case` was not
  run under any sanitizer.
- **The contact-pair probe counts the active set**, not contact forces, and excludes the broad-phase
  candidates and the vertex-half-plane pairs (which are 0 for this scene throughout).
- **`cc_min_dist` is a vertex-vertex proxy** for the true point-triangle gap, not the gap itself. It
  bounds it from above, it is the same proxy in every arm, and it never reached zero — but a genuine
  self-intersection test over 19 920 triangles per frame was not run.
- **The tolerance half of the replication is weaker than it looks**: `velocity_tol` binds on only ~11
  of 180 frames in this scene because the semi-implicit β rule terminates the rest. The replication
  reading stands; the "truncation shrinks with the tolerance" reading does not.
- **The crease micro-test has no contact and no gravity** — deliberately, to isolate the hinge — so it
  says nothing about how the Gauss-Newton direction interacts with a contact-dominated regime. It also
  reaches θ = π by construction, which is the end of the reachable range, not a sample of it.

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`tumbler-garments` does not stop Newton on `velocity_tol`.** `newton/semi_implicit/enable`
  defaults to **1**, and `SimEngine::convergence_check` returns on the accumulated-β rule
  (`beta_tol` 1e-3, `K_min` 6) *before* it ever calls the tolerance manager. Over a 180-frame run
  only **11 of 1 323** tolerance checks are all-converged; the other ~169 frames exit on β at 7-8
  iterations. Anybody tuning this scene's accuracy through `newton/velocity_tol` is turning a knob
  that binds on 6 % of its frames.
- **`MaxTranslationChecker` is an increment test, and a contact-pair spike can satisfy it.** It
  compares the *Newton increment* against `velocity_tol * dt`, so a frame in which the cloth pile
  momentarily locks up reports "converged" at iteration 2-3 with a residual 5x below tolerance while
  the soft-constrained drum is still ~1° behind its commanded pose. That is the whole mechanism of
  the tumbler's rare drum transient, and it is a property the scene's `SoftTransformConstraint` drive
  inherits. A kinematic drum, or a force-residual check alongside the increment check, would remove it.
- **`verify.py`'s `verify_ok` triangle-height threshold is 1.82 mm, not the 3.27 mm the narrative
  quotes.** The audit asserts `tri_height_min > 2*(r + d_hat)*0.4` = 1.82 mm; s00's `2r + d_hat` =
  3.27 mm is the *physical* "permanent self-contact" reference, and the two are easy to conflate.
  Both are stated separately above for that reason.
- **`UIPC_DSB_OCC` does not exist on main.** s01's ledger row says "kept in the branch default off",
  which reads like a shipped switch; `grep -rn UIPC_DSB_OCC src/` returns nothing on this head.
- **s00's garment centroid-travel figures are an n=1 sample and are not representative.** s00 records
  557 / 714 / 332 / 739 deg; at n=10 on the same code path the medians are 682 / 829 / 798 / 788 with
  per-garment run-to-run ranges as wide as 128-1030 deg. The qualitative statement ("carried and
  slipping") survives; the numbers should not be quoted as a reference.
- **Round 5's two sanitizer findings are still exactly where that pass left them**, at identical
  counts: the CUB `ExclusiveSum` uninitialised read from `GlobalDyTopoEffectManager::_distribute`,
  and the `MASPreconditionerEngine_*` racecheck warnings. Neither has an owner yet.


## s05 — the conservative bound does belong in the CCD narrow phase, not in the BVH leaf predicate: `pairFilter` is the wrong place, measured

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-13-round6-s05/`
(`cull_stats_tumbler.txt`, `cull_verify_inscene.txt`, `broad_stats.txt`, `ccd_cull_probe.{cu,txt}`,
`ccd_cull_probe_5M.txt`, `sass_identity.txt`, `sass_cull_twins.txt`, `sass_resusage.txt`,
`scope_tum.txt`, `scope_c2.txt`, `scope_cwc.txt`, `accounting_tum.txt`, `ab/`, `verify/`,
`gate.txt`, `gate_c1.txt`, `scope_mb.txt`).

**Verdict: measured and rejected.** The bound is computable in the leaf predicate, it is
conservative, and it removes **91 %** of the candidate array — taking **85 %** off *both* narrow
phases. It is still a **net loss on every scene** (`stiff-gipc-case2` **+7.43 % end to end,
disjoint, p=2.6e-04**), because the leaf predicate is a 2.1x larger and 48 %-divergent population
than the candidate array is. The branch is kept **default off** (`UIPC_CCD_CULL=1` turns it on);
what it produces for the round is the `distance::*_ccd_cull` predicate, its conservativeness proof,
and the placement arithmetic for the step that follows.

### The brief's premise, checked first — and it holds

The brief asserted that `InfoStacklessBVH_pairFilter_kernel` "already holds both primitives'
positions, and could evaluate the same conservative bound s04 hoisted". It holds, and more: the
leaf predicate (`..._detect_AllE_AllE_pred::operator()` and its three siblings) already loads the
positions, the displacements, **and** `EE_thickness(...)` / `EE_d_hat(...)` from the same four
per-vertex arrays `filter_toi_k4` re-reads. The only value it lacks is `eta`
(`toi_safety_margin`, a scalar), and `toc_prev` is the constant `large_enough_toi = 1.1`. So the
bound costs **no extra loads at all** there. That is what made the hypothesis worth testing, and
it is not what killed it.

### The bound is not s04's bound: `dcd_candidate_reuse` makes the d_hat window part of the contract

s04's first-pass exit is a statement about `filter_toi` alone. Culling the *candidate array* is
not, because `collision_detection/dcd_candidate_reuse` is **default 1**: `detect(alpha)` overwrites
the same four candidate buffers that `filter_active` then reads, both inside the line search's
`compute_energy` and — for every Newton iteration past the first — as the DCD candidate set
itself. A pair at `d = thickness + 0.5 d_hat` with a tiny displacement passes s04's exit and is an
**active contact**. Dropping it would delete a contact force, not merely a toi.

So the cull needs two conditions, not one, and `distance::*_ccd_cull` requires **both**
(`m` = the ACCD's own `maxDispMag`, built the same way — common translation removed, then the
largest vertex speed of each primitive added — so that `d(t) >= d0 - t*m`):

| | condition | what it buys |
|---|---|---|
| **CCD** | `(1 - eta) * (d0 - thickness) > toc_prev * m` | the ACCD provably returns `false`, so the pair writes `large_enough_toi` and cannot move the toi minimum |
| **DCD** | `(d0 - thickness) > d_hat + m` | `d(t) >= thickness + d_hat` for every `t` in `[0, 1]`, so `filter_active` rejects it wherever the line search lands |

The CCD half is proved without matching the ACCD's arithmetic ulp for ulp: on the ACCD's whole
domain `t <= toc_prev`, `d(t) - thickness > eta*(d0 - thickness)`, which is exactly its convergence
gap, so the `break` that reports a hit is unreachable; the `max_iter` bail needs 1000 passes each
advancing `toc` by `(1-eta)*(d_k - thickness)/m > eta*toc_prev`, which overruns `toc_prev` by the
second pass; only `toc > toc_prev` is left. Both comparisons carry an absolute slack of
`1e-9*(d0 + m)`, seven orders of magnitude above the double-precision error of the quantities and
one part in a billion of the threshold. A pair sitting exactly on the `d_hat` boundary contributes
exactly zero energy and zero gradient, so the boundary itself is benign.

### Conservativeness, proved two ways

**1. Standalone, against the real device functions** (`ccd_cull_probe.cu`, `ccd_cull_probe_5M.txt`):
5x10^6 randomised samples per pair type, **half of them adversarial** — near-parallel edges and
near-degenerate triangles, zero-length edges and coincident points, zero relative displacement, and
separations placed *exactly* at `thickness + d_hat` — with asymmetric per-primitive thickness and
`d_hat` log-uniform over 1e-4..1e-2 m. For every culled sample the probe runs the real
`*_ccd` **and** samples the swept segment at 221 points over `[0, 1.1]`:

| | EE | PT | PE | PP |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| samples | 5 000 000 | 5 000 000 | 5 000 000 | 5 000 000 |
| culled | 32.68 % | 29.23 % | 35.46 % | 34.24 % |
| hits on the full population (sanity) | 20.07 % | 27.54 % | 12.60 % | 19.61 % |
| **culled but the ACCD reports a hit** | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |
| **culled but inside the d_hat window somewhere on [0,1]** | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |
| **culled but within `thickness` somewhere on [0,1.1]** | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |

**2. In situ, over the real candidate population** (`cull_verify_inscene.txt`): the same two
conditions checked inside `filter_toi_k3`/`k4` with the cull **switched off**, so the probe sees
every pair the traversal stages. Over the first 400 `filter_toi` launches of a tumbler run —
**177 358 248 EE and 61 756 864 PT candidate pairs** — the cull fires on **91.1 %** (EE) and
**91.7 %** (PT) of them and there are **0 violations of either condition**.

The probe also cross-validates the CCD half against s04's exit exactly: `ccd_only` (the cull with
`d_hat = 0`) equals `first_pass_exits` **pair for pair** — 208 804 700 = 208 804 700 on EE,
71 453 224 = 71 453 224 on PT — over a quarter of a billion real pairs, despite being written as a
multiplication where the ACCD writes a division.

### The old arm reproduces main's binary exactly

`Cull` is a template parameter on all four leaf predicates, so the shipped instantiation carries
neither a branch nor a register for it. The `eta` member is appended to the predicate aggregate
unconditionally and is dead when `Cull = false`. Standalone compile of the filter's TU with the
build's exact flags at `1b9cc8a3` and at this head, SASS-diffed per instantiation: the new
template argument renames every leaf-predicate kernel, so they are matched by body hash
(`sass_cull_twins.txt`):

| | |
|---|---|
| base-only kernels (renamed) matched **byte for byte** in head | **13 / 13** — `pairFilter` x4, `stacklessSelf` x3, `stacklessOther` x6 |
| shipped `filter_toi_k1..k4<*, Stats=0>` | **identical**, REG 218 / 184 unchanged |
| `filter_active_k1..k4` | 1-3 `MOV Rn,<imm>` line-number constants (the `UIPC_KERNEL_ASSERT` source lines moved), identical instruction and FP64 counts — the same benign movement s04 recorded |
| `filter_toi_k*<*, Stats=1>` | the diagnostic instantiation only; it carries this step's probe |

### The measurement: 91 % of the candidates removed, and still a loss

Full-run nsys (`cuda_gpu_kern_sum`, fresh prefix per run), **3 complete runs per arm**, one build,
`UIPC_CCD_CULL=0` vs `=1`:

| µs/launch, tumbler-garments | old | new | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `filter_toi_k4` (CCD EE) | 1227.6 | 177.3 | **−85.6 %** |
| `filter_toi_k3` (CCD PT) | 587.5 | 114.2 | **−80.6 %** |
| `filter_active_k4` (DCD EE) | 529.0 | 73.0 | **−86.2 %** |
| `filter_active_k3` (DCD PT) | 303.0 | 47.4 | **−84.4 %** |
| **`pairFilter<self>`** (EE query) | 1023.4 | 2741.7 | **+167.9 %** |
| **`pairFilter<other>`** (PP/PE/PT) | 404.2 | 1396.7 | **+245.5 %** |
| `stacklessSelf` | 936.0 | 1051.1 | +12.3 % |
| `stacklessOther` | 417.6 | 406.2 | −2.7 % |

All eight ranges are disjoint. The accounting closes to 0.2 pp (`accounting_tum.txt`):

```
pairFilter<self>   +1718.3 us x 1602 launches  = +2752.7 ms
pairFilter<other>   +992.5 us x 1603 launches  = +1591.0 ms
stacklessSelf       +115.1 us x 1602 launches  =  +184.4 ms
stacklessOther       -11.4 us x 1603 launches  =   -18.3 ms
filter_toi_k4      -1050.3 us x 1411 launches  = -1482.0 ms
filter_toi_k3       -473.3 us x 1411 launches  =  -667.8 ms
filter_active_k4    -456.0 us x 1816 launches  =  -828.1 ms
filter_active_k3    -255.6 us x 1816 launches  =  -464.2 ms
                                          NET  = +1067.7 ms  on 22 711 ms  = +4.70 %
                                      measured scene GPU kernel time        = +4.88 %
```

| scene | BVH/CCD family | scene GPU kernel time | worst untouched family |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| tumbler-garments | **+11.4 %** | **+4.88 %** | bending +1.5 %, membrane +2.0 % (the scene's own drift) |
| stiff-gipc-case2 | **+31.1 %** | **+6.77 %** | every other family within **±0.8 %** |
| cube-wall-cloth | **+10.2 %** | **+2.75 %** | contact assemble +1.9 % |

### Why the leaf predicate is the wrong place — the number that decides it

A new diagnostic on the `detect` path (`broad_stats.txt`, `UIPC_CCD_STATS=1`) prints the two-phase
staging counts the host already reads every detect. On the tumbler, at detect 200:

```
staged / kept:   EE 812 342 / 387 081  (47.6 %)     PT 287 230 / 129 784  (45.2 %)
```

So `pairFilter` runs over a population **2.1x** the candidate array, of which fewer than half ever
reach the bound — and the ones that do are scattered across warps, so a warp pays the full
double-precision `*_distance_flag` + `*_distance2` latency for roughly half-empty lanes. This is
the same warp divergence round 4's s04 split `pairFilter` out of the traversal to escape; the cull
puts it back.

The consequence in one line: **adding the bound to the leaf predicate costs +1718 µs/launch, which
is 1.40x the entire `filter_toi_k4` kernel (1228 µs) it was meant to delete** — and for the
PT/PE/PP queries +992 µs against `filter_toi_k3`'s 588 µs, 1.69x. There is no arrangement of the
staging and re-reading cost that pays that back: the whole of `filter_toi` + `filter_active` on
this scene is 3442 ms/run and the leaf-predicate evaluation costs 4344 ms/run.

Second, smaller effect, and it is the s01/s14 lesson again: the culling predicate is inlined into
`stacklessSelf`'s *overflow fallback* (`flush_broad_pairs`, a rare path), which pushes that kernel
from **98 to 146 registers** — 2 blocks/SM to 1 on cc 7.5 — and costs the traversal +12.3 % on the
tumbler and **+35.7 %** on `stiff-gipc-case2` without any of its threads normally executing the
new code.

### Performance, end to end: n=3 per arm, ABBA + one discarded warm-up, one build

The scope gate predicts each scene directly (the GPU is ~100 % busy):

| scene | `filter_toi`+`filter_active`+`pairFilter` share | predicted (scope) | `meanFrameMs` | Newton / PCG |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| **mas-bunny** (n=5, deterministic) | BVH/CCD 8.4 % of GPU kernel time | **+0.89 %** | **+1.21 % (DISJOINT, p=2.7e-07)** | **+0.00 % / +0.03 %** |
| **stiff-gipc-case2** (n=3) | BVH/CCD 22.3 % | **+6.77 %** | **+7.43 % (DISJOINT, p=2.6e-04)** | **+0.00 %** / +0.69 % |
| cube-wall-cloth (n=3) | BVH/CCD 18.9 % | +2.75 % | +2.77 % (overlapping, p=0.076) | +1.32 % / +1.08 % |
| tumbler-garments (n=3) | BVH/CCD 39.5 % | +4.88 % | +5.80 % (ranges disjoint, p=0.20) | −0.18 % / +2.41 % |

**Every scene lands on its prediction, and every one of them is slower.** The two decisive ones are
the deterministic instruments: `mas-bunny` runs Newton **465 in all ten runs** and reads
**+1.21 %** against a predicted +0.89 %; `stiff-gipc-case2` reads **+7.43 %** at Newton **+0.00 %**
against a predicted +6.77 %. `mas-bunny` also shows the mechanism from the other side — with the
cull on, **348 of its 465 `filter_toi_k4` launches disappear entirely** (the candidate array is
empty and the kernel is not launched), and it is *still* 1.2 % slower.

### Gates

- **Correctness**: `bash /workspace/output/round6/gate.sh` against `baseline_tests.txt` in **both**
  arms — identical assertion counts on all eight suites (11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1,
  448/23, **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped). The only textual difference in either file is
  pytest's own wall-clock string. Note that the gate that ran with the cull **on** is the stronger
  one: `sim_case`'s 14 213 assertions all pass with 91 % of the candidate array removed.
- **Conservativeness**: the two proofs above (2x10^7 randomised samples with half adversarial, and
  2.39x10^8 real candidate pairs in situ), plus the tumbler `--verify` audit at n=14 per arm below.
- **Env-switch audit**: `UIPC_CCD_CULL=0` (also the default after this step) restores the old
  per-launch cost exactly on all four scenes — a 100-250 % per-launch difference on `pairFilter` is
  not something a broken switch could fake — and the old arm's 13 leaf-predicate kernels are
  byte-identical to main's.

### The tumbler `--verify` audit, n=14 per arm — and the one abort

180 frames, **14 runs per arm**, one build (`verify/`, `verify_compare.txt`). Every completed run
is clean and nothing separates the arms:

| | c0 (no cull) | c1 (cull) |
|---|---|---|
| `verify_ok` / contained radially / axially | **true in all 14** | **true in all 13 completed** |
| `cc_min_dist` (cloth-cloth closest approach) | 2.487 mm [2.318, 2.616] | **2.509 mm** [2.319, 2.656] |
| `bore_gap_min` | 0.852 mm [0.771, 0.963] | 0.804 mm [0.571, 0.915] |
| `tri_height_min` | 3.716 mm [3.244, 3.973] | 3.728 mm [3.287, 3.982] |
| `ccd_toi_min` / `ccd_toi_clamped_frames` | 0.555 / 7.6 | 0.531 / 7.2 |
| non-converged / Newton-limit / line-search-limit frames | **0 / 0 / 0** | **0 / 0 / 0** |

**Reported honestly: one run in the cull arm aborted.** `verify_c1_r3` died at frame 56, Newton 2
with `FusedPCG Init: r^T*z = nan` — the MAS preconditioner's inverse failed. It is **1 of 14 against
0 of 14**, one-sided Fisher exact **p = 0.50**, i.e. not resolvable at this sample size, and no
other statistic in the table moves. Three things are worth saying about it rather than waving it
away:

- the cull **cannot** remove an active contact pair — the in-situ probe checked exactly that
  condition on 239 million real candidate pairs and found **0** violations — so the two arms are
  draws from the same trajectory *distribution*;
- they are **not** the same trajectory: the compacted candidate array changes the atomic ordering in
  contact assembly, which V1 already measured to be a ~1e-7 m perturbation that saturates at 0.23 m
  on this scene. A 1-in-14 extreme run is what V1 recorded this scene to produce;
- but "cannot have caused it" is an argument, and 1-vs-0 is not a measurement. **The step that
  reuses this predicate must re-characterise the abort rate on both arms with n large enough to
  resolve a 1-in-14 event** (n >= 40 per arm for p < 0.05), and should treat a confirmed asymmetry as
  a defect in the predicate, not in the scene. This step's change is default off, so nothing ships
  on the strength of p = 0.50.

### Transfer prediction

**Algorithmic, and the rejection transfers — but its size does not.** The two mechanisms are
(a) 2.1x more pairs and ~48 % warp occupancy of the bound in the leaf predicate, which is a
property of the workload and the two-phase traversal, not of the GPU; and (b) a register-pressure
loss in `stacklessSelf` (98 -> 146), which PERF_METHOD §6 says an old 1/32-rate-FP64 part
over-rewards. On a part with better FP64 throughput the *absolute* cost of the divergent distance
evaluation falls, so the loss would shrink — but the ratio that decides the placement (leaf-
predicate population / candidate population = 2.1, lanes reaching the bound = 48 %) is unchanged,
and the direction of the verdict with it. **A rented run should not be spent re-testing this; it
should be spent on the placement the next step proposes.**

### Measurement doctrine after s11 (coordinator) — what this round has retired

Three instruments have now been disqualified for concurrency work, each by a measurement rather than
by argument. Recorded together because a later round will otherwise reach for them:

| instrument | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| `nsys stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` scene totals | **retired for scene totals** (s10) | a duration *sum*: it grows every time the round adds overlap. rwb sum/union went 1.060 (pre-K9) → 1.075 (s09) → **1.130** (s10). s10's winning arm reads **+0.9 %** here and −5.2 % by union. Still valid per kernel |
| GPU **busy union** from `cuda_gpu_trace` | **retired for ranking placements** (s11) | Spearman **rho = +0.10** against wall across five arms — it gave its *best* score to the *worst* arm. Per-arm sd 0.3–1.7 % against arms 4–9 pp apart, so this is not noise |
| repo `WB_TIMER=1` Timer tree | **unusable for any concurrency question** (s11) | `sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ wait_device(); })`, so every scope boundary synchronises the device — destroying the overlap and host run-ahead the arms differ in. Nothing at the call site says so |

**What remains for a step that changes where work is issued: end-to-end wall time, n large, through
`ab.py`.** Per-kernel `kern_sum` numbers stay valid for steps that change a kernel's own cost.

### Consequences for what the round already believes

- **s10's win stands and is confirmed at higher n**: s11 re-measured it at n=30 without a profiler at
  **−4.97 % ms/Newton (p=2.3e-21)**. What does *not* stand is s10's *ranking* of the placements — at
  n=8 it read `=2` as better and its trace called `=2` "does not work"; at n=30 modes 1, 2 and 4 are
  mutually indistinguishable on rwb (Welch p = 0.59–0.95). The placement does not matter on that scene.
- **s09's split conclusion is unaffected** — it compared union *and* wall and both agreed there.
- **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` no longer measures the split alone**, because modes 3/4 of the
  prepass only fire when the K9 split is taken. Pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms.

### And a 1-in-20 accident, caught live

s11's `stiff-gipc-case2` control — a scene that launches **zero** ABD G/H kernels — read **−0.46 %,
p=0.034, "DISJOINT" at n=4**, and reversed to +0.12 %, p=0.58 at n=6. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says a round of
twenty measurements manufactures one of these; this is the round's, on a scene where the effect is
known to be exactly zero.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **Put the cull where the bound is already computed: compact the candidate array inside `filter_toi`** | `filter_toi` evaluates exactly this distance for every candidate already, densely, at 100 % lane occupancy — which is why the *same* cull costs 1228 µs there and 2742 µs in `pairFilter`. Have it append the ~9 % of survivors (plus their `toi`) to a compacted array and resize the candidate buffers, and `filter_active` — **which runs 1816 times per tumbler run for 1292 ms, 5.7 % of the scene** — runs over 9 % of the pairs. Measured ceiling from this step's own c1 arm: `filter_active_k4` 529.0 -> 73.0 µs and `k3` 303.0 -> 47.4 µs, i.e. **−1292 ms/run on the tumbler (−5.7 %), −975 ms on `stiff-gipc-case2` (−2.2 %), −119 ms on cube-wall-cloth (−1.9 %)**, for the cost of one atomicAdd + one `int2` store per survivor and one batched D2H of the four compacted counts per `filter_toi`. The conservativeness proof in this step is exactly the proof that version needs (the d_hat half is what makes the compacted array legal for `filter_active` under `dcd_candidate_reuse`) | `filter_toi` + `filter_active` per launch; `stiff-gipc-case2` end to end (cv 0.3 %, Newton flat), plus the tumbler `--verify` audit |
| **The DCD detect at `alpha == 0` still stages 2.1x the pairs it keeps** | the leaf predicate's `alpha == 0` branch is already an exact distance test, so its 52 % rejection happens *after* the staging write. The two-phase split is paying a write + a re-read for every pair it then throws away. A cheap per-axis pre-test inside the traversal (before `flush_broad_pairs`) would cut the staged population without touching the predicate | `pairFilter` + `stacklessSelf` per launch |
| **`stacklessSelf` pays its overflow fallback's register pressure on every thread** | inlining the leaf predicate into `flush_broad_pairs` — a path taken only when a block's staged pairs exceed the `broad` capacity — took the kernel from **98 to 146 registers** and cost **+12.3 % / +35.7 %** on the tumbler / case2 in this step's c1 arm. The same is true today at 98 registers for the *shipped* predicate: marking the fallback `__noinline__` (or dropping it in favour of a capacity retry) is a pure occupancy win that needs no numerics argument | `stacklessSelf` / `stacklessOther` per launch, all four scenes |
| ~~`InfoStacklessBVH_pairFilter` evaluates the conservative bound~~ | **done — s05, rejected.** −85 % on both narrow phases, +168/+246 % on `pairFilter`, net +4.9 / +6.8 / +2.8 % of scene GPU kernel time | — |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`collision_detection/dcd_candidate_reuse` is documented "default off" in `advance_ipc.cu` and
  is `IndexT{1}` in `scene_default_config.cpp`.** The comment at the top of `do_advance` says
  "(collision_detection/dcd_candidate_reuse, default off)"; the schema default is 1 and the feature
  is on in every scene in the suite. The comment is stale and it is load-bearing — it is the
  paragraph a reader consults to decide whether the trajectory candidate set is also the DCD set,
  which is precisely the invariant any candidate-culling change has to respect.
- **The AL-IPC active-set manager consumes the raw candidate arrays and their per-pair toi.**
  `GlobalActiveSetManager::Impl::filter_new_candidates` reads `candidate_PTs()/EEs()` and
  `toi_PTs()/toi_EEs()` and builds `*_max_vertex_min_toi` per candidate; dropping a candidate whose
  own toi is `large_enough_toi` can still change that array, because the value is a max over the
  pair's *vertices'* minima. Any future candidate cull must stay off when
  `contact/constitution == "al-ipc"` (this step's switch does, unconditionally). It is built only
  under `require<ALIPCPipelineFlag>()`, so no scene in the suite exercises it.
- **`clang-format` is still not installed on this box** (s04 recorded the same), so no step in this
  round can run the repo's format check.

## s06 — the conservative bound pays where the distance is already computed: a compacted candidate array for the DCD narrow phase

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-13-round6-s06/`
(`predictions.txt`, `ccd_compact_probe.{cu,txt}`, `ccd_compact_probe_5M.txt`, `insitu_verify_tumbler.txt`,
`sass_identity.txt`, `sass_resusage.txt`, `scope_all.txt`, `accounting.txt`, `cub_accounting.txt`,
`rerank_c1.txt`, `gate.txt`, `gate_c0.txt`, `ab/`, `verify/`, `nsys/`).

**Verdict: accepted, default on.** s05 proved the bound and proved the *placement* wrong. Moving it to
the one kernel that already evaluates the distance — and compacting the candidate array with it —
makes it free: `filter_active` runs **−84 % / −82 %** per launch on the tumbler and the four-kernel
accounting closes to **0.2 pp** against the scene. **`mas-bunny` −0.53 % `meanFrameMs`, disjoint,
p=4.4e-04 at Newton 465 in all ten runs; `stiff-gipc-case2` −1.88 % mean and −2.02 % ms/Newton, both
disjoint (p=2.4e-03 / 8.2e-05) with the iteration guard silent.** The active contact set handed to
contact assembly is **provably unchanged, sequence for sequence**, which is what makes this a
bit-identical change rather than a conservative-with-an-argument one.

### The brief's premise, checked first — and it is right, but two of its details are not

The brief (from s05's own candidate 1) proposed: *append the ~9 % survivors and their `toi` to a
compacted array, resize, and let `filter_active` run over 9 % of the pairs.* Three corrections, all
of which make the step smaller and safer:

1. **Only the DCD half of s05's condition is needed.** The compacted array is read by `filter_active`
   and by nothing else — `filter_toi` has already run over the full array by the time the compaction
   exists. So the CCD half (`(1-eta)(d0-thickness) > toc_prev*m`) is not required at all, and the cull
   is `(d0 - thickness) > d_hat + m` alone. That is strictly *looser* than s05's conjunction, so
   s05's 91.1 % / 91.7 % drop rate is a floor: measured in situ, **90.7 %** of a billion real tumbler
   candidates (the two are not the same population — s05 measured its rate at `detect` time).
2. **The `toi` must NOT be compacted, and neither must the candidate arrays themselves.** s06 writes
   the survivors into *separate* buffers and leaves `candidate_*_pairs` and `tois` exactly as they
   were. That is what keeps every position-indexed consumer on its old contract: the AL-IPC
   `GlobalActiveSetManager` (which reads `candidate_PTs()/EEs()` **and** `toi_PTs()/toi_EEs()` and
   builds `*_max_vertex_min_toi` per candidate), the `dcd_candidate_reuse_verify` snapshots, and the
   `dump_candidates` exporter. s05 had to force its cull off under `contact/constitution == "al-ipc"`;
   **s06 does not need that guard at all**, because AL-IPC never sees the compacted array.
3. **The ceiling arithmetic is right for three scenes and wrong for one.** Recomputed from s05's own
   `scope_*.txt`: tumbler `(529.0-73.0)+(303.0-47.4)` µs x 1816 launches = **−1292.3 ms / −5.69 %**
   (the brief's number, confirmed); `stiff-gipc-case2` **−940.4 ms / −2.16 %** (brief: −975 / −2.2 %,
   confirmed); `cube-wall-cloth` **−93.1 ms / −1.49 %**, *not* the brief's −119 ms / −1.9 %. All of
   this step's predictions were written down before any measurement (`predictions.txt`), including a
   coverage correction the brief does not have: the compaction only exists between a `filter_toi` and
   the next `detect`, and the frame's **first** `filter_active` — the one after
   `detect_dcd_candidates(0)` — has no `filter_toi` in front of it, so 90.1 % (tumbler) / 94.1 %
   (case2) / 83.1 % (cwc) / 98.7 % (mas-bunny) of `filter_active` launches can be covered at all.

### The change

`distance::{edge_edge,point_triangle,point_edge,point_point}_ccd` gain a fourth template parameter
`bool DcdCull` and two trailing defaulted arguments `T d_hat, uint8_t* dcd_keep`. When `DcdCull` is
on, at the point where the function has *already* computed `dist2_cur` and `maxDispMag`:

```
const T dcd_lim = (thickness + d_hat + maxDispMag) * (T(1) + T(1e-9));
*dcd_keep       = (dist2_cur > dcd_lim * dcd_lim) ? 0 : 1;
```

Two adds, two multiplies, a compare and a byte store; no sqrt (the test is done in squares, both
sides non-negative) and **no extra loads at all**. It reads the *first* `dist2_cur` — the one
`edge_edge_ccd`'s degenerate fall-back would otherwise overwrite with a **larger** endpoint distance
— so the fall-back cannot make the verdict unsafe. A zero `maxDispMag` stores 1 = keep.

`filter_toi_k1..k4` gain `bool Compact` and a trailing `uint8_t* keep`; `filter_toi` then runs
`cub::DeviceSelect::Flagged` (**order preserving**) per candidate array into four `compact_*` buffers
and reads the four counts back in one D2H that lands immediately before
`GlobalTrajectoryFilter::filter_toi`'s own blocking read of the per-filter toi, so it costs no extra
pipeline drain. `filter_active` reads the compacted arrays when they exist; `detect` clears the flag
that says they do. `UIPC_CCD_COMPACT=0` restores the old path; `UIPC_CCD_COMPACT_VERIFY=1` turns on
the two-pass in-situ check below.

**`filter_active_k1..k4` are not touched at all** — the same kernels, byte for byte, launched over a
shorter array.

### The register trap, recorded because it was invisible in the source

The obvious shape — have the ACCD return the verdict through a `bool*` and let the kernel do
`keep[i] = ...` after the call — costs **+15 registers on `filter_toi_k4` (218 -> 233)** and +8 on
`k3` (184 -> 192). On cc 7.5 that crosses a register granule: 218 regs allow 9 resident warps per SM,
233 allow 8; 184 allow 11, 192 allow 10. `best_block_dim` calls `cudaOccupancyMaxPotentialBlockSize`
**per kernel function pointer**, so the two arms would have launched at *different block sizes* — the
exact confound s04 went to trouble to avoid. Moving the store **inside** the ACCD, at the point where
the distance is first available, kills the pointer before the loop starts and puts both instantiations
back at **REG 218 / 184, STACK 0, LOCAL 0**, identical to main's. Cost of the compacting
instantiation: **+16 SASS instructions on `k4`, +24 on `k3`**.

### The old arm reproduces main's binary exactly

Standalone compile of the filter's TU with the build's exact flags at `711d2300` and at this head,
SASS-diffed (`sass_identity.txt`). The new template argument renames all 16 `filter_toi`
instantiations, so those are matched by body hash:

| | |
|---|---|
| common SASS streams identical | **88 / 88** — including `filter_active_k1..k4`, `pairFilter` x4, `stacklessSelf/Other`, every `detect_*` leaf predicate |
| base-only (renamed) `filter_toi_k1..k4<EarlyOut, Stats>` found **byte-identical** in head | **16 / 16** |
| registers, `filter_toi_k4/k3`, both arms | **218 / 184**, STACK 0, LOCAL 0 — unchanged from main |
| new in head | the 16 `Compact = true` instantiations, and one `cub::DeviceSelectSweepKernel<Vector2i>` |

### Numerics: the active contact set is provably unchanged, sequence for sequence

This is stronger than "conservative". Three facts compose into bit-identity:

1. **Every dropped pair is inactive over the whole swept step.** `filter_active` keeps a pair iff
   `d < thickness + d_hat`; the relative motion of the two primitives is bounded by `maxDispMag` per
   unit `t` (the ACCD's own bound), the line search only applies `alpha_ls <= alpha_detect`, so
   `d(t) >= d0 - maxDispMag > thickness + d_hat` for every `t` in `[0, 1]`.
2. **`cub::DeviceSelect::Flagged` is order preserving**, so the compacted array is an ordered
   *subsequence* of the raw one — checked element-wise, not assumed (below).
3. Therefore the compacted run's `temp_*` arrays are the subsequence of the raw run's restricted to
   surviving candidates, and since `DeviceSelect::If` is order preserving too, the four selected
   active sets are **equal as sequences iff their four sizes agree**.

**Standalone, against the real shipped device function** (`ccd_compact_probe.cu`,
`ccd_compact_probe_5M.txt`): 5x10^6 samples per pair type, **half adversarial** (near-parallel edges,
near-degenerate triangles, zero-length edges, coincident points, zero relative displacement,
separations placed *exactly* at `thickness + d_hat`, asymmetric per-primitive thickness, `d_hat`
log-uniform over 1e-4..1e-2 m). The verdict comes out of
`distance::*_ccd<Float, EarlyOut=true, Stats=false, DcdCull=true>` itself, and for every dropped
sample the probe walks the swept segment at 221 points:

| | EE | PT | PE | PP |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| samples | 5 000 000 | 5 000 000 | 5 000 000 | 5 000 000 |
| dropped | 27.13 % | 22.86 % | 29.70 % | 27.51 % |
| active somewhere on [0,1] over the whole population (sanity) | 33.25 % | 30.61 % | 26.26 % | 31.90 % |
| **dropped but ACTIVE somewhere on [0,1]** | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |
| **dropped but within `thickness` somewhere on [0,1]** | **0** | **0** | **0** | **0** |
| dropped and active only *past* t=1 (informational) | 19 | 12 | 29 | 143 |

The last row is worth keeping: a handful of samples per five million become active just **outside**
the domain of the claim. The bound is tight, not vacuous — it is not culling pairs that were never
going to matter.

**In situ, on the real population** (`UIPC_CCD_COMPACT_VERIFY=1`, one full run of **all five**
scenes; `insitu_verify_tumbler.txt`, `insitu_verify_other.txt`). `filter_active` runs its whole pass
**twice** — once over the raw candidate arrays, once over the compacted ones — and compares the four
active-set sizes; `filter_toi` downloads the raw array, the flags and the compacted array and checks
element-wise that the latter is exactly the ordered subsequence the flags select:

| scene | candidate pairs | dropped | active pairs produced | active-set size mismatches | contents mismatches |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| tumbler-garments | **1 056 871 554** | **90.73 %** | 40 765 709 | **0 / 1600** | **0 / 1200** |
| stiff-gipc-case2 | 773 960 982 | 92.00 % | 28 894 953 | **0 / 1800** | **0 / 1600** |
| cube-wall-cloth | 53 118 066 | 77.43 % | 10 653 487 | **0 / 400** | **0 / 400** |
| rigid-wrecking-balls | 44 536 097 | 71.28 % | 10 369 342 | **0 / 400** | **0 / 400** |
| mas-bunny | 15 219 166 | **99.71 %** | 2 797 | **0 / 400** | **0 / 400** |
| **total** | **1.94 x 10^9** | | 90.7 M | **0** | **0** |

So over nearly two billion real pairs at the real line-search positions, on every scene in the suite,
contact assembly receives the identical four arrays in the identical order. (`mas-bunny`'s 99.71 %
is why 348 of its 465 `filter_active_k4` launches disappear entirely — the same effect s05 saw.) Newton and PCG counts therefore cannot be moved by this
change, and `ms_per_newton` is legitimate wherever the harness's guard fires (the s04 reading).

### Performance, targeted scope: n=3 full runs per arm, one build, five scenes

`nsys --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` over **complete** benchmark runs, fresh prefix per run.
(`scope_all.txt`, `accounting.txt`.)

| µs/launch | `filter_active_k4` (DCD EE) | `filter_active_k3` (DCD PT) | `filter_toi_k4` | `filter_toi_k3` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **tumbler-garments** | 484.2 -> 75.9 **−84.3 %** | 277.5 -> 49.0 **−82.3 %** | +3.1 % | +2.7 % |
| **stiff-gipc-case2** | 355.9 -> 47.0 **−86.8 %** | 195.9 -> 42.3 **−78.4 %** | +2.4 % | +2.4 % |
| **cube-wall-cloth** | 172.5 -> 64.9 **−62.4 %** | 103.7 -> 55.2 **−46.7 %** | +2.4 % | +2.1 % |
| **mas-bunny** | 43.9 -> 8.9 **−79.7 %** | 40.7 -> 8.2 **−80.0 %** | +1.2 % | +0.9 % |
| **rigid-wrecking-balls** | 107.1 -> 40.6 **−62.1 %** | 71.8 -> 39.4 **−45.1 %** | +3.7 % | +1.8 % |

All ten `filter_active` deltas have disjoint per-run ranges. The `filter_toi` column is the price:
the keep-flag store plus four arithmetic ops, **+1 to +4 %**, exactly the size the +16/+24 SASS
instructions predict. On `mas-bunny` and `cube-wall-cloth` the compacted array is often empty and the
kernel is then **not launched at all** (471 -> 123 / 471 -> 107 launches on mas-bunny, 589 -> 447 on
cube-wall-cloth), which is why those two read a smaller per-launch delta than the others and a bigger
total one.

**The cub stream compaction is a net win, not a cost** (`cub_accounting.txt`). The four new
`DeviceSelect::Flagged` passes are smaller than the shrink of `filter_active`'s own four
`DeviceSelect::If` passes, because the `temp_PP`/`temp_PE` arrays it selects over are sized as the
*sum* of the four candidate arrays:

| scene | `DeviceCompactInit` | `DeviceSelectSweep` | net |
|---|---|---|---|
| tumbler | +3.2 ms | −60.5 ms | **−57.3 ms (−0.26 % of scene)** |
| case2 | +3.7 ms | −52.9 ms | **−49.1 ms** |
| cube-wall-cloth | +1.1 ms | −5.6 ms | −4.6 ms |
| mas-bunny | +0.9 ms | −3.6 ms | −2.6 ms |
| rigid-wrecking-balls | +1.0 ms | −3.9 ms | −2.9 ms |

**The accounting, and how it is normalised.** The tumbler drew +3.2 % more Newton iterations in the
new arm over these three runs (`filter_toi_k4` launches 1425 -> 1470), so its *raw* scene total is
unreadable; dividing scene GPU kernel time by `filter_toi` launches removes it. The other four scenes
move <2 %.

| scene | four targeted kernels (ms) | + cub | = predicted | scene GPU kernel time / `filter_toi` launch |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| tumbler-garments | −1072.1 | −57.3 | **−5.05 %** | **−5.26 %** |
| stiff-gipc-case2 | −893.9 | −49.1 | **−2.16 %** | −1.57 % |
| cube-wall-cloth | −103.3 | −4.6 | **−1.75 %** | −1.57 % |
| mas-bunny | −37.3 | −2.6 | **−0.61 %** | −0.75 % |
| rigid-wrecking-balls | −59.1 | −2.9 | **−1.69 %** | −0.64 % (Newton −2.0 % between arms; unreliable) |

The tumbler closes to **0.2 pp**, which is s05's standard. On `stiff-gipc-case2` the gap is 0.6 pp in
the other direction and it is scatter, not a hidden cost: the untouched families read +1.0 to +1.3 %
in the new arm at n=3, but their per-run ranges overlap (spmv c0 7513.8 / 7623.6 / 7723.8 vs c1
7662.1 / 7739.1 / 7762.8), and the n=5 end-to-end below lands on −2.02 %, i.e. on the accounting
rather than on the n=3 normalised total.

### Performance, end to end: ABBA + one discarded warm-up, one build

| scene | predicted | `meanFrameMs` | `ms_per_newton` | Newton / PCG | n |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| **mas-bunny** | −0.61 to −0.75 % | **−0.53 % (DISJOINT, p=4.4e-04)** | −0.53 % (DISJOINT) | **+0.00 % / −0.01 %**, Newton 465 in all ten runs | 5 |
| **stiff-gipc-case2** | −1.57 to −2.16 % | **−1.88 % (DISJOINT, p=2.4e-03)** | **−2.02 % (DISJOINT, p=8.2e-05)** | +0.14 % / +0.50 % — guard silent | 5 |
| **cube-wall-cloth** | −1.57 to −1.75 % | −1.37 % (overlapping, p=0.41) | −1.86 % (p=0.040) | +0.51 % / −0.17 % | 5 |
| **rigid-wrecking-balls** | −1.69 % | −1.73 % (overlapping, p=0.17) | −1.35 % (p=0.031) | −0.38 % / −0.34 % — guard silent | 6 |
| **tumbler-garments** | −5.05 to −5.26 % | −2.06 % (overlapping) | −2.31 % | +0.30 % / **+3.92 %** — **guard fired** | 5 |

**No scene regresses on any statistic.** The two deterministic-ish instruments carry the result:
`mas-bunny` runs Newton 465 and PCG flat to 0.01 % in all ten runs, so `meanFrameMs` *is* its
throughput statistic, and it reads −0.53 % disjoint against a predicted −0.61 %; `stiff-gipc-case2`
reads −1.88 % / −2.02 % disjoint with Newton +0.14 % and PCG +0.50 %.

**The tumbler is unreadable and that is the honest reading**: PCG moved **+3.92 %** against a 2.06 %
wall change, so the harness's guard fired and the wall number is a different scene state, not
throughput. Its evidence is the scope gate (−5.26 % normalised, −5.05 % accounted) and the fact that
the four other scenes land on their predictions. This is the same conclusion the round record already
records for this scene: it cannot resolve its own biggest wins.

### Gates

- **Correctness**: `bash /workspace/output/round6/gate.sh` against `baseline_tests.txt` in **both**
  arms — identical assertion counts on all eight suites (11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23,
  **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped). The only textual difference in either file is pytest's
  own wall-clock string (1.20 vs 1.21 / 1.22 s). The gate that ran with the compaction **on** is the
  stronger one: `sim_case`'s 14 213 assertions all pass with 91 % of the DCD candidate array removed.
- **Numerics**: the two proofs above (2x10^7 randomised samples with half adversarial, and 1.06x10^9
  real candidate pairs in situ with 0 active-set mismatches over 1600 `filter_active` calls and 0
  contents mismatches over 1200 `filter_toi` calls).
- **Env-switch audit at kernel level**: `UIPC_CCD_COMPACT=0` restores the old per-launch cost exactly
  on all five scenes (a 45-87 % per-launch difference on `filter_active` is not something a broken
  switch could fake), and the old arm's 16 `filter_toi` instantiations plus all 88 other kernels in
  the TU are byte-identical to main's SASS.

### Transfer prediction

**Algorithmic — it transfers.** The step launches `filter_active` over 9 % of the threads, writes 9 %
of the `temp_*` traffic, and selects over 9 % of it, at an unchanged output. Nothing about it depends
on occupancy, register pressure, spill traffic or wave quantisation — and the register trap above was
found and removed precisely so that it does not.

Three caveats a rented run should test:
- the work removed from `filter_active` is a **mix**: an FP64 distance-flag + distance evaluation
  (which this 1/32-rate part over-rewards) and ~36 B/pair of `temp_*` writes (which it does not). The
  per-launch −62 to −87 % should hold, but the FP64 half of it shrinks on a modern part.
- the **share** of `filter_active` in scene GPU kernel time is what sets the end-to-end number, and
  shares move with architecture (§6). On cc 7.5 it is **6.16 %** of the tumbler, 2.95 % of
  rigid-wrecking-balls, 2.64 % of cube-wall-cloth, 2.58 % of stiff-gipc-case2 and 0.61 % of
  mas-bunny.
- the **drop rate is a property of the workload, not the hardware**: 90.7 % on the tumbler over a
  billion pairs, and the per-launch deltas on five very different scenes agree in sign and to within
  40 points, so the mechanism reproduces anywhere.

180 frames, **40 runs per arm**, one build (`verify/`, `verify_compare.txt`). s05 recorded 1 of 14
cull-arm runs aborting on a `FusedPCG Init: r^T*z = nan` against 0 of 14, called it unresolvable at
p = 0.50, and required the next step reusing the predicate to re-characterise it at n >= 40 per arm.

| `bore_gap_min` | 0.8148 mm [0.636, 0.959] | 0.8116 mm [0.640, 0.985] |

**0 of 80 runs aborted.** With 0 events in 40 runs the rule-of-three 95 % upper bound on either arm's
abort rate is **7.5 %**, so s05's point estimate of 7.1 % (1 in 14) is right at that edge and is not
formally excluded — but it did not reproduce, there is no asymmetry between the arms, and this step's
numerics proof says there cannot be one: the two arms hand contact assembly *bit-identical* input, so
they are draws from the same distribution by construction rather than by argument. The honest
statement is that **the s05 event is now bounded at <= 7.5 % and is not attributable to the
predicate**; a validation pass that wants a tighter bound needs n >= 60 per arm.

`tri_height_min` reaches 2.97 mm (c0) and 3.05 mm (c1) — **both** arms cross the 3.27 mm `2r + d_hat`
margin s00 measured on a single run, which is the confirmation s04 asked for that the statistic is a
property of the scene's membrane and not of any CCD change.

### Measurement doctrine after s11 (coordinator) — what this round has retired

Three instruments have now been disqualified for concurrency work, each by a measurement rather than
by argument. Recorded together because a later round will otherwise reach for them:

| instrument | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| `nsys stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` scene totals | **retired for scene totals** (s10) | a duration *sum*: it grows every time the round adds overlap. rwb sum/union went 1.060 (pre-K9) → 1.075 (s09) → **1.130** (s10). s10's winning arm reads **+0.9 %** here and −5.2 % by union. Still valid per kernel |
| GPU **busy union** from `cuda_gpu_trace` | **retired for ranking placements** (s11) | Spearman **rho = +0.10** against wall across five arms — it gave its *best* score to the *worst* arm. Per-arm sd 0.3–1.7 % against arms 4–9 pp apart, so this is not noise |
| repo `WB_TIMER=1` Timer tree | **unusable for any concurrency question** (s11) | `sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ wait_device(); })`, so every scope boundary synchronises the device — destroying the overlap and host run-ahead the arms differ in. Nothing at the call site says so |

**What remains for a step that changes where work is issued: end-to-end wall time, n large, through
`ab.py`.** Per-kernel `kern_sum` numbers stay valid for steps that change a kernel's own cost.

### Consequences for what the round already believes

- **s10's win stands and is confirmed at higher n**: s11 re-measured it at n=30 without a profiler at
  **−4.97 % ms/Newton (p=2.3e-21)**. What does *not* stand is s10's *ranking* of the placements — at
  n=8 it read `=2` as better and its trace called `=2` "does not work"; at n=30 modes 1, 2 and 4 are
  mutually indistinguishable on rwb (Welch p = 0.59–0.95). The placement does not matter on that scene.
- **s09's split conclusion is unaffected** — it compared union *and* wall and both agreed there.
- **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` no longer measures the split alone**, because modes 3/4 of the
  prepass only fire when the K9 split is taken. Pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms.

### And a 1-in-20 accident, caught live

s11's `stiff-gipc-case2` control — a scene that launches **zero** ABD G/H kernels — read **−0.46 %,
p=0.034, "DISJOINT" at n=4**, and reversed to +0.12 %, p=0.58 at n=6. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says a round of
twenty measurements manufactures one of these; this is the round's, on a scene where the effect is
known to be exactly zero.
