### §8 The crease micro-test, run against this change as a null

V1's `crease_micro.py` unchanged, 6 runs per arm, 200 frames, θ reaching **3.115 rad** (a complete
fold). Contact is disabled in that scene, so the contact Hessian is never assembled and the two arms
must be indistinguishable — this is an instrument check, not a physics test.

In **seven of the eight** crease-severity bins the two arms agree **to three decimal places on the
mean Newton count and on PCG**. The eighth (θ_p95 ∈ [2.0, 3.5)) reads 5.293 vs 5.280 (−0.25 %,
p = 0.91). That is not a leak: the scene is not bit-reproducible run to run — arm 0's own six runs
total 778, 778, 779, 779, 778, 778 Newton iterations — and the per-frame trace differs between two
runs of the **same** arm on 61–81 lines against 77–102 lines between arms. The cross-arm difference
is inside the within-arm null. Zero non-converged frames, zero limit hits, all positions finite, in
both arms.

### The contact-severity micro-test — built for this change, and it is the strongest instrument here

`contact_micro.py`, `micro.txt`. The device probe above says the dropped curvature is tangential, so
the case has to slide as well as press. Six cloth patches stacked with a lateral offset of half an
element in x and a third in z (so a vertex lands over an **edge**, not over a vertex), gravity and
every other load **off**, the top and bottom patches soft-position-constrained as cloth platens, the
top one driven down (and optionally sideways) on a linear ramp. Severity is **measured**, not
assumed: `g = (clearance − 2r)/d_hat`, recomputed from the retrieved positions every frame, where
g = 1 is the activation distance and g = 0 is touching.

**The pair population in this scene is PE 16 893 / PP 0** (the engine's own
`SimplexTrajectoryFilter` line): **100 % of contact part 2's pairs take the branch
`UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1=1` approximates**, where the tumbler's PE:PP is about 3:1. That is what makes it
a better instrument than any benchmark scene for this question.

Five configurations, 5 runs per arm, 80 frames, arms interleaved:

| config | press / drag | severity reached (min g) | Newton total, arm 0 | Newton total, arm 1 | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `base` | 9.0 mm / 0 | −0.61 | 292, 292, 292, 292, 292 | 291, 292, 292, 292, 292 | **−0.07 %** |
| `deep` | 11.5 mm / 0 | −1.07 | 289 ×4, 290 | 289 ×4, 290 | **+0.00 %** |
| `slide` | 9.0 mm / 30 mm | −2.15 | 440, 443, 444, 446, 448 | 440, 441, 441, 444, 445 | **−0.45 %** |
| `fastslide` | 11.5 mm / 70 mm | −6.22 | 505, 506, 507, 510, 511 | 506, 508, 509, 509, 511 | **+0.16 %** |
| `tight` (vel-tol 2e-5) | 11.5 mm / 30 mm | −3.35 | 483, 483, 484, 484, 484 | 483, 483, 484, 484, 485 | **+0.04 %** |

Binned by measured severity — the pre-declared reading is that Newton counts must rise in arm 1
relative to arm 0 **as severity rises, with the gap growing**:

| `g` bin (deeper downward) | `base` Δ | `slide` Δ | `fastslide` Δ | `tight` Δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1.00, ∞) | +0.0 % | −2.5 % | −0.9 % | +0.0 % |
| [0.75, 1.00) | −0.1 % | −1.3 % | +0.7 % | +0.2 % |
| [0.50, 0.75) | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % |
| [0.35, 0.50) | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % |
| [0.20, 0.35) | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % |
| [0.10, 0.20) | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | — | — |
| [0.00, 0.10) | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | — | +0.0 % |
| below 0 | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % | +0.0 % |

**In every configuration, at every severity from the activation distance down to full compression,
arm 1's mean Newton count is equal to arm 0's or lower. It is never higher.** Zero non-converged
frames, zero `hit_newton_limit`, zero `hit_line_search_limit`, all positions finite, in all 50 runs.

One honest note on the automated verdict line: the analyser's mechanical test
(`slope > 0 and p < 0.05`) prints **PRESENT** for `slide` (slope +0.0028 per bin, p = 0.033). Reading
the table rather than the slope, that "positive trend" is a **negative** gap (−2.5 %, −1.3 %)
shrinking to zero — arm 1 is *better* at low severity and identical at high severity, and it does not
exceed arm 0 in any bin of any configuration. The pre-declared signature is a *rise*; there is none.
The other four configurations return `absent` outright.

The safety question in this scene has a direct answer too: **the softened tangential curvature does
not let the layers approach further.** Minimum `g` reached per run, arm 1 against arm 0, is
indistinguishable in four of five configurations (p = 0.14, 0.84, 0.15, 0.21) and differs in the
fifth (`base`, p = 0.011) by **1e-5 of d_hat** — 8 picometres.

**Within reachable configurations — five decades of contact severity, sliding at up to 70 mm over
80 frames, and a tolerance tightened 2 500× from the scene default — there is no regime where the
rank-1 PE contact Hessian degrades convergence.** That is the part of this pass that generalises
past our scenes, and it agrees with the device probe: the approximation is only inaccurate where the
matrix it approximates is numerically zero.

### Cost re-measured at this head — the win is intact and slightly larger

s08 landed in the same kernel family since s07's n = 50 sweep, so the cost claim was re-measured.
`cube-wall-cloth`, n = 20 per arm, four arms in one interleaved sweep, one build (`cost_cwc.txt`).
`pnull` is a second copy of the rollback (`=00`, which `std::atoi` reads as 0), so the null envelope
is measured beside the effects.

| | p0 (exact) | pnull (null twin) | p5 (shipped) | **p1 (the change)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Newton | 511.45 ± 8.95 | 508.85 (−0.51 %) | 507.05 (−0.86 %) | **509.95 (−0.29 %, p = 0.73)** |
| PCG | 19 927 | −0.46 % | −0.34 % | **−1.73 % (p = 0.047)** |
| line-search | 532.75 | −0.70 % | −1.05 % | **−0.01 % (p = 0.99)** |
| `mean_ms` | 60.240 | −0.78 % | −1.23 % | **−4.07 % (p = 4.1e-04)** |
| **`ms_per_newton`** | 11.7775 | −0.28 % | −0.37 % | **−3.81 % (p = 2.5e-15)** |
| `ms_per_pcg` | 0.3023 | −0.32 % | −0.89 % | −2.41 % |

**The Newton drift is still absent** (−0.29 %, inside the null twin's own −0.51 %), and the win is
**−3.81 % ms/Newton**, a little larger than s07's −3.23 % — consistent with s08 having shortened
part 1, which changes part 2's share of the critical path.

Two things to report rather than bury:

- **The cube-wall-cloth Newton tail landed in the p1 arm this time.** p1's maximum is 579 against
  p0's 533, with sd 16.6 against 8.95. s07 saw the same event at n = 50 on the **exact default**
  (577) and on the **null twin** (550). Pooled over s07 and this pass, runs above Newton 530 are
  **3 of 140 on non-approximating arms** (577, 550, 533) and **2 of 70 on p1** (555, 579) — Fisher
  exact p = 1.0. It is a property of the scene, as s07 concluded, and one more draw does not change
  that; but the draw fell on the approximating arm here and that is worth recording.
- **`cloth_min_y`**, the scene's own physics observable, reads p0 0.2862, pnull 0.2875 (+0.43 %),
  p5 0.2860 (−0.09 %), **p1 0.2910 (+1.66 %, raw p = 0.015)**. That is 0.8 sd and 23 % of p0's own
  run-to-run range, and Holm-adjusted across the sweep's 24 comparisons it is ≈ 0.36 — but it is the
  only physics observable that moves anywhere in this pass, so it was re-measured at n = 40 rather
  than argued away (below).

### Env-switch audit, at kernel level

`envaudit.txt`, nsys `cuda_gpu_kern_sum` over 12-frame tumbler runs, reading the demangled template
arguments of the kernels actually launched. All three arms are proved on the binary:

| arm | contact part 1 | contact part 2 | hinge kernel |
|---|---|---|---|
| `UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1=0` | `do_assemble_kernel<0,1,1,1,0,**8**>` | `<0,2,0,0,1,**0**>` exact | `DiscreteShellBending_..._kernel<3,1>` |
| default (unset) | `<0,1,1,1,0,**8**>` | `<0,2,0,0,1,**5**>` PP closed form | `<3,1>` |
| `UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1=1` | `<0,1,1,1,0,**8**>` | `<0,2,0,0,1,**1**>` PE+PP rank-1 | `<3,1>` |

**Part 1 is `Proj = 8` in all three arms** — s08's basis-free reduction is the default and is *not*
part of the comparison, so A-vs-B isolates contact part 2 exactly as the design intends. The hinge
kernel is `<3,1>` (s02's Gauss-Newton, the shipped default) in all three, so nothing about s02 is
being re-litigated here.

### Correctness gate

`gate.sh` in all three arms — the rollback (`=0`), the shipped default (unset) and the change under
test (`=1`) — **identical to `baseline_tests.txt` in every assertion count**: 11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46,
100/3, 4/1, 448/23, **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped 86 deselected. The only textual
difference in any of the three is pytest's wall-clock line (1.22 s against the baseline's 1.20 s).
`sim_case`'s 95 cases include the contact suite.

### The one physical observable that moves, and what it is

`cloth_min_y` on `cube-wall-cloth` — the lowest cloth vertex at frame 100, and the only physics
observable that scene reports — moved in the n = 20 cost sweep. It was **not** argued away: it was
re-measured twice more (n = 40 and n = 25, all at the default tolerance), and every sweep is positive.

| | exact (p0) | null twin (`=00`) | shipped (`=5`) | **rank-1 (`=1`)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| n = 20 | 0.28623 | +0.43 % | −0.09 % | **+1.66 %** |
| n = 40 | 0.28652 | −0.13 % | — | **+1.04 %** |
| n = 25 | 0.28751 | — | — | **+0.97 %** |
| **pooled, n = 85** | **0.286741** | **−0.05 % [−0.62, +0.52], p = 0.86** (n = 60) | −0.26 % (n = 20) | **+1.17 % [+0.56 %, +1.77 %], p = 2.1e-04** |

That is a real, one-directional, reproducible shift, about twenty times the bit-identical null twin's,
and it is the kind of thing this pass exists to find. So it was diagnosed rather than weighed.

**The diagnosis, with the reading fixed before it ran** (`TRUNCATION_PREDICTION.md`). Mode 1 changes
only the Hessian; the energy and the gradient are untouched (s03, s07), so **both arms have the same
Newton fixed point — they solve the same equations.** A systematic difference in the accepted state
can therefore only be *where the solver stops*: `cube-wall-cloth` exits on an increment /
accumulated-β rule, and a different model Hessian gives a different increment at the same state. The
prediction: if that is the mechanism, the gap must shrink when both arms converge harder; if it is a
change of the simulated physics, it must not. `UIPC_CWC_TIGHT=<k>` divides both Newton stopping
tolerances by k (default k = 1 leaves every literal as it was, so the benchmark scene stays
byte-stable).

| Newton tolerance | Newton count | exact | rank-1 | **gap** | 95 % CI | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| default (×1), n = 85 | 509.0 | 0.286741 | 0.290084 | **+1.17 %** | [+0.56 %, +1.77 %] | 2.1e-04 |
| **×10 tighter**, n = 25 | 659.0 (+29 %) | 0.286828 | 0.289057 | **+0.78 %** | [−0.24 %, +1.79 %] | 0.14 |
| **×100 tighter**, n = 30 | 686.0 (+35 %) | 0.286402 | 0.287580 | **+0.41 %** | [−0.53 %, +1.36 %] | 0.40 |

**−0.377 percentage points per decade of tolerance, r = −1.000, monotone.** The pre-declared
truncation signature is present and the alternative is excluded: the gap does not survive
convergence. It is a convergence-quality difference bounded by the tolerance the user chooses, not a
different simulated world — which is what the identical energy and gradient already imply, now
measured rather than asserted.

**Reported at its full size anyway**: at the tolerance `cube-wall-cloth` actually ships with, the
accepted state differs by **+1.2 % of `cloth_min_y`**, which is 0.7 σ of that scene's own
run-to-run spread. Anyone who needs the last percent of that observable should tighten the
tolerance, and this is now measurable because the knob exists.

### Verdict: ship it

Against the rule fixed at `877d0cf9`, before the first arm ran:

- **§3 safety — pass.** 3 620 frames in 20 runs: zero non-finite positions, zero containment
  failures, zero lifter penetrations, zero inversions, zero interpenetrations, zero non-converged
  frames, zero `hit_newton_limit`, zero `hit_line_search_limit`, `verify_ok` true in all 20. B is not
  the worst arm on a single one of the seven worst-value statistics. The one `verify_ok` failure in
  the pass belongs to the **shipped default**.
- **§4 the membrane family — pass, with the envelope.** No member is significant (all Holm p = 1.00),
  and the statistic the decision turns on has a 95 % CI of **[−2.50 %, +7.47 %]** on median areal
  strain against a pre-registered fail line of **+10 %**. The null of the null — A against A′ —
  produces +1.34 %, so B's +1.99 % is what seed size alone does.
- **§5 the run maximum — does not escalate, and points the other way.** B has the **tightest**
  distribution of all four arms (range ratio 0.167), the **lowest** tail rate at every threshold, and
  the eight largest run maxima in the pass all belong to a non-approximating arm.
- **§6 the screen and the joint signature — pass.** Nothing flagged among 36 observables; the
  `mean_disp_mm_last_quarter` pairing s07 flagged does not reproduce (p = 0.31).
- **§7 cost — the claim holds and is larger.** Newton drift −0.29 % (inside the null twin's −0.51 %);
  **ms/Newton −3.81 %, p = 2.5e-15** at n = 20, −3.18 % at n = 25.
- **§8 the micro-tests — no reachable degradation regime.** In five configurations of a
  **100 % PE** contact case, across five decades of severity, arm 1's Newton count is never higher
  than arm 0's in any bin. The crease null is indistinguishable inside its own within-arm scatter.
- **Gate — identical in all three arms**, `sim_case` 14213/95 included.
- The one physical observable that moves is **solver truncation, proved by a pre-declared
  tolerance sweep**, and it shrinks monotonically toward zero as both arms converge.

**Recommendation: flip `UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1` to default 1.** The `=0` rollback works and is proved at
kernel level; it should stay. **This pass does not flip it — that is the coordinator's call.**

### Limits, stated plainly

- **N runs is N runs.** 20 per arm on the tumbler (80 full runs, 14 480 frames), 85 per arm on
  `cube-wall-cloth`'s `cloth_min_y` at the default tolerance but only 25 and 30 at the two tighter
  ones — **the ×10 and ×100 points are individually consistent with zero and with the default gap**;
  what carries the truncation conclusion is the monotone trend across three points plus the
  a-priori argument that the two arms have the same fixed point, not either point alone.
- **n was not raised above 20 on the tumbler**, because the pre-registered marginality condition was
  not met: no §4 statistic has Holm p < 0.05, and the two positive shifts are +1.99 % and +1.95 %
  against a +10 % line. Raising n would narrow the CI; it would not change which side of the line it
  is on.
- **Two scenes.** The physics comparison is `tumbler-garments` (36 observables, four arms) and
  `cube-wall-cloth` (one observable, four arms). `stiff-gipc-case2`, `rigid-wrecking-balls` and
  `mas-bunny` were not given a physics pass — s07's iteration-count evidence is what covers them, and
  mode 1 buys nothing on rwb anyway.
- **One GPU.** Everything is cc 7.5. The iteration counts and the physics are architecture-independent;
  the −3.81 % is not, and s07's transfer note stands — much of mode 1's per-launch win is a
  255 → 154 register occupancy jump, which PERF_METHOD §6 says can flip sign on another part.
- **`cc_min_dist` is a vertex-vertex proxy** for the true point-triangle gap, as V1 recorded. It
  bounds it from above, it is the same proxy in every arm, and it never reached zero — but no
  triangle-level self-intersection test was run.
- **The micro-test's severity variable breaks down past full compression.** `g` is an interpolated
  vertical clearance; once the free sheets wrinkle it reads negative, which the true point-triangle
  gap cannot. It is monotone and identical in both arms, which is what a severity axis needs, but the
  negative values are a proxy artefact and are labelled as such.
- **No sanitizers this pass.** V1 ran memcheck / racecheck / initcheck at `7316bbc5` and reproduced
  both findings on a `perf-round5-base` binary; s04–s08 have not been re-checked under them. That is
  a gap for whoever runs the round's final validation, not something this pass closes.
- **The device probe samples a distribution, not the scenes' own pairs.** Its sweep is log-uniform in
  `d_hat`, `kappa` and gap, which is why the "0 of 1.27 M" statement is conditioned on a reference
  drawn from the same sweep. The in-scene equivalent is the micro-test.

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`tumbler-garments` has a rare one-frame membrane transient that makes `verify_ok` fail, and it is
  the scene's, not any change's.** One run in 80 — on the **shipped default** — reached
  `area_ratio_max = 74.04` at frame 26, which trips `verify.py`'s `area_ratio_max < 20` assertion and
  returns `verify_ok = False`. The exact path produced 3.699 in the same 80 runs. Both are single
  frames, both recover completely in the next frame, both leave the 99th percentile of the triangle
  area ratio unmoved, and both converge normally with no CCD clamp and no line-search cut. **Any
  future `--verify` gate at n ≥ 20 will see `verify_ok` failures that have nothing to do with the
  change under test**, and the rate on a non-approximating path is about 1 in 40 runs.
- **A rounding-level change moves the tumbler's membrane statistics further, and far more
  significantly, than the approximation under test does.** The shipped default (`Proj = 5`, exact up
  to rounding by s07's eigendecomposition) shifts `ar_mean_mean` and `ar_total_mean` with
  **p = 3e-04** against the full-exact path at n = 20/40, in the safe direction, by +3.4e-4 of bulk
  areal strain. The likely mechanism is that the closed form returns the analytic eigenvalue where
  the iterative solve returns a slightly smaller one, i.e. very slightly stiffer contact. Nobody
  should read a p-value on these statistics as evidence of a physics change without an effect size.
- **V1's 2.15° drum transient did not occur in 80 runs here**; `drum_track_err_deg_max` is the
  0.0395724° start-up transient in the median of all four arms. V1 saw two events above 0.3° in 30
  runs, both on the exact path. The event is rarer than V1's n could establish.
- **`verify.py`'s `verify_ok` conflates a physical threshold with an audit threshold**, as V1 noted
  for `tri_height_min`; the same is true of `area_ratio_max < 20`, which is an anti-NaN guard and not
  a physical statement, yet it is the assertion that failed above.
- **`cube-wall-cloth`'s `cloth_min_y` is sensitive enough to resolve a solver-truncation difference
  at n = 85 and is the only physics observable that scene emits.** Anyone using it as a physics gate
  should know it moves ~1 % under any change to the Hessian and ~0.05 % under none, and that
  `UIPC_CWC_TIGHT` now exists to separate the two.
- **`clang-format` is still not installed on this box** (s04–s08 recorded the same).
- **`nsys` must be invoked as `/workspace/deps/nsight/nsight-systems/2024.6.2/bin/nsys`.** The
  `bin/nsys` wrapper V1's `envaudit.sh` used fails with
  `Nsight Systems #VERSION_RSPLIT# hasn't been installed with CUDA Toolkit #CUDA_MAJOR#.#CUDA_MINOR#`
  and produces no csv. V1's script as committed does not run on this box today; s07's `nsysrun.sh`
  has the working path.

## s09 — the K9 contact stream split, re-measured: it is worth 3.3 % of the wall against the arm nobody would ship and **0.0 % against the one it replaced**, and the reason is 8 of 32 warp slots

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s09/` (`README.md` maps every file onto
the claim it backs; `predictions.txt` / `predictions2.txt`, `critpath.txt`, `overlap_*.txt`,
`scope_*.txt`, `occupancy.txt`, `grid_census_rwb.txt`, `sass_parts.txt`, `res_usage_parts.txt`,
`default_selects.txt`, `pairs_and_grids.txt`, `neighbours_rwb_a2.txt`, `busy_union.txt`, `ab/`,
`nsys/`, `gate_*.txt`, `verify/`).

**Verdict: no behaviour change — the only source edit is the comment above `m_split`.
`UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT=2` stays the default** — it is the best of the three
arms on three of four scenes and a tie on the fourth. What changes is what the round believes about
it, and the belief is worth more than the switch: **part 2 is 92–98 % hidden in every scene**, so
every further PE+PP optimisation is worth ~0 on wall time, and the mechanism that hides it is not
"balance" but **warp slots** — the contact kernels run at 25 % / 38 % of cc 7.5's occupancy and the
split is the only thing in the code that adds a second resident block.

### The premise, checked first — and one half of it is wrong

The brief said "part 1 is the longer stream in all four scenes, so the split is mis-tuned". Part 1
*is* the longer stream, and this step makes the imbalance worse, not better: **the shipped default
moved to `UIPC_CONTACT_RANK1=1` at `53aa9712`** (V2), which is s07's −44 %-per-launch mode on part 2.
Re-measured at this head on the same basis s08 used (full-run totals, part 2 as it runs
concurrently), part 1 is now **2.1–3.7x** part 2 against s08's **1.3–2.3x**; measured *serially*, so
that part 2's number is not inflated by the concurrency, it is **3.3–6.5x**.

But "imbalanced" turned out to be the wrong question, because **an imbalanced split is exactly what
this hardware wants**. The measurement that settles it is not the per-stream totals, it is the
per-launch timeline:

| scene | part 2 hidden | union / max(part1, part2) | what the split saves of the two-launch cost |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| rigid-wrecking-balls | **98.3 %** | 1.006 | 21.7 % |
| cube-wall-cloth | **91.7 %** | 1.035 | 26.8 % |
| stiff-gipc-case2 (60-frame window) | **96.8 %** | 1.012 | 25.7 % |
| tumbler-garments (60-frame window) | **98.1 %** | 1.005 | 16.8 % |

`cuda_gpu_trace`, 456 / 375 / 329 / 470 launch pairs. The two big scenes are full runs
(rwb 120 frames, 71 MB csv; cwc 100 frames, 134 MB); case2 and the tumbler are **60-frame windows**,
scoped because their full-run csv is 250–400 MB, and cross-checked against the full-run kernel sums:
the window's part-1 per-launch time is 883.6 vs 874.4 µs (case2, +1.1 %) and 1678.4 vs 1855.3 µs
(tumbler, −9.5 %, the window ends before the densest phase) — the *ratio* the window is used for is
stable even where the level is not. Both windows contain 329 and 470 launch pairs, so s03's
"a 20-frame cube-wall-cloth window has zero contact-assembly launches" trap was checked, not assumed.

**Part 2 is not on the critical path in any scene.** s07 inferred that from one scene's
kernel-sum/wall ratio; here it is on the timeline, and it is 92–98 % everywhere.

### What the split actually buys, per scene, against both alternatives

`UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` has three arms and the round had only ever discussed two. `=1` — the two
launches back to back on the *default* stream — is the arm that isolates the streams from the split;
`=0` is the **fused pre-K9 kernel**, which is the arm K9 actually replaced.

Contact assembly, µs per assemble call, nsys full runs, n=3 per arm (`critpath.txt`):

| scene | a2 part1 | a2 part2 | **a2 union** | a0 fused | vs a2 | a1 serial | vs a2 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| rigid-wrecking-balls | 1422.1 | 507.8 | **1430.6** | 1423.9 | **−0.47 %** | 1733.7 | +21.18 % |
| cube-wall-cloth | 1955.4 | 773.9 | **2023.8** | 2183.2 | +7.88 % | 2427.6 | +19.95 % |
| stiff-gipc-case2 | 874.4 | 411.4 | **884.9** | 979.3 | +10.66 % | 1101.6 | +24.49 % |
| tumbler-garments | 1855.1 | 506.6 | **1864.4** | 1996.6 | +7.09 % | 2037.6 | +9.29 % |

End to end, one build, four arms interleaved (`abn.py`, rotated order + one discarded warm-up per
arm). The fourth arm is a **null** — `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT=02`, which `std::atoi` reads as 2, so it
executes bit-identical code to a2 and its delta is the scene's own envelope measured in the same
sweep:

| scene | n | null | **a1 serial** | **a0 fused** |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| rigid-wrecking-balls | 20 | mean +1.21 %, ms/Newton **+0.44 %** (p=0.24) | mean +3.63 %, ms/Newton **+3.25 %** (p=3.4e-10), median +4.02 % | mean +0.14 %, ms/Newton **+0.07 %** (p=0.86) |
| cube-wall-cloth | 20 | mean −0.38 %, ms/Newton **−0.15 %** (p=0.65) | mean +3.22 %, ms/Newton **+2.66 %** (p=1.5e-10), ms/PCG +2.83 % **disjoint** | mean +1.76 %, ms/Newton **+1.30 %** (p=3.6e-05) |
| stiff-gipc-case2 | 7 | mean +0.10 %, ms/Newton **−0.13 %** (p=0.62) | mean +0.85 %, ms/Newton **+0.79 %** (p=3.2e-04), ms/PCG +0.94 % **disjoint** | mean +0.34 % (p=0.24), ms/Newton +0.09 %, ms/PCG +0.59 % (p=0.016) — **not cleanly resolved at n=7** |
| tumbler-garments | 3 (nsys) + 6 (`--verify`) | — | wall unreadable: the launch count moves 1464 → 1404 between arms | wall unreadable; scope says +7.1 % of the critical path |

Predicted from the scope alone, before the arms were read (`predictions2.txt`): rwb a1 **+4.26** /
a0 **−0.09**, cwc a1 **+2.62** / a0 **+1.03**, case2 a1 **+0.88** / a0 **+0.38**. Measured +3.25 /
+0.07, +2.66 / +1.30, +0.79 / +0.09-to-+0.59. **Every one inside 1.0 pp, and four of the six inside
0.3 pp** — the critical-path model converts at ~1.0, and it is the only model in this round that
predicted a *null* (rwb a0) as well as an effect.

Counts never move: Newton +0.06 to +0.54 %, PCG −0.28 to +0.64 %, line search −0.07 to +0.57 %,
**all overlapping in all three scenes**, against envelopes of ±1.5 % (rwb), 1.6–2.4 % (cwc) and
±0.15 % (case2). The harness's iteration guard stayed silent everywhere. The wall numbers are
readable.

**So: the split is worth +3.3 % (rwb) and +2.7 % (cwc) of the wall against running the same two
launches serially, and 0.0 % / +1.3 % against the fused kernel it replaced.** On the scene with the
most concurrency in the suite it is worth *nothing at all* over the pre-K9 code, and the round-3
measurement that accepted it (−3 % on the contact kernel of a drum scene) is still about the right
size.

### Why — and it is not balance, it is warp slots

`Reg/Trd` and `BlkX` from the timeline, put through cc 7.5's register file (`occupancy.txt`):

| launch | block | reg/thread | blocks/SM | warp slots of 32 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| contact part 1 (`Proj=8`) | 256 | **255** | 1 | **8 (25 %)** |
| contact part 2 (`Proj=1`) | 384 | **154** | 1 | **12 (38 %)** |

At 255 registers one 256-thread block is all that fits, and at 154 registers a 384-thread block is
still only one block. **Each kernel alone leaves 62–75 % of the SM's warp slots empty**, and a
second *concurrent kernel* is the only way this code has of filling them — a `__launch_bounds__` or a
smaller block cannot, because the register file is the binding constraint in both.

The grid census says the same thing from the other side (`grid_census_rwb.txt`,
`pairs_and_grids.txt`, full rwb trace, 40 SMs): part 1's median grid is **24 blocks of 256**, part
2's 27 blocks of 384; on case2 part 1's median grid is **8 blocks**. Part 1 does not even reach every
SM, and at one block per SM it cannot. Measured directly, over part 1's 622.4 ms of window on rwb,
something else is resident for **28.0 % of it** and that something is entirely part 2.

That is why the imbalance is not a defect: what part 2 is doing is not "the other half of the work",
it is **filling part 1's empty slots**. The exchange rate is the number to keep: hiding part 2 costs
part 1 **−0.09 % (rwb), +1.40 % (cwc), +3.83 % (case2), +4.98 % (tumbler)** of its own time, while
hiding 310 / 499 / 259 / 271 µs of part 2. On rwb that is free; on case2 it is 259 µs bought for 32.

### Is the imbalance structural or incidental? Structural

Part 1's shipped instantiation is **28 272 SASS instructions, 10 563 FP64, 2 434 LDL / 2 378 STL**;
part 2's is **4 848 / 1 014 / 53 / 413** (`sass_parts.txt`). Measured per pair from the serial arm
(threads from the timeline, µs from the full-run kernel sums), part 1 costs **6.5x** part 2 on
cube-wall-cloth, **7.7x** on rigid-wrecking-balls and the tumbler, and **13.4x** on case2. For part 2
to become the critical stream its pair population would have to exceed part 1's by that factor; the
measured ratios (part 2 pairs / part 1 pairs) are **1.69** (rwb), **1.69** (cwc), **4.12** (case2),
**1.18** (tumbler) — case2 is the closest and it is still a factor of **3.3** short. **No scene is
within a factor of three of inverting it**, and the mechanism that fixes the mix is s08's census finding — the trajectory
filter demotes every degenerate PT/EE candidate into the PE/PP lists, so part 1 gets the interior
(dim-4, 12x12) cases and part 2 gets the cheap ones, by construction. A scene could shift the
populations; it cannot shift the per-pair cost ratio.

### Can the work be re-partitioned? Round 4 already answered most of it, and this step answers the rest

**Round 4 §s16 rejected splitting further** (`UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT=3/4` on a temporary `Part` bitmask,
built, measured, reverted): union per Newton iteration 2.360 vs 2.103 (wrecking balls) and 1.523 vs
1.376 (cube-wall) for 4-way against the 2-way default, because **PT alone + EE alone (2.815 ms) is
more expensive than PT+EE fused (1.926 ms)** — the PT threads ride along inside the EE launch for
free, and separating them pays 4 008 B of spill stores where fused pays 2 896 B. The same ledger
records the one contact *fusion* the round-4 brief asked about (`UIPC_DSB_FUSE_GH` was the bending
one; the contact one is s16's 3/4-way entry read in reverse): its data point is that **fusing more
was the direction the numbers pointed in** — which is exactly what `SPLIT=0` tests, and which this
step has now measured properly.

So the partitions of {PT, EE, PE, PP} are exhausted: 4-way and 3-way are losses (round 4), 1-way is a
loss on three scenes and a tie on the fourth (this step), and the 2-way boundary cannot move without
separating PT from EE (round 4: a loss) or moving part-1 work onto the stream that is already free
(which cannot help — the free stream is free *because* part 1 leaves slots, and moving part 1's work
there does not change how many slots part 1 leaves).

The one thing nobody has tried is a **third stream carrying a kernel from another subsystem**, and
that is where the remaining prize is; see the candidates.

### Why the fused arm loses where it loses, and ties where it ties

`SPLIT=0` compiles one kernel for all four branches: **27 816 instructions, 2 627 LDL / 2 919 STL**.
A PE or PP thread in that kernel carries part 1's stack frame — **7x part 2's own local-store
count** — even though it never executes a PT or EE branch. That is K9's original rationale, and it is
still true; what has changed is that it now costs 154 → 255 registers on the bulk (the two rank-1
modes V2 shipped are what made part 2 lean enough for the gap to matter).

It ties on rigid-wrecking-balls for a reason worth writing down. The fused launch there is **95
blocks of 256 on 40 SMs** — nearly three waves — but the pairs are laid out PT, EE, PE, PP, so the
**expensive blocks carry the low block indices and are dispatched first**, and the two cheap waves
behind them cost almost nothing. The fused kernel finishes in **1423.9 µs against part 1 alone at
1423.3** (kernel sums, n=3): on that scene *all* of part 2's work is absorbed for **+0.6 µs**. Where
part 1's own grid already exceeds the machine (cube-wall-cloth: 56 blocks on 40 SMs) the expensive
blocks no longer all fit in the first wave, the cheap threads start competing with them, and the
fused arm pays its spills — +7.9 % of the critical path and +1.30 % of the wall.

### Transfer prediction

The split is **grid-size-against-machine-size plus occupancy**, and §6 splits those two ways:

- The *grid* half **transfers and grows**. Part 1's median grid is 24 blocks (rwb) and 8 (case2) on
  a **40-SM** part. On a 170-SM 5090 the same launch leaves 86–95 % of the SMs with no block at all,
  so the amount of work that can hide under part 1 goes **up**, and `SPLIT=1` should look **worse**
  there than the +3.3 % it costs here. This is the rare §6 row that is a *lower* bound on a bigger GPU.
- The *occupancy* half (8 of 32 warp slots at 255 registers) is the row §6 warns about, and the
  warning cuts in our favour for once: a part with a bigger register file per SM would raise part 1's
  own occupancy and **shrink** the split's value, and no shipping part has one — 64 K 32-bit
  registers per SM and a 255-register-per-thread ceiling have been constant for ten architectures.
  What *has* grown is the warp-slot count (48 on cc 8.9/12.0 against 32 here), so the same launch is
  **8 of 48** slots there instead of 8 of 32 — the under-occupancy the split exploits is worse, not
  better, on a newer part.
- The 1/32-rate FP64 of this box **over-rewards part 1** specifically (10 563 FP64 instructions
  against part 2's 1 014), which makes part 1 longer here than it will be elsewhere and therefore
  makes part 2 *easier* to hide here. On a part with faster FP64 the two streams get closer together
  and the hidden fraction should fall from 92–98 % toward the balanced limit.

Net prediction for a rented run: `SPLIT=1` costs **more** than 3.3 % on a 5090; `SPLIT=0` costs
**more** than 1.3 % (its spill penalty is unchanged and its grid problem is worse); the 92–98 %
hidden fraction is the number most likely to move, and it should move **down**.

### Gates

- **No code change ships**, so the SASS-identity gate is vacuous by construction: the tree is
  `53aa9712` plus documentation. The three arms are the pre-existing `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` switch.
- **Env-switch audit at kernel level** (`default_selects.txt`): with no env var (and with `=2`)
  the launches are `do_assemble_kernel<(bool)0,(int)1,(bool)1,(bool)1,(bool)0,(int)8>` on **stream
  14** and `<(bool)0,(int)2,(bool)0,(bool)0,(bool)1,(int)1>` on **stream 7**; with `=1` both are on
  stream 7; with `=0` there is a single `<(bool)0,(int)0,(bool)1,(bool)1,(bool)1,(int)1>`. The stream
  ids come from the timeline, not from the source.
- **Correctness**: `gate.sh` against `baseline_tests.txt`, run in all three arms.
- **Numerics**: the arms are the *same arithmetic* in a different execution order — no computed
  quantity changes expression. What does change is the order of the atomic accumulations into the
  gradient/Hessian buffers, i.e. FP64 summation order, so this is a **rounding-level** change and it
  is gated as one: Newton/PCG/line-search counts against each scene's null envelope (above) and the
  tumbler `--verify` audit below.
- **Correctness gate**: `gate.sh` identical to `baseline_tests.txt` in **all three arms** —
  `=2` (shipped), `=1` and `=0`: 11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, **14213/95**,
  pytest 48 passed 1 skipped (`gate_a2.txt`, `gate_a1.txt`, `gate_a0.txt`).
- **Non-penetration and containment**: tumbler `--verify`, 180 frames, **n=6 per arm, three arms**,
  one build (`verify_stats.txt`). `verify_ok`, `all_finite`, radial and axial containment **true in
  all 18 runs**; **0** non-converged, **0** `hit_newton_limit`, **0** `hit_line_search_limit` frames
  in every run of every arm; **0 aborts** — V2's ~1-in-40 membrane transient did not appear.
  Every statistic overlaps, and **no observable reaches p < 0.05 in either comparison**: the two
  smallest are `mean_disp_mm_last_quarter` (14.80 → 16.61 a1, p=0.077; → 16.45 a0, p=0.071) and
  `verify_pcg_total` (50 023 → 46 593 / 45 970, p=0.29 / 0.12). The round's watch item is flat:

  | `verify_area_ratio_max` | sorted, 6 runs |
  |---|---|
  | a2 (shipped split) | 1.212 1.232 1.237 1.261 1.475 **1.706** |
  | a1 (serial) | 1.174 1.298 1.311 1.403 1.455 **1.587** |
  | a0 (fused) | 1.174 1.189 1.229 1.259 1.348 **1.379** |

  Both alternative arms sit inside the shipped arm's range, which is what a pure
  accumulation-order change should do.

### What this means for the rest of the round

1. **Part-2 (PE+PP) optimisation is worth ~0 on wall time on every scene in the suite.** Not "less
   than its GPU-time share" — ~0. It is 92–98 % hidden, and s07's −45 % cut to it bought 0.00 % on
   rwb for exactly this reason. Three of round 6's steps have touched part 2. A fourth should be
   justified by something other than wall time (GPU-time for multi-tenant/MPS throughput is a real
   reason; so is register pressure if it ever becomes the fused arm's problem).
2. **Part-1 work converts at ~1.0**, which is what s08 measured without knowing why (−6.8 to −11.0 %
   per launch → −0.98 % of a wall where part 1 is 13 % of GPU time). That is the correct prior for
   the next contact step.
3. **s07's "GPU-kernel-sum / wall = 1.071 on rwb" is the split, and it is a double count.** Measured:
   in arm a2 the kernel-duration sum is **1.060x** the union of the kernel intervals; in a1 and a0 it
   is **1.000x**. The excess is 174.3 ms and the co-residency census attributes 174.3 ms of part 1's
   window to part 2 — the same number. Any future step reading `cuda_gpu_kern_sum` totals on a scene
   with contact assembly is reading a number inflated by ~6 % on rwb, ~4 % on cwc.

### Measurement doctrine after s11 (coordinator) — what this round has retired

Three instruments have now been disqualified for concurrency work, each by a measurement rather than
by argument. Recorded together because a later round will otherwise reach for them:

| instrument | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| `nsys stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` scene totals | **retired for scene totals** (s10) | a duration *sum*: it grows every time the round adds overlap. rwb sum/union went 1.060 (pre-K9) → 1.075 (s09) → **1.130** (s10). s10's winning arm reads **+0.9 %** here and −5.2 % by union. Still valid per kernel |
| GPU **busy union** from `cuda_gpu_trace` | **retired for ranking placements** (s11) | Spearman **rho = +0.10** against wall across five arms — it gave its *best* score to the *worst* arm. Per-arm sd 0.3–1.7 % against arms 4–9 pp apart, so this is not noise |
| repo `WB_TIMER=1` Timer tree | **unusable for any concurrency question** (s11) | `sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ wait_device(); })`, so every scope boundary synchronises the device — destroying the overlap and host run-ahead the arms differ in. Nothing at the call site says so |

**What remains for a step that changes where work is issued: end-to-end wall time, n large, through
`ab.py`.** Per-kernel `kern_sum` numbers stay valid for steps that change a kernel's own cost.

### Consequences for what the round already believes

- **s10's win stands and is confirmed at higher n**: s11 re-measured it at n=30 without a profiler at
  **−4.97 % ms/Newton (p=2.3e-21)**. What does *not* stand is s10's *ranking* of the placements — at
  n=8 it read `=2` as better and its trace called `=2` "does not work"; at n=30 modes 1, 2 and 4 are
  mutually indistinguishable on rwb (Welch p = 0.59–0.95). The placement does not matter on that scene.
- **s09's split conclusion is unaffected** — it compared union *and* wall and both agreed there.
- **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` no longer measures the split alone**, because modes 3/4 of the
  prepass only fire when the K9 split is taken. Pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms.

### And a 1-in-20 accident, caught live

s11's `stiff-gipc-case2` control — a scene that launches **zero** ABD G/H kernels — read **−0.46 %,
p=0.034, "DISJOINT" at n=4**, and reversed to +0.12 %, p=0.58 at n=6. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says a round of
twenty measurements manufactures one of these; this is the round's, on a scene where the effect is
known to be exactly zero.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **Part 1's shadow is 60 % empty and only part 2 uses it; two once-per-Newton kernels from other subsystems are sitting right next to it at 45 % SM fill** | rwb full-trace census (`grid_census_rwb.txt`): `abd_diag_preconditioner_do_assemble_kernel` **169.2 ms, 5.5 %, median grid 18 blocks on 40 SMs**, and `ortho_potential_compute_gradient_hessian_kernel` **152.5 ms, 5.0 %, median grid 18** — 465 launches each, once per Newton, i.e. the same cadence as the contact assembly. During part 1's 622.4 ms window on rwb, **the only thing co-resident is part 2** (174.3 ms of it), and hiding part 2 cost part 1 **−0.09 %**. A second side stream carrying one of these two is the only untried partition left. The prize is bounded by their own duration: up to **−10 % of rwb's kernel time** | rwb first (both kernels are ABD, and rwb is the ABD scene), then case2 as the negative control (case2 has no ABD). Needs a real dependency argument — both feed the global linear system — and the same atomic-order gate this step used |
| **Part 1 is stuck at 8 of 32 warp slots and no launch-bounds tuning can move it** | 255 reg/thread, 256-thread blocks, measured on the timeline. On cc 7.5 a second 256-thread block needs **≤ 128 registers per thread** (65536 / (16 warps x 32)), so the register count must be **halved**, not trimmed: `__launch_bounds__`, a smaller block, or shaving 20 registers all land on the same 8 warps. Round 3's K14 already hit this from the other side (ptxas refused a 128-register cap on the PE+PP part). The only levers that move it are the ones that delete state: s08's basis-free reduction took stack 10 456 → 9 496 B and LDL 329 → 92 without moving registers | static ptxas/`cuobjdump -res-usage` first — if a change does not cross 128, it cannot change occupancy and must be justified as arithmetic |
| **s08's dead-branch candidate, re-costed** | s08 measured 0 of 6.3 M PT and 0 of 19.3 M un-mollified EE pairs taking the dim-2/dim-3 branches, and this step prices what carrying them costs: part 1 is **28 272 instructions with 2 434 LDL / 2 378 STL per thread** against part 2's 4 848 / 53 / 413. Moving the two dead branches behind a `noinline` device function is the cheapest available attack on that frame — and per the row above it only pays as *arithmetic and local traffic*, not as occupancy, unless it reaches 128 registers | cube-wall-cloth + case2 (the two clean instruments); needs a correctness argument for the rare branch |
| **`UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT=0` is the better arm for a multi-process / MPS workload, and nobody has measured that** | the fused kernel does the same work in **one** launch with no events and no second stream, at a kernel-duration sum 26 % below a2's double-counted total, and it ties a2 on rwb end to end (+0.07 %, p=0.86, n=20). Round 3 measured this suite under **MPS x3** and reported aggregate throughput as a headline; under MPS the SM slack the split exploits is being filled by the *other processes* instead, so the split's value should collapse and its cost (an extra launch and two events per Newton) should not | MPS x3 aggregate on rwb + cwc, `SPLIT=2` vs `SPLIT=0`; this is the one place the round's single-process numbers may invert |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`mas-bunny` is a vacuous control for contact assembly** and the round should stop reaching for it
  here. Measured: **21 contact-assembly launches in a whole run** (against 465 Newton iterations) for
  **9.6 ms = 0.15 %** of its GPU kernel time, and `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT=0/1/2` moves its wall by
  −0.19 / −0.05 %. It is a genuine control for CCD (s04 proved that) and a vacuous one for this
  subsystem — the check s04 recorded has to be redone per subsystem, which is exactly what the brief
  says.
- **More than a third of rigid-wrecking-balls' GPU kernel time runs on fewer than 70 % of the SMs.**
  Full-trace census: contact part 1 (24 blocks), part 2 (27), `abd_diag_preconditioner_do_assemble`
  (18), `ortho_potential_compute_gradient_hessian` (18) — **1 121 ms of 3 056 ms = 36.7 %** at a grid
  below 28 blocks on a 40-SM part. The K9 split is the only place in the code that does anything
  about it, and it addresses two of those four kernels.
- **The `cuda_gpu_trace` numbers in this step are single runs and are used only for ratios measured
  *inside* one run** (overlap fraction, union/max, sum/union, co-residency). Cross-arm *levels* from
  trace runs disagree with the n=3 kernel sums by up to 6 % on rwb — the scene's pair population
  moves that much between runs, and the trace instrumentation is not free either. Every cross-arm
  number in this section comes from the kernel sums (n=3) or the end-to-end A/B (n=7–20).
- **`clang-format` is still not installed on this box** (s04, s05, s06, s07 and s08 recorded the same).

## s10 — the ABD body-local gradient/Hessian on a side stream: **−5.07 % of `rigid-wrecking-balls`, disjoint**, and the thing that decides it is *where in the host's issue order the launch goes*, not which stream it goes to

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s10/` (`README.md` maps every file onto the
claim it backs; `predictions.txt`, `shadow_note.txt`, `iter_census_rwb.txt`, `shadow_cwc_c2.txt`,
`overlap_rwb_final.txt`, `hidden_c1.txt`, `scope_union.txt`, `scope_rwb.txt`, `scope_cwc.txt`,
`control_census.txt`, `unspread_rwb.txt`, `verify_prepass.txt`, `sass_identity.txt`,
`ab_summary.txt`, `ab/`, `gate_after.txt`, `gate_off.txt`).

**Switch `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS`: `0` = the old order, `1` = shipped, `2` = the placement that does
not work (kept behind the switch, measured, see below).** Verify mode
`UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS_VERIFY=1`.

### The brief's premise: round 5's R4 is real, and it kills half of s09's candidate outright

s09's candidate was "a second side stream carrying `abd_diag_preconditioner_do_assemble` **or**
`ortho_potential_compute_gradient_hessian` under contact part 1's shadow". Round 5's **R4**
(w3-blackwell) had already rejected a side stream for the first of those: *"the trace shows the fork
and join adjacent with no concurrent work between them"*. **R4 is still true at this head and the
mechanism is in the data flow, not in the schedule.** The round-3 side stream (K13,
`UIPC_ABD_DIAG_SIDE_STREAM`) is still in the tree and still **default on**; on s09's rwb trace the
assemble runs 361 µs on stream 13 and the default stream resumes **2.3 µs** after it ends.

It cannot be fixed by moving the fork, because **`diag_hessian` accumulates contact**:
`abd_linear_subsystem.cu:101` writes it from the kinetic+shape Hessian, but `:153`, `:277` and
`:627` `atomic_add` the **dytopo-effect (contact) blocks** into the same buffer. The ABD diagonal
preconditioner genuinely depends on the contact assembly and can never be hoisted in front of it.
**That half of the candidate is dead, and R4 is the reason, and the reason R4 gives is right.**

The *other* half was never measured. Round 5's w3 assignment said "`ortho_potential` +
`abd_diag_preconditioner` on two streams"; what shipped from that worker was **s20/s24** (launch
geometry) and the R4 row retires only the `abd_diag` side stream. **Nobody ever put the ABD
body-local gradient/Hessian on a side stream.** This step does, and it is the first thing in three
rounds to move `rigid-wrecking-balls` by 5 % without touching a single instruction of arithmetic.

### What is hoistable, proved from the data flow

`_assemble_kinetic_shape` opens with `abd().kinetic->compute_gradient_hessian(...)` and a loop over
`abd().constitutions` (`ortho_potential`, `arap`). Those kernels read **`qs`, `q_prevs`, `q_tildes`,
`masses`, `is_fixed`, `kappas`, `volumes`** — all frozen from the end of the previous Newton
iteration's line search — and write **only** `body_id_to_{shape,kinetic}_{gradient,hessian}`, four
buffers allocated once in `Impl::init()` (no per-iteration resize) that **nothing else in the
backend reads or writes**: the only readers are `assemble_kinetic_shape_k1/k2`, which run after.
The contact phase writes none of them. So the work can be forked before the contact phase and
joined inside `_assemble_kinetic_shape`, and the dependency argument is a grep over the four buffer
names, not an inference from plausible numbers.

### The finding: a side stream is not enough — the *issue order* is the whole effect

The first implementation forked **and launched** the prepass before `compute_dytopo_effect()`. It
bought **nothing**: rwb GPU busy union 3111.2 ms against 3106.6 ms for the old path (+0.15 %). The
timeline says why (`shadow_note.txt`): the work distributor hands SMs out in submission order, so
ortho's **18 blocks of 32 threads at 192 registers** were placed first — and one such warp costs
6 144 of an SM's 65 536 registers, while contact part 1's block needs **65 280** (256 threads x 255
reg). **One 32-thread block denies a whole SM to part 1 for its entire duration.** Measured: part 1
started **567 µs later** than its fork event allowed and finished 2.3 µs after ortho did; ortho
itself ran **83 % slower** (332 → 608 µs/launch) from sharing SMs with part 2.

The fix splits the hook in two, and that split is the step:

* **`do_arm_assemble_prepass()`** runs *before* the contact phase and only records the fork event —
  that is the last point at which the default stream is not yet ordered behind contact part 1's join;
* **`do_launch_assemble_prepass()`** runs *after* `compute_dytopo_effect()` has issued part 1 and
  part 2, and enqueues the kernels on the side stream, which is already waiting on the earlier event.

Both hooks are `virtual`s on `DiagLinearSubsystem` with empty defaults, driven by
`GlobalLinearSystem::arm_assembly_prepass()` / `launch_assembly_prepass()` from
`SimEngine::advance()`. Only `ABDLinearSubsystem` uses them today; FEM is free to adopt them.

### Scope — and the prepass does *not* land in part 1's shadow

`cuda_gpu_trace`, one full 120-frame rwb run per arm (`overlap_rwb_final.txt`, `scope_union.txt`):

| | `=1` (shipped) | `=0` (off) | `=2` (ahead) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| **GPU busy union** | **2934.2 ms** | 3094.7 ms | 3094.8 ms |
| vs off | **−5.19 %** | — | +0.00 % |
| trace span | 3381.7 ms | 3557.0 ms | 3560.6 ms |
| kernel duration sum | 3316.8 ms | 3327.5 ms | 3280.7 ms |
| sum / union | **1.130** | 1.075 | 1.060 |
| ortho G/H µs/launch | 332.2 | 331.7 | **607.7** |
| contact part 1 µs/launch | 1440.1 | 1448.8 | 1400.2 |
| part 1 empty shadow | 411.9 ms | 430.7 ms | 628.1 ms |

**The prepass kernels are concurrent with something for 95.0 % of their 158.8 ms** (`hidden_c1.txt`)
— but **0.0 % of that is inside contact part 1's window**. They land in the shadow of the
*post-contact* chain instead: friction assemble, the dytopo pair key/sort/scatter, and the matrix
converter. The host does not run far enough ahead to get them queued while part 1 is still resident
— in the timeline ortho starts 2 µs after part 1 ends. **Part 1's own 412 ms of empty shadow is
still empty; this step captured a different one.** That is the single most useful thing to carry
forward: see the candidates.

`cuda_gpu_kern_sum`, n=3/arm (`scope_rwb.txt`): ortho G/H **155.6 ms in both arms** — the shipped
placement costs the kernel itself nothing — and contact part 1 660.5 vs 666.4 ms (−0.9 %). **The
whole effect is overlap, not speed.** Note the kern_sum *total* is a duration sum and therefore
double-counts the new overlap (s09's lesson): it reads **+0.9 %** for the winning arm while the union
reads **−5.2 %**. On this step, read the union.

### End to end, one build, four arms interleaved (rotated order, one discarded warm-up per arm)

`rigid-wrecking-balls`, **n=8/arm**, the fourth arm a bit-identical null (`=00`, which `atoi` reads
as 0):

| | off | **after (`=1`)** | before (`=2`) | null |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| mean ms | 27.2421 | **25.5958 −6.04 % DISJOINT** | 25.4345 −6.64 % DISJOINT | 27.2609 +0.07 % |
| ms/Newton | 6.9812 | **6.6270 −5.07 %, p=8.7e-07** | 6.5672 −5.93 %, p=2.2e-07 | 7.0013 +0.29 %, p=0.56 |
| ms/PCG | 0.2567 | **0.2422 −5.64 % DISJOINT** | 0.2405 −6.33 % DISJOINT | 0.2575 +0.31 % |

Counts never move: Newton 461–480 (off) vs 456–473 (after), PCG and line search likewise
overlapping. The null arm is the instrument check and it reads +0.07 % / +0.29 %.

| scene | n | ms/Newton, `=1` vs off | note |
|---|---:|---|---|
| cube-wall-cloth | 8 | **−0.86 %, p=0.155** (mean −3.31 %, p=0.145) | ortho G/H is +11.6 % slower under contention here (scope), which eats most of the overlap |
| tumbler-garments | 5 | −2.33 %, p=0.397 | this scene cannot resolve below ~4 % |
| mas-bunny | 4 | **+0.12 %, p=0.466**, Newton 465 in all 8 runs | **control by construction** |
| stiff-gipc-case2 | 4 | **+0.20 %, p=0.484** | **control by construction** |

**Both controls were verified, not inherited** (`control_census.txt`): a full `mas-bunny` run and a
full `stiff-gipc-case2` run launch **zero** `ortho_potential` / `arap` / ABD-kinetic
gradient-Hessian kernels. They cannot execute the code under test, so what they measure is exactly
the cost of the two extra host hooks per Newton iteration on scenes that get nothing back:
**+0.12 % and +0.20 %, neither resolved.** That is the overhead, reported separately as the brief
asked. (`tumbler-garments` does run the prepass — ortho G/H 324.8 ms = 1.56 % of its kernel time,
one block per launch for the single drum body — so it is a real but small target, not a control.)

### Numerics: bit-identical, proved on device

`UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS_VERIFY=1` snapshots the four prepass outputs after the join, recomputes the
same work in place on the default stream, and counts mismatching 64-bit words on device:
**126 625 200 words compared over 80 frames of two scenes, 0 mismatching** (`verify_prepass.txt`).
This is the strong form, not rounding-level: the kernels are the same instantiations with the same
arguments and the same launch geometry, **every output element has exactly one writer** (there is no
atomic anywhere in the body-local gradient/Hessian), and the join precedes every reader. Because
nothing downstream can see a difference, Newton/PCG counts **cannot** be moved by this change and any
drift between arms is the scene's own non-determinism — which is why `ms_per_newton` is the
legitimate statistic above.

**SASS gate**: the four touched device TUs compiled at this head and at the branch base with the
identical command line and compared **per function**, after normalising the anonymous-namespace
mangling (nvcc encodes the source path and the temp-file PID into every such symbol):
`ortho_potential` 28 functions / 15 240 instructions, `affine_body_bdf1_kinetic` 17 / 888, `arap`
17 / 1 784, `abd_linear_subsystem` 59 / 37 584 — **121 functions, 55 496 instructions, 0 differing,
0 removed**, and one head-only function, the verify kernel that only exists behind
`UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS_VERIFY` (`sass_identity.txt`).

### Gates

- `gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, **identical counts in both arms** (`=1` and `=0`): 11/3,
  1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, 14213/95, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped.
- **Env-switch audit from the timeline**: with `=1` the ortho and kinetic G/H launches are on
  **stream 15** and contact part 1 on 14; with `=0` both are on **stream 7** (the default);
  with `=2` they are on 15 but *ahead* of part 1. The switch is read once through `std::atoi`, and
  `UIPC_GRID_SPREAD_VERIFY=1` forces the prepass **off** — the s20/s24 spread verifiers stage their
  shadow copies on the default stream and would not see a launch that ran on the side stream, so
  leaving both on would have made that verification silently vacuous.

### Transfer prediction

**Grid-size against machine size, and it grows** — with one caveat this step is in a position to
state precisely. The gain is pure overlap: 158.8 ms of latency-bound work (18 blocks of one warp
each, 192 registers, ~332 µs per launch for 576 bodies) moved off a critical path that is otherwise
serial. On a 170-SM part the *same* launch occupies 18 of 170 SMs instead of 18 of 40, so the
machine has **more** room to absorb it, and the chain it hides under (friction, pair sort, converter)
also under-fills a bigger GPU. Predict the *fraction* hidden stays at 90–95 % and the *share of the
wall* it is worth falls only as fast as ortho's own share falls — and ortho is an FP64
eigendecomposition per thread, which this box's 1/32-rate FP64 **over-rewards**, so its share will be
smaller on a 5090 and so will this step's percentage. Best estimate for a rented run: **−2 to −4 %
on rwb rather than −5 %**, same sign, same mechanism.

The `=2` arm is the §6 "occupancy tuning can flip sign" row in its purest form and should be
re-tested rather than trusted: see the limits.

### Limits, stated plainly

- **The two instruments disagree about arm `=2`.** End to end it reads −5.93 % ms/Newton, disjoint,
  *better* than the shipped arm and statistically indistinguishable from it (t=1.77, p≈0.10, n=8).
  Under nsys it reads a **zero** change in the GPU busy union, an 83 %-slower ortho kernel and a
  570 µs delay to contact part 1. One of the two instruments is wrong about that arm. The shipped
  arm is the one where **both** agree, which is why it ships; `=2` stays behind the switch with this
  contradiction recorded rather than resolved.
- Trace numbers are single runs, used only for ratios and unions measured **inside** one run.
  Every cross-arm level comes from the n=3 kern_sum or the n=4–8 end-to-end sweep.
- cube-wall-cloth's −0.86 % is not resolved at n=8 and is not claimed as a win.

### Measurement doctrine after s11 (coordinator) — what this round has retired

Three instruments have now been disqualified for concurrency work, each by a measurement rather than
by argument. Recorded together because a later round will otherwise reach for them:

| instrument | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| `nsys stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` scene totals | **retired for scene totals** (s10) | a duration *sum*: it grows every time the round adds overlap. rwb sum/union went 1.060 (pre-K9) → 1.075 (s09) → **1.130** (s10). s10's winning arm reads **+0.9 %** here and −5.2 % by union. Still valid per kernel |
| GPU **busy union** from `cuda_gpu_trace` | **retired for ranking placements** (s11) | Spearman **rho = +0.10** against wall across five arms — it gave its *best* score to the *worst* arm. Per-arm sd 0.3–1.7 % against arms 4–9 pp apart, so this is not noise |
| repo `WB_TIMER=1` Timer tree | **unusable for any concurrency question** (s11) | `sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ wait_device(); })`, so every scope boundary synchronises the device — destroying the overlap and host run-ahead the arms differ in. Nothing at the call site says so |

**What remains for a step that changes where work is issued: end-to-end wall time, n large, through
`ab.py`.** Per-kernel `kern_sum` numbers stay valid for steps that change a kernel's own cost.

### Consequences for what the round already believes

- **s10's win stands and is confirmed at higher n**: s11 re-measured it at n=30 without a profiler at
  **−4.97 % ms/Newton (p=2.3e-21)**. What does *not* stand is s10's *ranking* of the placements — at
  n=8 it read `=2` as better and its trace called `=2` "does not work"; at n=30 modes 1, 2 and 4 are
  mutually indistinguishable on rwb (Welch p = 0.59–0.95). The placement does not matter on that scene.
- **s09's split conclusion is unaffected** — it compared union *and* wall and both agreed there.
- **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` no longer measures the split alone**, because modes 3/4 of the
  prepass only fire when the K9 split is taken. Pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms.

### And a 1-in-20 accident, caught live

s11's `stiff-gipc-case2` control — a scene that launches **zero** ABD G/H kernels — read **−0.46 %,
p=0.034, "DISJOINT" at n=4**, and reversed to +0.12 %, p=0.58 at n=6. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says a round of
twenty measurements manufactures one of these; this is the round's, on a scene where the effect is
known to be exactly zero.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **Contact part 1's shadow is still 412 ms and still empty — and the reason is the host's issue order, not the GPU** | this step's own trace: the prepass is 95 % overlapped but **0 %** of it is inside part 1's window; ortho starts **2 µs after part 1 ends**, while part 1 runs with 11–12 of 40 SMs free for ~760 µs. The launch simply is not in the queue yet. **The fix is to issue the prepass from inside the contact system, between part 1's launch and part 2's**, instead of after `compute_dytopo_effect()` returns — a one-line move of `launch_assembly_prepass()` into the K9 fork, using the same armed event. Prize: the 412 ms currently empty, against the 158.8 ms the prepass needs, i.e. the *same* work could be hidden twice over, and the next 150 ms of ABD work after it | rwb trace first (does ortho start before part 1 ends?), then the same four-arm end-to-end. Watch part 1's per-launch time: if a 32-thread block steals an SM from part 1 the way arm `=2` showed, this inverts |
| **`UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=2` reads −5.93 % end to end and +0.00 % on the GPU busy union, and one of those is wrong** | n=8, disjoint, p=2.2e-07 end to end; nsys says the union is unchanged (3094.8 vs 3094.7 ms), ortho is 83 % slower and part 1 starts 567 µs late. nsys with `--cuda-graph-trace=node` changes how far the host runs ahead, which is exactly the variable this whole step turns on — so the *instrumented* run may not reproduce the un-instrumented scheduling for this arm | re-measure `=2` with the repo timer tree (`WB_TIMER=1`) instead of nsys, or with CUDA events around the assembly, on rwb and cwc. If `=2` survives without nsys it is the better arm and should replace `=1` |
| **The FEM linear subsystem has the same shape and a bigger kernel** | the two new hooks are on `DiagLinearSubsystem` and FEM inherits them as no-ops. `fem_linear_subsystem.cu` has the identical `_assemble_*` structure, and round 5's s23 put the SNH G/H at **12.5 % of stiff-gipc-case2** and 15 % of mas-bunny. case2's contact part 1 median grid is **8 blocks on 40 SMs** (s09) with 188.9 ms of empty shadow in a 60-frame window — the largest relative shadow in the suite | case2 + mas-bunny; first prove the same data-flow property (which FEM per-element buffers are written by the elastic G/H and read only by the assemble), because the FEM path writes into `info.hessians()` directly in places, which the ABD path does not |
| **`abd_diag_preconditioner`'s 169 ms is genuinely serial and the only way at it is arithmetic** | R4 + this step's data-flow proof: `diag_hessian` takes contact `atomic_add`s at `abd_linear_subsystem.cu:153/:277/:627`, so the inverse cannot start before the contact assembly finishes, and nothing after it is independent of it. It is **5.5 % of rwb kernel time** at grid 18 x 32 threads, 255 registers — one 12x12 FP64 `eigen::inverse` per thread, single-thread latency. Round 4 recorded that there is no bit-identical lane-parallel form | a rounding-level blocked/lane-parallel 12x12 inverse, gated the way s06's lane-parallel *apply* was (device-side bit comparison against the old kernel, which will **not** be bit-identical here — it needs the full numerics treatment) |
| **s09's remaining two: part 1's 255-register wall, and `SPLIT=0` under MPS** | untouched by this step and unchanged by it | as s09 wrote them |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`UIPC_GRID_SPREAD_VERIFY=1` was one step away from becoming vacuous.** The s20/s24 spread
  verifiers stage their shadow copies and their comparison kernels on the **default stream**; a
  launch that moves to a side stream is simply not ordered against them. This step forces the
  prepass off whenever that verifier is on, but the same trap applies to **any** future step that
  moves a spread-launched kernel to another stream, and nothing in `spread_launch.h` says so.
- **`cuda_gpu_kern_sum` totals are now inflated by ~13 % on rwb**, not the ~6 % s09 measured: the
  sum/union ratio moves 1.075 → 1.130 with this step on. Any future step reading scene GPU totals
  from `kern_sum` on an ABD scene is reading a number that grows every time the round adds overlap.
- **`clang-format` is still not installed on this box** (s04–s09 recorded the same).
