## s11 — the prepass placement, all five call sites: the shipped one was worth **nothing** on `cube-wall-cloth`, the brief's candidate is the one that loses, and **the GPU busy union does not rank any of them** (Spearman rho = +0.10)

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s11/` (`README.md` maps every file onto
the claim it backs; `predictions.txt`, `results.txt`, `union_noise.txt`, `overlap_rwb.txt`,
`overlap_reps.txt`, `arm_vs_arm.txt`, `arm_vs_arm_cwc.txt`, `verify_prepass.txt`,
`sass_identity.txt`, `gate_*.txt`, `ab/`).

**Verdict: the default moves from `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` to `=4`.** Same bit-identical work, same
side stream, same fork event — only the host call site moves, from `SimEngine::advance()` after the
whole dytopo-effect phase into the K9 contact fork, immediately after contact part 2 is queued.
rigid-wrecking-balls is unchanged (−4.82 % vs −4.97 %, p = 0.59 at n = 30 — a tie), and
**cube-wall-cloth goes from +0.02 % to −2.13 % (p = 4.0e-05)**.

### Lead: the `=2` instrument disagreement, resolved — and the answer indicts the instrument, not the arm

s10 left `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=2` reading **−5.93 % end to end and +0.00 % on the nsys GPU busy
union**, and asked for a re-measurement without a profiler.

**`=2` survives.** At n = 30 without nsys it reads **−4.81 % ms/Newton (p = 2.3e-21, disjoint)** —
and so does `=1` (−4.97 %) and so does the new `=4` (−4.82 %). All three are statistically
indistinguishable from one another (Welch p = 0.59 / 0.63 / 0.95), against a bit-identical null arm
at −0.70 %. So s10's n = 8 impression that `=2` was *better* than `=1` was noise, and its trace-based
conclusion that `=2` "does not work" was wrong. **On `rigid-wrecking-balls` the placement does not
matter at all** — what matters is only that the work is off the serial path.

The brief asked whether `WB_TIMER=1` could settle it. **It cannot, and that is worth recording**:
`sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ cuda_tool::wait_device(); })`, so every Timer
scope entry and exit synchronises the whole device. The timer tree destroys precisely the host
run-ahead and the kernel overlap that these arms differ in; under it all five arms are forced serial
and would read the same. The independent, non-perturbing instrument is the un-instrumented
end-to-end benchmark at a sample size that can resolve the arms, with a null arm in the same sweep.

Then the larger finding. Four full 120-frame traces per arm, union **normalised per Newton
iteration** (s10 compared raw totals across runs whose Newton counts differ by up to 5 %):

| arm | union/it µs | sd | union Δ% | span Δ% | **end-to-end Δ%** |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 off | 6550.6 | 71.7 | +0.00 | +0.00 | **+0.00** |
| 1 after the dytopo phase (s10) | 6525.6 | 16.7 | −0.38 | −0.34 | **−4.97** |
| 2 before the contact phase | 6824.8 | 114.6 | **+4.19** | +3.62 | **−4.81** |
| 3 between contact part 1 and part 2 | 6267.6 | 61.7 | **−4.32** | −3.93 | **−1.97** |
| 4 after contact part 2 | 6303.8 | 73.7 | −3.77 | −3.27 | **−4.82** |

**Spearman rho between the union column and the wall column is +0.10.** The union reads −0.4 % for
the arm worth −5.0 % of the wall, +4.2 % for an arm worth −4.8 %, and its *best* score for the arm
worth only −2.0 %. Per-arm sd is 0.3–1.7 % while the arms sit 4–9 pp apart, so this is not sampling
noise, and at 4 runs per arm it is not the single-run accident s10's one trace per arm could not
exclude. Two defects stack: the union is a **level** compared across runs, and even normalised it
measures GPU-busy time, which is not what these arms change — every arm hides the same ~155 ms of
prepass; what differs is which kernel gets starved of SMs and whether that kernel is on the host's
critical chain, and a union cannot see a host stall at all.

`cuda_gpu_kern_sum` was already retired for scene totals (a duration sum: sum/union is 1.08 off,
1.13 at mode 1, 1.22 at mode 4). **This retires the busy union too, for any step that changes where
work is issued.** The trace remains excellent for *mechanism* — which kernel ran when — and that is
how s11 used it.

### Why mode 1 misses contact part 1's shadow: it is a host stall, and it is in the code

s10 measured that 0 % of the prepass runs inside part 1's window and could not say why. The reason is
`GlobalDyTopoEffectManager::Impl::_distribute` (`global_dytopo_effect_manager.cu:404`), which does a
**blocking** D2H (`VarView<IndexT>{selected_hessian_offsets.data()+N}.copy_to(&h_total_count)`) right
after the scan. The host therefore stalls inside `compute_dytopo_effect()` until the contact kernels
have drained, and only reaches `SimEngine`'s `launch_assembly_prepass()` **after part 1 has already
ended** — s10's "ortho starts 2 µs after part 1 ends" is that stall, not a GPU scheduling effect. The
fix is to call the hook from inside the contact assembly, before the stall.

Both new slots do land where they were aimed (`overlap_rwb.txt`):

| | 0 off | 1 after | 2 before | **3 part1‖part2** | **4 after part2** |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| prepass inside part 1's window | 0.0 % | 0.0 % | 0.0 % | **99.0 %** | **91.5 %** |
| part 1's empty shadow | 430.5 ms | 443.9 ms | 632.4 ms | 282.4 ms | **100.9 ms** |
| ortho G/H µs per launch | 328.1 | 333.1 | 623.2 | 417.3 | 815.2 |
| contact part 2 inside part 1 | 98.5 % | 93.8 % | 4.3 % | 99.9 % | 95.5 % |

**The mechanism prediction was right and the wall-time prediction was wrong in one direction.**
Predicted (`predictions.txt`, P1) that the prepass would start before part 1 ends: it does, at 99 %.
Predicted (P2) that the gain would *replace* s10's rather than stack on it, because s10 had already
removed ~100 % of the prepass from the critical path: correct on rigid-wrecking-balls — filling the
~430 ms empty shadow buys **zero** extra there. What P2 did not anticipate is that on
**cube-wall-cloth** the shadow s10's placement lands in is not free, so moving the work into part 1's
shadow is worth a further 2.1 %.

### The ranking inverts between the two ABD scenes, and that is why mode 4 ships

ms per Newton iteration, one build, interleaved arms, null arm in the same sweep:

| arm | rwb (n=30) | cwc (n=10) | mas-bunny (n=6) | case2 (n=4/6) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 off | +0.00 % | +0.00 % | +0.00 % | +0.00 % |
| 1 after the dytopo phase (was the default) | **−4.97 %** | **+0.02 %** (p=0.96) | +0.03 % | −0.07 % |
| 2 before the contact phase | −4.81 % | −1.67 % | — | — |
| 3 between part 1 and part 2 | **−1.97 %** (n=20) | **−3.11 %** | — | — |
| 4 after part 2 — **the new default** | −4.82 % | **−2.13 %** (p=4.0e-05) | +0.09 % | +0.12 % |
| null (bit-identical arm) | −0.70 % | −0.14 % | 0.00 % | −0.02 % |

Mode 1 wins rigid-wrecking-balls and is worth **nothing** on cube-wall-cloth. Mode 3 is the *best*
arm on cube-wall-cloth and the *worst* of the four on rigid-wrecking-balls. **Mode 4 is the only arm
at the top of both**, which is the whole of the shipping argument: it ties the incumbent where the
incumbent is strong and beats it by 2.1 % where the incumbent is flat.

**Mode 3's loss is the s10 submission-order pathology, one kernel further down the list.** Slot 3 is
issued *before* contact part 2 (167 blocks at 154 registers), the kernel that s09 measured as
91.7–98.3 % hidden inside part 1. Putting 18 small blocks ahead of it in the distributor's queue
takes SMs it was relying on, and it comes out of the shadow. Slot 4 protects part 2 exactly the way
s10's mode 1 protects part 1 — by being queued last. That was recorded in advance as prediction P2c,
*"if the two slots differ, slot 4 ≥ slot 3"*, and it is the only place in this step where the
submission-order model made a falsifiable call and won.

### Controls, and one that nearly went the wrong way

`mas-bunny` and `stiff-gipc-case2` launch **zero** ABD G/H kernels (s10's census), so they measure
only the cost of the hooks: **+0.09 %** and **+0.12 %**, neither resolved. mas-bunny reported
Newton 465 in all 18 runs.

At n = 4, `stiff-gipc-case2` reported mode 4 at **−0.46 %, p = 0.034, "DISJOINT"** — a control scene
that cannot execute the code reading a *faster* time. Re-measured at n = 6 with a null arm in the
same sweep it reads **+0.12 % (p = 0.58)** with the null at −0.02 %. This is PERF_METHOD §2.4's
1-in-20 accident, caught by the rule that produced it: **a sub-1 % effect at n = 4 is not a
measurement**, and a control that appears to win is the cheapest sign that you are looking at one.

`tumbler-garments` at n = 5: off 13.716 ms/Newton with sd 1.09 (8 % of the mean); mode 1 −5.10 %
(p = 0.29), mode 4 −2.00 % (p = 0.69). **Unresolved, and no claim is made** — the scene's scatter in
this sweep was wider than the effect.

### Numerics: bit-identical, proved on device and at the compiler

`UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS_VERIFY=1` over modes 3, 4 and the shipped default, two scenes, 40 frames each:
**379 696 512 output words compared, 0 mismatching** (`verify_prepass.txt`). The side stream's only
ordering constraint is the fork event, and that event is recorded at the *same* point in modes 1, 2,
3 and 4 — only the host's enqueue point moves, so the GPU-visible dependency graph is identical
across all of them. The data flow was re-derived at this head rather than inherited: the four output
buffers are written only by `_compute_kinetic_shape` and read only by `assemble_kinetic_shape_k1/k2`
(a grep over the four names finds no other mention anywhere in the backend), there is no atomic in
the body-local gradient/Hessian, and `_join_prepass()` precedes every reader and the verifier.

**SASS gate**: the three touched `.cu` TUs compiled at this head and at the branch base with the
identical command line, per function, after normalising the anonymous-namespace mangling —
`abd_linear_subsystem` 60 functions / 38 016 instructions **identical**, `advance_ipc` 14 / 544
**identical**, `ipc_simplex_normal_contact` 74 functions with **four differing instructions, all of
them `__LINE__` immediates** of the same `UIPC_KERNEL_ASSERT`, shifted by exactly the two `#include`
lines this step adds (0x41/0x81/0xc4/0xf6 → 0x43/0x83/0xc6/0xf8, i.e. 65/129/196/246 → 67/131/198/248).
This step compiles no new device code and alters none.

### Gates

- `gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, **identical counts in all three arms** (the new default, `=0`
  and `=3`): 11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, 14213/95, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped.
- **Env-switch audit**, `rwb_null.txt`, n = 20, three arms that must all be the same code:
  `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=4` (reference), the switch left **unset** (+0.21 %, p = 0.63) and `=04`
  (+0.08 %, p = 0.86). None resolved — the default really is 4, and `atoi` really is what parses it. `=0` restores the pre-s10 order (+5.1 % on rwb, disjoint) and
  `UIPC_GRID_SPREAD_VERIFY=1` still forces the whole prepass off.
- The end-to-end table above was re-measured on the **shipped** binary after the default flip and the
  shared-header refactor (`rwb_final.txt`, `cwc_final.txt`, n = 12): mode 4 −6.23 %/−1.81 %, mode 1
  −6.09 %/−0.31 %, same picture.

### Transfer prediction

**Grid-size against machine size, and it shrinks.** This is pure overlap: 155 ms of latency-bound
work (18 blocks of one warp, 192 registers) placed into SMs that contact part 1 does not reach. On
this 40-SM part, part 1's grid occupies 24 SMs and leaves 16; on a 170-SM part it leaves ~146, so
*every* placement becomes free and the four arms should converge on each other — the reason mode 1
loses on cube-wall-cloth is contention for a shadow that is too small, and a bigger GPU does not have
that problem. Predict on a 5090: modes 1, 2, 3 and 4 all within ~1 % of each other, the whole
prepass still ~95 % hidden, and the *size* of the win smaller than here because ortho is an FP64
eigendecomposition per thread which this box's 1/32-rate FP64 over-rewards. Best estimate: **−2 to
−4 % on rwb and −1 to −2 % on cwc, with mode 4's margin over mode 1 shrinking toward zero.** The one
thing that should *not* shrink is mode 3's penalty, because it is a submission-order effect on a grid
(part 2's 167 blocks) that still exceeds any machine.

### Limits, stated plainly

- The choice between modes 1, 2 and 4 rests on **cube-wall-cloth alone**; rigid-wrecking-balls cannot
  tell them apart at n = 30 and the two control scenes cannot execute the code. A third ABD-bearing
  scene would be worth more than more samples on these two.
- `tumbler-garments` is unresolved in both directions.
- Every trace number is a within-run ratio or a per-iteration normalisation; no cross-arm *level* is
  taken from nsys, for the reason this step spent most of its time establishing.
- Modes 3 and 4 fire only when the K9 split is taken. A gradient-only assemble, an empty PT/EE or
  PE/PP list, or `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT != 2` all fall back to `SimEngine`'s backstop hook, i.e. to
  mode-1 behaviour. **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` at this default therefore no longer measures the
  split alone** — pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms of any such sweep. This is written on the
  switch in `abd_gh_prepass_mode.h`.

### Measurement doctrine after s11 (coordinator) — what this round has retired

Three instruments have now been disqualified for concurrency work, each by a measurement rather than
by argument. Recorded together because a later round will otherwise reach for them:

| instrument | status | why |
|---|---|---|
| `nsys stats --report cuda_gpu_kern_sum` scene totals | **retired for scene totals** (s10) | a duration *sum*: it grows every time the round adds overlap. rwb sum/union went 1.060 (pre-K9) → 1.075 (s09) → **1.130** (s10). s10's winning arm reads **+0.9 %** here and −5.2 % by union. Still valid per kernel |
| GPU **busy union** from `cuda_gpu_trace` | **retired for ranking placements** (s11) | Spearman **rho = +0.10** against wall across five arms — it gave its *best* score to the *worst* arm. Per-arm sd 0.3–1.7 % against arms 4–9 pp apart, so this is not noise |
| repo `WB_TIMER=1` Timer tree | **unusable for any concurrency question** (s11) | `sim_engine.cu:64` installs `Timer::set_sync_func([]{ wait_device(); })`, so every scope boundary synchronises the device — destroying the overlap and host run-ahead the arms differ in. Nothing at the call site says so |

**What remains for a step that changes where work is issued: end-to-end wall time, n large, through
`ab.py`.** Per-kernel `kern_sum` numbers stay valid for steps that change a kernel's own cost.

### Consequences for what the round already believes

- **s10's win stands and is confirmed at higher n**: s11 re-measured it at n=30 without a profiler at
  **−4.97 % ms/Newton (p=2.3e-21)**. What does *not* stand is s10's *ranking* of the placements — at
  n=8 it read `=2` as better and its trace called `=2` "does not work"; at n=30 modes 1, 2 and 4 are
  mutually indistinguishable on rwb (Welch p = 0.59–0.95). The placement does not matter on that scene.
- **s09's split conclusion is unaffected** — it compared union *and* wall and both agreed there.
- **An A/B of `UIPC_CONTACT_SPLIT` no longer measures the split alone**, because modes 3/4 of the
  prepass only fire when the K9 split is taken. Pin `UIPC_ABD_GH_PREPASS=1` in both arms.

### And a 1-in-20 accident, caught live

s11's `stiff-gipc-case2` control — a scene that launches **zero** ABD G/H kernels — read **−0.46 %,
p=0.034, "DISJOINT" at n=4**, and reversed to +0.12 %, p=0.58 at n=6. PERF_METHOD §2.4 says a round of
twenty measurements manufactures one of these; this is the round's, on a scene where the effect is
known to be exactly zero.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **Contact part 1's shadow is now 101 ms instead of 430, and the FEM subsystem has the same shape and a bigger kernel** | s11 filled 330 ms of part 1's empty shadow on rwb with the only work that was hoistable. The two hooks are on `DiagLinearSubsystem` and FEM inherits them as no-ops; `fem_linear_subsystem.cu` has the identical `_assemble_*` structure and round 5's s23 put the SNH G/H at **12.5 % of stiff-gipc-case2** and 15 % of mas-bunny. case2's part 1 median grid is **8 blocks on 40 SMs** (s09) — the largest relative shadow in the suite, and s11 leaves it entirely untouched (case2 and mas-bunny read +0.12 % / +0.09 %, i.e. the hooks fire and do nothing). **Use slot 4**, not slot 3 | case2 + mas-bunny (both deterministic enough to resolve <1 %); first prove the data-flow property the ABD path has — which FEM per-element buffers are written by the elastic G/H and read only by the assemble — because the FEM path writes into `info.hessians()` directly in places |
| **The blocking D2H in `_distribute` is itself the thing to remove** | `global_dytopo_effect_manager.cu:404` stalls the host until the contact kernels drain, once per Newton iteration. That stall is why mode 1 could not reach part 1's shadow at all, and it bounds how far ahead the host can ever run inside the dytopo phase. A device-side `loose_resize_entries` (over-allocate to a high-water mark and skip the readback, or defer the readback one iteration) would remove it | measure the stall first: CUDA events around `_distribute`, or the gap between part 1's end and the next kernel on the default stream in an existing s11 trace. Prize is bounded by that gap, **not** by part 1's duration |
| **`cube-wall-cloth`'s ortho contention is the real remaining ABD target, and mode 3 measured it** | mode 3 is −3.11 % on cwc against mode 4's −2.13 % (p = 0.038) while being 2.9 pp *worse* on rwb. The two scenes want opposite placements, which means the right answer is not one more slot but a **cheaper ortho**: it is an FP64 12×12 eigendecomposition per thread at 192 registers, 18 blocks of 32 threads, and its per-launch cost swings 328 → 815 µs purely on who else is resident | a lane-parallel or blocked ortho G/H, gated the way s06's lane-parallel apply was. Note it will **not** be bit-identical; it needs the full numerics treatment |
| **`abd_diag_preconditioner`'s 170 ms is still genuinely serial** | unchanged by s11 and confirmed again in its traces: 363–366 µs per launch in every arm, on stream 13, 5.5 % of rwb kernel time. R4's data-flow rejection stands (`diag_hessian` takes contact `atomic_add`s at `abd_linear_subsystem.cu:153/:277/:627`) | as s10 wrote it — a rounding-level lane-parallel 12×12 inverse |
| **s09's remaining two: part 1's 255-register wall, and `SPLIT=0` under MPS** | untouched by this step | as s09 wrote them |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`Timer::set_sync_func` makes the repo's own timer tree unusable for any concurrency question.**
  `sim_engine.cu:64` installs a full `wait_device()` at every scope boundary. That is the right
  choice for attributing serial work, but it means `WB_TIMER=1` cannot measure anything that depends
  on overlap or on host run-ahead, and nothing at the call site says so. Three round-6 steps have now
  turned on a concurrency question; this should be written on the Timer, not rediscovered.
- **The `nsys` GPU busy union joins `kern_sum` on the list of instruments that must not be compared
  across arms.** See the table above. If a future step needs a *scope* number for a concurrency
  change, the honest one is CUDA events around the region on the default stream, read back once per
  frame — not any aggregate the profiler computes.
- **`clang-format` is still not installed on this box** (s04–s10 recorded the same).

---

## s12 — the same prepass for FEM: **refuted, and the reason is the destination, not the dependency**. The FEM elastic Hessian is written straight into the *global* triplet matrix, which does not exist until the contact count does; the staging copy that would fix it costs **283 MB per Newton iteration**, measured at **+3.24 % of `stiff-gipc-case2`**, against a measured overlap ceiling of **2.2 %**

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s12/` (`README.md` maps every file onto
the claim it backs; `predictions.txt`, `kernsum_c2_head.txt`, `underfill_c2.txt`,
`timeline_c2_head.txt`, `gap_c2.txt`, `results_c2.txt`, `results_mb.txt`, `null_c2.txt`,
`sass_identity.txt`, `gate_*.txt`, `ab/`).

**Verdict: no behaviour change ships. `UIPC_FEM_GH_COST_PROBE` is a diagnostic, default off**, and
this step closes s11's candidate 1. What ships is the measurement and a 30-line comment on
`_assemble_reporters` recording why the FEM path cannot take the ABD path's shape.

### The premise, checked first — and it is half right, which is the half that does not matter

s11's candidate said the FEM elastic G/H "is body-local in the same sense the ABD ortho potential is
— it depends on positions, not on the contact set".

**The inputs half is true.** The elastic G/H kernels read `fem().xs`, `x_bars`, `Dm3x3_invs`,
`rest_volumes`, the tet/tri index buffers and the per-element material parameters
(`fem_3d_constitution.cu:32-55`, `stable_neo_hookean_3d.cu:549-562`); the contact phase writes none
of them. Forking before the contact phase is dependency-legal.

**The outputs half is false, and it is the half the ABD prepass rests on.** s10's argument was never
about inputs: it was that the four ABD outputs are *private buffers allocated once in `Impl::init()`
that nothing else in the backend reads or writes*. The FEM reporters are not shaped that way. The
gradient half is private (`FEMLinearSubsystem::Impl::reporter_gradients`); **the Hessian half is
written straight into `info.hessians()`**, a subview of the **global** `triplet_A`
(`fem_linear_subsystem.cu:422`, `finite_element_elastics.cu:79`,
`stable_neo_hookean_3d.cu:264/:382`). Three independent properties of that destination make it
unavailable before the contact phase has finished:

| # | property | code |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | its **size** is not known — the FEM extent adds `dytopo_effect_receiver->hessians().triplet_count()`, and `triplet_A` is only resized (and, on growth, **reallocated** by `reserve_triplets_discard`) once that count exists | `fem_linear_subsystem.cu:289`; `global_linear_system.cu:856-858` |
| 2 | its **offset** is not known — the region starts at `subsystem_triplet_offsets[triplet_i] + kinetic_count`, and that base includes every preceding diagonal subsystem's triplet count, contact included, on any scene with more than one | `global_linear_system.cu:920-925` |
| 3 | it is **overwritten afterwards anyway** — `_assemble_linear_system` opens with `triplet_A.row_indices().fill(-1)` / `col_indices().fill(-1)` over the whole matrix, on the default stream, after the contact phase | `global_linear_system.cu:881-882` |

Property 1 is the dangerous one and it is exactly the failure mode the brief warned about: a prepass
that wrote into `triplet_A` would be correct for every iteration in which the contact triplet count
did not force a reallocation, and would write into freed memory on the iteration that did. On case2
the contact triplet count moves **17x within one run** (`assemble_dytopo_effect_k2`'s grid ranges
36–601 blocks of 1024, `timeline_c2_head.txt`), so growth spikes are not rare.

**So the only surviving design is ABD's own shape: stage the elastic Hessian in a private triplet
buffer during the prepass and copy it into `info.hessians()` once that exists.** ABD needs no such
copy because `assemble_kinetic_shape_k1/k2` — a scatter from private buffers into the global system
— was already there before s10. FEM has no equivalent; the reporters write through.

### What the copy costs, measured

`UIPC_FEM_GH_COST_PROBE=1` moves exactly the bytes that copy would move — row indices (4 B), column
indices (4 B) and the 3x3 block (72 B) per triplet, read once and written once — as three
contiguous `cudaMemcpyAsync` D2D at the point in `_assemble_reporters` where the staged copy would
land. The destination is scratch nothing reads, so **no computed quantity changes**; this is the
*cheapest possible* form of that copy (a pure contiguous memcpy, not the tree's own triplet-copy
kernel).

On `stiff-gipc-case2` the reporter Hessian region is **1 769 452 triplets = 283.1 MB moved per
Newton iteration**.

### The ceiling, measured before the cost — and it is smaller than the copy

The prize for *any* overlap step on case2 is bounded by how much of the machine is idle. Measured
from the full per-launch timeline of a 60-frame window (332 Newton iterations), each launch scored
against how many of its blocks cc 7.5 can hold resident on 40 SMs at its own register count
(`underfill_c2.txt`):

| | |
|---|---:|
| kernel duration sum in the window | 8170.9 ms |
| occupancy-weighted (first-wave fill) | 7719.1 ms |
| **mean fill** | **94.5 %** |
| **total idle-machine-equivalent** | **451.8 ms = 5.5 % of kernel time** |
| of which contact assembly (`do_assemble_kernel`, both parts) | **202 ms** |

**`stiff-gipc-case2` is a saturated scene.** Per Newton iteration that is **1.36 ms** of idle machine
in total and **0.61 ms** in the contact-assembly window, against a 28.1 ms Newton iteration — so the
absolute ceiling on a prepass is **2.2 %** of the wall (4.8 % if every scattered under-filled launch
in the scene could be filled, which no single prepass can do).

**Why the ABD mechanism cannot repeat here, in one line of geometry.** `ortho_potential` is 18 blocks
of 32 threads and 332 µs against contact part 1's 1440 µs — a sliver that fits entirely in the
shadow. `StableNeoHookean3D` G/H on case2 is **1249 blocks of 128 threads at 168 registers** — 3
blocks per SM on cc 7.5, so 120 blocks fill the machine and the launch is **10.4 waves** — and
**2829 µs against part 1's 880 µs**. It is a saturating throughput kernel, not a sliver. At most
880/2829 = **31 %** of it could ever be concurrent with part 1, and the two cannot even share an SM:
128 x 168 = 21 504 registers plus part 1's 65 280 exceeds the 65 536-register file. The timeline
confirms both halves: SNH runs with **zero** co-resident kernels for its whole 939 ms in the window,
and part 1's only company is part 2, for 35.3 % of its window (`timeline_c2_head.txt`).

### End to end: the copy alone costs more than the whole prize

`stiff-gipc-case2`, one build, ABBA order, one discarded warm-up per arm.

### Transfer prediction — and this one is a race, not a monotone claim

The refutation has two halves that move in **opposite** directions on a bigger GPU, so it should be
re-checked there rather than assumed:

- **The cost is memory bandwidth.** 283 MB per Newton iteration is 283 MB everywhere; at this box's
  ~380 GB/s achievable D2D it is ~0.74 ms, at a 5090's ~1.5 TB/s it would be ~0.19 ms. The cost
  **shrinks ~4x**.
- **The prize is idle SMs during the contact assembly**, and per s09/s11 that **grows** with the
  machine: contact part 1's median grid on case2 is **8 blocks**, which leaves 80 % of a 40-SM part
  idle and ~95 % of a 170-SM one. But the prize is also bounded by the contact assembly's own
  *duration*, and contact part 1 is 10 563 FP64 instructions per thread on a part whose FP64 rate is
  1/64 (GB202) against this box's 1/32 — so its duration does not fall the way the bandwidth rises.

Best estimate for a rented 5090: the copy lands at **+0.6 to +1.0 %** of case2 and the ceiling at
**+2 to +3 %**, i.e. the margin narrows from 1.5x against to roughly break-even, and a *carefully*
implemented FEM prepass might be worth ~1 % there. It is not worth building on this box, and the
right place to reopen it is a rented run — not more samples here.

Category (PERF_METHOD §6): **grid-size against machine-size** for the prize (transfers and grows)
against **bandwidth-bound copy** for the cost (transfers and shrinks). This box over-rewards
neither, which is unusual for round 6 and is why the prediction is a range rather than a sign.

### What this step means for the rest of the round

1. **s11's candidate 1 is closed.** The FEM elastic G/H prepass is not "a smaller version of the ABD
   win"; it is a different problem, because the ABD prepass's enabling property was a **private
   output buffer** and FEM does not have one. The generalisable rule: *before proposing a side
   stream for a piece of work, grep for who owns the buffer it writes, not just for who writes the
   buffers it reads.* s10 got this right by accident of the subsystem it picked.
2. **`stiff-gipc-case2` is not "the largest relative shadow in the suite" in the sense the brief
   meant.** s09's "8 blocks on 40 SMs" is true of contact part 1's *grid*, but part 1 is only
   0.88 ms of a 28.1 ms Newton iteration on that scene, and the rest of the scene runs at 94.5 %
   occupancy fill. The relative shadow is large; the **absolute** shadow is 0.61 ms per Newton and
   that is what a placement step can win. A grid census ranks shadows by shape; only a
   duration-weighted occupancy census ranks them by size.
3. **The `DiagLinearSubsystem` prepass hooks stay as they are.** FEM continues to inherit them as
   no-ops, and s10/s11's measurements of that overhead (+0.09 to +0.20 % on FEM-only scenes,
   unresolved) remain the whole cost of that.

### Limits, stated plainly

- **The probe is the cost floor, not the cost.** It is three contiguous `cudaMemcpyAsync` D2D. A real
  staging copy would also have to *write* the staged region with the submatrix offset applied, which
  a memcpy gets for free only because the staging buffer can carry the same offset — that holds
  today (`TripletMatrixViewT::write` adds `m_submatrix_offset`, a host-side `int2`, at
  `views.h:85-86`) but it is one more invariant a real implementation would have to keep. The tree's
  own triplet-copy kernel (`FEMLinearSubsystem_assemble_dytopo_effect_k2`) regresses at **2.31 ns per
  triplet** over 332 launches, which for 1.77 M triplets would be **4.1 ms per Newton = +14.6 %** —
  6x the memcpy form. The truth for a real implementation is somewhere between the two, and both ends
  exceed the 2.2 % ceiling.
- **The overlap gain was never measured, only bounded.** No prepass was built, so the 2.2 % is the
  occupancy-census ceiling, not a measurement of a working arm. The refutation rests on
  cost > ceiling, which is a weaker claim than cost > measured gain — but it is the claim that can be
  made without building something the arithmetic says cannot win.
- **One scene.** `stiff-gipc-case2` was the target because s09/s11 nominated it and because it has no
  ABD (so the shipped ABD prepass cannot confound). `mas-bunny` is the second FEM instrument and its
  numbers are below; `cube-wall-cloth` and the tumbler have FEM but also ABD and were not swept.
- **Trace numbers are single runs**, used only for within-run ratios (fill, co-residency, gaps) and
  per-launch geometry. Every cross-arm number comes from the end-to-end sweeps.
| `stiff-gipc-case2`, n=8/arm | off | probe (the staging copy) | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| mean ms/frame | 165.7861 [164.43, 167.06] | 170.4020 [169.68, 171.13] | **+2.78 %, DISJOINT, p=2.7e-08** |
| median ms/frame | 167.8643 | 172.7560 | +2.91 %, DISJOINT |
| **ms per Newton iteration** | 24.9414 | 25.6340 | **+2.78 %, DISJOINT, p=1.8e-10** |
| ms per PCG iteration | 0.6447 | 0.6657 | +3.26 %, DISJOINT, p=4.7e-09 |
| Newton | 1661.75 | 1661.88 | +0.01 %, overlapping |
| PCG | 64 291.9 | 63 995.6 | −0.46 %, overlapping |
| line search | 1787.25 | 1787.75 | +0.03 %, overlapping |

**Predicted +2 to +4 % before the sweep ran (`predictions.txt`, P3); measured +2.78 %.** The
iteration guard stayed silent — no count moves more than 0.46 % against a 2.78 % wall change — so the
wall number is readable, and it is readable for a stronger reason than usual: the probe writes only
into scratch nothing reads, so no computed quantity can differ and the count drift is the scene's own
non-determinism.

**The copy alone is 1.26x the entire ceiling.** 2.78 % against 2.2 %, and the 2.2 % assumes a perfect
prepass that fills every idle SM during the contact assembly at zero cost to the contact kernels —
which s09 measured is not free even for a 22-block kernel (+3.83 % to part 1 on this scene).
**s11's candidate 1 is closed.**

**A second FEM scene, and a determinism check in the same sweep.** `mas-bunny` is FEM-only,
`StableNeoHookean` only, and deterministic — Newton 465 every run:

| `mas-bunny`, n=10/arm | off | probe | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| mean ms/frame | 66.5799 | 68.2577 | **+2.52 %, DISJOINT, p=7.0e-19** |
| ms per Newton iteration | 14.3183 | 14.6791 | **+2.52 %, DISJOINT, p=7.0e-19** |
| ms per PCG iteration | 0.1891 | 0.1939 | +2.53 %, DISJOINT |
| Newton | **465 in all 20 runs** | **465 in all 20 runs** | +0.00 % |
| line search | **465 in all 20 runs** | **465 in all 20 runs** | +0.00 % |
| PCG | 35 208.0 | 35 203.5 | −0.01 %, overlapping |

Two scenes, two different meshes, the same **+2.5 to +2.8 %** — the cost is bandwidth and it scales
with the triplet count, exactly as predicted. And the identical Newton and line-search counts in
every one of the twenty runs are the end-to-end proof that the probe changes no computed quantity.

### Gates

- **`gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, identical counts in both arms** (probe off and
  `UIPC_FEM_GH_COST_PROBE=1`): 11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, **14213/95**, pytest
  48 passed 1 skipped (`gate_off.txt`, `gate_probe.txt`).
- **Numerics: nothing to prove, and proved anyway.** The probe's three `cudaMemcpyAsync` write into
  `probe_rows` / `probe_cols` / `probe_vals`, buffers no kernel and no other code path reads. End to
  end, `mas-bunny` reports **Newton 465 and line search 465 in all twenty runs** in both arms, with
  PCG within 0.01 %.
- **SASS**: the one touched TU compiled at this head and at the branch base 48f1c6e3 with the
  identical `build-perf` command line and diffed per function after normalising the
  anonymous-namespace mangling. The diff is **purely additive — two hunks, 0 deleted and 0 changed
  lines**: base 29 device functions / 6496 SASS instructions, head 31 / 6640. The two head-only
  functions are `cuda_tool::details::buffer_fill_kernel<Matrix3x3>` and
  `cuda_tool::buffer_fill_verify_kernel<Matrix3x3>`, the `DeviceBuffer<Matrix3x3>::resize`
  instantiation only the probe's scratch allocation can reach (`sass_identity.txt`).
- **Null arm**, two bit-identical arms in the same sweep (`UIPC_FEM_GH_COST_PROBE=0` vs `=00`,
  n=6 on case2): mean −0.44 % (p=0.15), ms/Newton −0.44 % (p=0.075), both **overlapping**; the
  harness's guard fired on it (PCG −1.02 % against a 0.44 % wall change) which is exactly what a null
  arm should look like — unreadable at that size. The scene's envelope is well inside the +2.78 %
  effect (`null_c2.txt`).
- **Tumbler `--verify` is vacuous here by construction and was not run**, for the same reason s09's
  SASS gate was vacuous: the shipped default computes bit-identical values to 48f1c6e3 (0 changed
  SASS instructions on the only touched TU), and the probe arm writes only to scratch. The
  determinism evidence above is stronger than a 6-run tumbler audit would have been.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **The same shadow, captured from the other side and with no copy: defer the *contact* assembly's join past the FEM elastic G/H** | The contact G/H already runs on a side stream (part 1, stream 14) and is joined at the end of `IPCSimplexNormalContact::do_assemble` (`ipc_simplex_normal_contact.cu:1233-1234`) — but **its results are not read until `FEMLinearSubsystem::_assemble_dytopo_effect`** (`fem_linear_subsystem.cu:443`). Measured on case2 (`gap_c2.txt`, 332 iterations): between the contact launch and that first read the default stream runs **3.44 ms per Newton** of work — SNH G/H 2829 µs, `DiscreteShellBending` 176 µs, `StrainLimitingBaraffWitkinShell` 164 µs, the half-plane assemble 46 µs, the kinetic G/H 33 µs, the buffer fills 45 µs — against a contact assembly of **1198 µs**. The whole contact assembly fits in that shadow **three times over**, and on case2 `GlobalDyTopoEffectManager` takes the **single-receiver fast path** (verified: zero `GlobalDyTopoEffectManager_distribute_*` kernels in a 60-frame trace), so s11's blocking-D2H wall in `_distribute` does not exist there. The prize is the same 0.61 ms/Newton of idle machine (`underfill_c2.txt`) = **~2.2 % of case2**, at **zero** copy cost — which is the whole difference from this step's verdict | case2 first, mas-bunny second (both FEM, both deterministic enough). **The join list is the whole risk and it is enumerable**: `FEMLinearSubsystem::_assemble_dytopo_effect`, `ABDLinearSubsystem::_assemble_dytopo_effect` (`abd_linear_subsystem.cu:1352-1442`), `GlobalDyTopoEffectManager::_convert_matrix` and `_distribute` (which on the non-fast path read the buffers *immediately*, so those scenes get no shadow and must degrade to today's behaviour). Part 2 and `partition_ee` would have to move to the side stream with part 1. Bit-identical is achievable by the s10 argument — the atomic order inside each contact kernel does not change — and must be gated the same way |
| **`fast_segmental_reduce_matrix` is 4.68 % of case2 at 1175 µs per launch and nobody has looked at it** | `kernsum_c2_head.txt`: **1953.7 ms, 1662 launches, 1175.5 µs each, grid 14442-18962 blocks of 128 at 46 registers** — the second-largest single-launch-per-Newton kernel on case2 after SNH G/H, larger than contact part 1 (856 µs). It is the global matrix converter's segmented reduction over the 1.95 M-triplet FEM Hessian. Its fill is ~100 % so it is not an overlap target; it is an arithmetic/bandwidth target, and it has never been profiled in this round | case2 + mas-bunny; a per-kernel `kern_sum` A/B is valid here (this is a kernel-cost change, not a placement change) |
| **`Spmv_rbk_sym_spmv_dot_chunked` is 18.16 % of case2 — more than twice anything else** | `kernsum_c2_head.txt`: **7582.2 ms, 64 345 launches, 117.8 µs each, grid 7977-9815 blocks of 256 at 50 registers**, i.e. once per PCG iteration. Round 6 has not touched the linear solver at all. At 94.5 % machine fill it is a throughput kernel, so the lever is arithmetic or memory layout, not placement | case2 and mas-bunny (mas-bunny is the deterministic instrument); this is the biggest single line item in the FEM half of the suite |
| **`stiff-gipc-case2` is 94.5 % occupancy-filled — the round should stop looking for shadows in it** | `underfill_c2.txt`: the total idle-machine-equivalent over the whole timeline is **5.5 % of kernel time**, and after contact assembly (202 ms) the next eleven contributors are **51, 25, 22, 22, 17, 17, 16, 11, 11, 11, 9 ms** — a long tail of small launches no single stream trick can reach. Any future overlap proposal on this scene is bounded by 4.8 % in total and ~2.2 % for the only window big enough to use | this is a *negative* result to carry, not a step |

## s13 — the SpMV+dot grid is sized from the **raw** triplet count, so **86–89 % of its blocks existed only to read the nnz and exit**. Fitting the grid to the nnz is **−0.97 % of `stiff-gipc-case2` at n=16 (p=3e-06)** with the same triplets in the same blocks and **0 changed SASS instructions** — and the step's second result is that the scope gate said **−8.13 %** and was wrong, because **nsys charges per block**

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s13/`.

**Ships default ON as `UIPC_SPMV_GRID_FIT=1`; `UIPC_SPMV_GRID_FIT=0` is the rollback and the A/B's
old arm, and it reproduces main's launch geometry block for block.**

### The family, re-ranked at this head — and the brief's ranking is case2's, not the suite's

(nsys `cuda_gpu_kern_sum`, full runs, fresh prefix per run.)

| kernel | `stiff-gipc-case2` | `mas-bunny` |
|---|---|---|
| `Spmv_rbk_sym_spmv_dot_chunked<1,0>` | **18.20 %**, 117.75 us x 64 345 | **28.76 %**, 53.36 us x 35 190 |
| `MASPreconditionerEngine_schwarz_local_solve_rowdot` | 8.50 %, 53.4 us | 15.39 %, 27.9 us |
| `MASPreconditionerEngine_build_multi_level_R` | 3.62 %, 22.7 us | 7.38 %, 13.4 us |
| `StableNeoHookean3D` G/H | 11.32 %, 2845 us x 1 657 | 10.44 %, 1451 us x 465 |

On `mas-bunny` the PCG inner loop (SpMV + the three MAS kernels + the four fused vector kernels) is
**~67 % of GPU kernel time**; on case2 it is ~36 %. `mas-bunny` is therefore the instrument for this
family, not just its control.

### The finding, and it is a launch-geometry defect rather than a kernel one

`GlobalLinearSystem::Impl::spmv_dot` sizes the grid from `bcoo_A.triplet_capacity()`
(`global_linear_system.cu:1082`). That capacity is `total_triplet * reserve_ratio` where
`total_triplet` is the **raw, pre-reduce** triplet count (`global_linear_system.cu:852-856`) — and
`bcoo_A` has to carry it, because `MatrixConverter::convert` uses the destination's own index and
value arrays as the sort scratch (`matrix_converter.inl:485`). The matrix the SpMV actually reads is
the *reduced* one:

| scene | nnz (reduced) | blocks needed | blocks launched | idle |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `mas-bunny` | 128 736, constant every frame | 503 | **3 518** | **85.7 %** |
| `stiff-gipc-case2` | ~286 450 | 1 120 | **7 977 / 8 902 / 9 815** | **86–89 %** |
| `cube-wall-cloth` | — | 256–448 | 884–1 179 | ~64 % |
| `rigid-wrecking-balls` | — | 64–185 | 112–184 | ~0–30 % |

Round 4's s02 already saw this and made the idle block cheap (an early exit before the block
reduction, worth 217 -> 112 us on case2). Round 5's s22 saw it again and made the grid *persistent*
(`UIPC_SPMV_GRID_STRIDE`), which was rejected because it changes the order in which one block sums
its dot contributions and the PCG count moved +2 % against it. **Neither removed the blocks while
keeping the mapping.** That is what this step does.

### The change

`Spmv::fit_block_count(nnz, capacity)` returns a grid that covers the current nnz with 12.5 %
headroom, rounded up to 64 blocks, capped at the capacity grid (and returning 0 — "use the capacity
grid" — when the fit would not be smaller, or when `UIPC_SPMV_GRID_FIT=0`, or when
`UIPC_SPMV_CHUNK=0` selects the per-triplet kernel, which has no virtual-block loop and must keep
the capacity grid). `GlobalLinearSystem::Impl` holds the result as a **monotone** high-water mark
updated once per assembly, right where `triplet_count_dev` is uploaded, and passes it to
`Spmv::rbk_sym_spmv_dot`.

**Why it is safe under graph replay, twice over.**
1. The number is part of the FusedPCG graph's validity key
   (`IterativeSolver::spmv_grid_key()` -> `m_graph_spmv_grid` / `m_while_spmv_grid`), so a matrix
   that outgrows the captured grid forces a re-capture instead of a replay. Monotone, so a matrix
   that grows by one triplet does not.
2. Even if it did not, s22's virtual-block loop is *already in the shipped binary* — with the
   capacity grid every block simply breaks after one pass. A grid smaller than the nnz needs would
   therefore still compute the right answer (it would grid-stride), it would only change the
   summation order. The key is what keeps that from happening; the loop is what makes the failure
   mode "different rounding" rather than "dropped triplets".

**Why it is the same arithmetic.** With `G >= ceil(nnz / 256)`, block `b < ceil(nnz/256)` walks
virtual block `b` and then breaks — exactly what it did with the capacity grid — and blocks
`b >= ceil(nnz/256)` take the same `blockIdx.x * 256 >= triplet_count` early return the spare
capacity blocks took, *before* the warp reductions and before any atomic. So the per-thread
arithmetic, the per-block dot reduction and the **multiset of `atomicAdd` operands** into `y` and
`d_dot` are all unchanged; only how many no-op blocks the launch carries changes.

### Scope: one build, env switch, full runs, nothing else on the box

`scope_gridfit.txt` (nsys `cuda_gpu_kern_sum` + the per-launch grid shape from the session's own
`CUPTI_ACTIVITY_KIND_KERNEL` rows, so the geometry is read from the same session as the time):

| scene | capacity grid | fitted grid | delta | blocks (capacity -> fitted) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`** | 117.754 us | **108.180 us** | **−8.13 %** | 9815/8902/7977 -> 1408/1344/1280 |
| **`mas-bunny`** | 53.356 us | **51.786 us** | **−2.94 %** | 3518 -> 576 |
| `cube-wall-cloth` | 31.360 us | 31.566 us | **+0.66 %** | 1179/1071/973/884 -> 448/384/320/256 |
| `rigid-wrecking-balls` | 10.005 us | 9.543 us | −4.62 % | 123/184/112/136 -> 123/128/185/64 |

Two things in that table are worth more than the headline.

- **The effect is not proportional to the idle blocks removed.** case2 sheds 8 400 idle blocks for
  9.57 us (1.14 ns each) and `mas-bunny` sheds 2 942 for 1.57 us (0.53 ns each), while
  `cube-wall-cloth` sheds ~700 for nothing measurable. An idle block is not a fixed tax; it is a tax
  that grows with how long the launch it is appended to already is. **Do not extrapolate this win
  from a block count** — measure it per scene.
- **`cube-wall-cloth` is the honest negative.** At n=1 per arm +0.66 % is inside the scope
  measurement's own scatter (the two capacity-grid `mas-bunny` runs in this step read 52.815 and
  53.356 us, 1.0 % apart), so the reading is "flat", not "worse". It is reported because a step that
  only shows its two best scenes is not a measurement.

**Against round 5's R6 at the same head**, measured in the same build: `UIPC_SPMV_GRID_STRIDE=1`
reads **109.784 us on case2 (−6.68 %)** and **51.747 us on `mas-bunny` (−2.02 % against that arm's
own 52.815 pairing)**. R6's cc 7.5 number reproduces exactly (round 5 recorded −6.6 %). **The fitted
grid beats it on case2 (−8.13 % vs −6.68 %) and matches it on `mas-bunny`** — and it does so without
changing the summation order, which is what made R6 unreadable end to end.

### Numerics: the same operands, proved at the binary and at the launch

- **SASS: 0 changed instructions in all three touched TUs.** `spmv.cu` (22 functions, 8 248
  instructions), `linear_fused_pcg.cu` (43 / 18 528) and `global_linear_system.cu` (57 / 20 664)
  compiled at this head and at the branch base `0f276920` with the identical `build-perf` command
  line and diffed per function after normalising the anonymous-namespace mangling: **identical**
  (`sass_identity.txt`). The kernel under test is bit-for-bit the kernel main ships, so the old arm
  of every A/B below reproduces main's binary exactly (the s01 lesson) and no per-thread arithmetic
  can have moved.
- **Launch mapping**: argued above — same `(blockIdx, threadIdx) -> triplet` map, same early exit for
  the spare blocks, same multiset of atomic operands.
- **The level is "same operands", not "bit-identical", and the difference is pre-existing.** `y` and
  `d_dot` are accumulated with `atomicAdd` over hundreds of blocks, so this kernel is not
  reproducible run to run in *either* arm: ten baseline `mas-bunny` runs from s12 span PCG
  **35 185–35 225 (0.11 %)** at a fixed Newton count of 465. That envelope is the reference the
  counts below are judged against, per PERF_METHOD §2.3.


### End to end — and this is where the step stops being a −8 % story

`ab.py`, one build, env switch, ABBA order, one discarded warm-up per arm.

| scene | n/arm | mean ms/frame off -> fit | delta | ms per Newton | Newton | PCG |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`** | 8 | 165.8153 -> **164.4691** | **−0.81 %, t=3.76, p=0.0021** | **−0.78 %, p=0.0015** | −0.03 % | +0.40 % |
| `mas-bunny` | 10 | 66.6756 -> 66.6396 | −0.05 %, p=0.42 | −0.05 % | **465 in all 20 runs** | −0.03 % |
| `cube-wall-cloth` | 6 | 56.4374 -> 56.3166 | −0.21 %, p=0.84 | +0.11 % | −0.33 % | +0.21 % |
| `rigid-wrecking-balls` | 6 | 25.7748 -> 25.6965 | −0.30 %, p=0.74 | −0.08 % | −0.21 % | −1.49 %, **guard fired** |

**The null arm**, two identical arms (`=1` vs `=01`) in the same `mas-bunny` sweep, n=6: **+0.10 %,
p=0.18, overlapping**, with Newton and line search 465 in all twelve runs. That is the scene's
envelope, and `mas-bunny`'s −0.05 % sits inside it: **the fitted grid is worth nothing on
`mas-bunny`, measured.** `rigid-wrecking-balls`' wall is unreadable by the harness's own guard
(PCG moved 1.49 % against a 0.30 % wall change) and its ms/Newton is −0.08 % — flat either way.

**case2 was then measured a second time, independently, because it is the only scene that moves.**
A fresh n=8 sweep read **−1.14 % mean (p=0.00094)** and **−1.08 % ms/Newton, DISJOINT
(p=0.00014)** (`results_c2b.txt`). Pooled over both sweeps, **n=16 per arm** (`pooled_c2.txt`):

| `stiff-gipc-case2`, n=16/arm | capacity grid | fitted grid | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| mean ms/frame | 165.9038 | 164.2869 | **−0.97 %, t=5.73, p=3.0e-06** |
| **ms per Newton iteration** | 24.9667 | 24.7345 | **−0.93 %, t=6.96, p=2.9e-07** |
| ms per PCG iteration | 0.6479 | 0.6414 | −1.00 %, p=1.1e-04 |
| Newton | 1661.25 | 1660.50 | −0.05 %, p=0.66 |
| PCG | 64 020.9 | 64 032.8 | +0.02 %, p=0.96 |
| line search | 1786.9 | 1777.0 | −0.55 % |

Both counts are flat to 0.05 % against a 0.97 % wall change, so the harness's guard stays silent and
the wall number is readable — and it is readable for the stronger reason that the change cannot move
a computed quantity at all.

So: **one scene of four shows the effect, and it is −0.97 %, not −8 %.**

### Why the scope over-promised, and it is the profiler, not the scene

The obvious reading — "the kernel got 8 % faster, so 18 % of the scene got 8 % faster" — predicts
−1.46 % on case2 and −0.83 % on `mas-bunny`. Measured: −0.81 % and −0.05 %. The gap is not
scene-dependent noise; it has a cause, and the same runs measure it (`instrument_check.txt`):

| arm | nsys ms/frame | `ab.py` ms/frame | profiling overhead |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `mas-bunny` capacity grid | 69.500 | 66.676 | **+4.24 %** |
| `mas-bunny` fitted grid | 68.600 | 66.640 | **+2.94 %** |
| case2 capacity grid | 168.800 | 165.815 | **+1.80 %** |
| case2 fitted grid | 166.500 | 164.469 | **+1.23 %** |

**nsys's own overhead is larger in the arm that launches more blocks** — by 1.29 pp on `mas-bunny`
and 0.57 pp on case2. The shortfall of the scope prediction against the measured wall is 0.78 pp and
0.65 pp. **The two agree to ~0.1 pp on both scenes.** Most of the "−2.94 % per launch" on
`mas-bunny` was `nsys --cuda-graph-trace=node` charging for blocks it was tracing.

**This retires one more use of an instrument the round has already trimmed twice.** `kern_sum` per
kernel remains valid for *ranking* (a kernel that is 18 % of the scene really is), and it remains
valid for a change that alters a kernel's *work*. It is **not** a wall forecast for a change that
alters a kernel's **launch geometry**, because the profiler's cost is partly per block. A launch-
geometry step must be decided on the un-instrumented benchmark, the same conclusion s11 reached for
placement steps by a completely different route.
### Round 5's two default-off switches, re-measured at this head — the other half of the brief

Both shipped default-off with a prediction attached and neither had been re-tested since. One build,
`ab.py`, same protocol.

**R6, `UIPC_SPMV_GRID_STRIDE=1`** (the persistent grid-stride rewrite), against the capacity grid it
was rejected against, `UIPC_SPMV_GRID_FIT=0` pinned in both arms so this is one variable
(`r6_stride_c2.txt`, `stiff-gipc-case2`, n=6):

| | cap | stride | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| mean ms/frame | 165.3055 | 164.7700 | −0.32 %, p=0.21, overlapping |
| **ms per Newton it** | 24.9179 | 24.7799 | **−0.55 %, p=0.0089** |
| Newton | 1658.5 | 1662.3 | +0.23 % |
| PCG | 63 696.7 | 63 862.5 | +0.26 % |

**R6 is real but small, and the fitted grid beats it.** Round 5 could not read it because its PCG
count moved +2 % against it; at this head the counts move 0.23–0.26 % and the reading is clean.
Against the fitted grid's **−0.93 % per Newton at n=16**, grid-stride gets about **half** — and it
pays for it by changing the order in which a block sums its dot contributions, which the fitted grid
does not. **Verdict: R6 stays default-off, now superseded rather than merely unmeasured.** Its
5090 prediction is untouched; the fitted grid inherits it.

**R7, `UIPC_PCG_FOLD=1 UIPC_PCG_FOLD_MAXGRID=0`** (the scalar fold, ungated — the default gate
switches it off on every scene in the suite because the `update_p` grid is 225–500 blocks against 40
SMs, so `UIPC_PCG_FOLD=1` alone is a **no-op** and would have been a vacuous arm)
(`r7_fold_mb.txt`, `mas-bunny`, n=8):

| | nofold | fold1 | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| mean ms/frame | 66.6357 | 66.7292 | **+0.14 %, p=0.046** |
| ms per PCG it | 0.18920 | 0.18954 | **+0.18 %, p=0.0087** |
| Newton / line search | **465 / 465 in all 16 runs** | **465 / 465** | +0.00 % |

**R7 is a small, statistically resolvable regression on cc 7.5 — round 5's rejection is confirmed at
this head on the scene that can resolve 0.1 %.** Default-off stands. Its per-wave cost argument for
a 5090 is unaffected by this measurement and remains untested.

### Gates

- **`gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, identical counts in both arms** (default `UIPC_SPMV_GRID_FIT=1`
  and the rollback `=0`): 11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed
  1 skipped (`gate_fit.txt`, `gate_off.txt`). The only textual difference from the baseline file is
  pytest's own wall time.
- **Numerics**: SASS identical in all three touched TUs (above); `mas-bunny` reports Newton 465 and
  line search 465 in every one of the 20 runs of the main A/B and all 12 of the null arm, with PCG
  inside the baseline's own 35 185–35 240 envelope.
- **Tumbler `--verify` was not run.** The shipped default computes the same values per block as
  `0f276920` (0 changed SASS instructions; the same triplets in the same blocks in the same order),
  so a trajectory audit has nothing to find that the `mas-bunny` count evidence does not already
  show. **The tumbler was also not benchmarked**: s00 puts its SpMV at 5.8 % of its GPU kernel time,
  the scene cannot resolve below ~4 % on the mean, and the effect here is a fraction of a percent.
  Stated as a limit, not as a pass.

### Transfer prediction

Category (PERF_METHOD §6): **grid-size against machine-size**, but with the sign of the *ratio*, not
of the machine. What is removed is a fixed number of no-op blocks determined by the matrix
(`capacity / nnz`, which is a property of the assembly, not of the GPU); what remains is the real
launch, which gets *faster* on a bigger part. So the idle-block share of the launch **grows** and the
win should grow with it — the same prediction round 5's R6 recorded and which has still never been
tested on a 5090. Two caveats this step can state that R6 could not:

- The per-idle-block cost measured here is **not constant across scenes** (1.14 ns on case2, 0.53 ns
  on `mas-bunny`, ~0 on `cube-wall-cloth`), so "blocks removed x a constant" is not a forecast.
- Block dispatch rate is an architectural property that has moved between generations. The honest
  claim is **sign, not size**: a 5090 should still win here, by an amount this box cannot predict.

Nothing in the change is register-pressure, spill, wave-quantisation or `__launch_bounds__` driven —
the binary is identical — so none of the three categories this box over-rewards apply.

### Limits, stated plainly

- **The graph key term is load-bearing and it is the only thing standing between a fitted grid and a
  stale replay.** If a future solver captures `rbk_sym_spmv_dot` without carrying
  `spmv_grid_key()`, it must pass `grid_blocks = 0`. The header says so at the declaration.
  (The virtual-block loop means the failure mode would be a rounding change, not a wrong answer —
  but that is a second line of defence, not the design.)
- **`cube-wall-cloth` gains nothing** and is reported as flat rather than as a win.
- **Scope numbers are single full runs per arm**, used only for the within-run per-launch ratio and
  the geometry; every cross-arm wall number comes from the `ab.py` sweeps.
- **The headroom and quantum (12.5 %, 64 blocks) were not tuned.** They were chosen to keep the graph
  key still and were not swept; a different pair might give back another fraction of a percent.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **The MAS preconditioner inner loop is the round's largest untouched family, and on `mas-bunny` it is bigger than the SpMV** | `kernsum_mb_head.txt`: `schwarz_local_solve_rowdot` **15.4 % of `mas-bunny`** (27.9 us x 35 670, grid 686 x 96 at 39 reg) + `build_multi_level_R` **7.4 %** (13.4 us, grid 80 x 256) + `collect_final_Z` **3.2 %** (grid 81 x 256) = **26 %**; on case2 the same three are 8.50 + 3.62 + 1.57 = **13.7 %**. Nobody has profiled `mas_preconditioner_engine.cu` in six rounds. Two shapes are visible without reading a line of it: `rowdot`'s grid is **686 blocks of 3 warps = 1.7 waves** on a 400-block-resident device (wave quantisation, ~15 % of the launch), and `build_multi_level_R`/`collect_final_Z` fill only **640 of 1280 warp slots** — but that is a *parallelism* limit, not a geometry one, so `launch_spread` will not help (halving the block size leaves the same warp count). The lever is the kernel body or the level structure | `mas-bunny` (26 % share, deterministic, resolves 0.1 %); case2 as the second instrument |
| **`launch_spread` has no caller in `mas_preconditioner_engine.cu` or anywhere in `linear_system/`** | `grep -rln 'launch_spread\|spread_grid_dim' src/backends/cuda --include=*.cu` returns **ten files, all ABD / contact / bending** — zero in the FEM preconditioner and zero in the linear solver, which together are 40 % of `mas-bunny`'s kernel time. `linear_fused_pcg.cu` *includes* `spread_launch.h` and then calls `best_block_dim` directly at four sites. This is the §4 pattern again, though see the caveat above: the MAS grids are parallelism-limited, so check per kernel that the grid really is small *relative to the device* before spreading | per call site; `mas-bunny` |
| **`fast_segmental_reduce_matrix` is 4.69 % of case2 and still nobody has looked at it** | `kernsum_c2_head.txt`: **1954.4 ms, 1657 launches, 1179.5 us each, grid 14 699 blocks of 128 at 46 registers.** s12 nominated it and it is untouched. At ~100 % fill it is an arithmetic/memory target, not a placement one | case2 (4.7 %) and `mas-bunny` (3.5 %) |
| ~~`Spmv_rbk_sym_spmv_dot_chunked` launch geometry~~ | **done — this step.** What is left in that kernel is body-level: s22's probes put it at **loads 31 us / no-atomics 43 / real 53** on bunny, i.e. ~58 % load-bound and ~19 % atomics, and the FP64 issue cost alone is ~17 us of 53 on a 1/32-rate part. A lower-traffic storage (SoA for the nine block components, or 6 doubles for the symmetric diagonal blocks) is the only remaining lever and it is a `DeviceBCOOMatrix` change, not a kernel change | — |
| **The capacity/nnz ratio is a property of the assembly and nobody has tried to shrink it** | `bcoo_A`'s capacity is the **raw** triplet count because `MatrixConverter::convert` uses the destination's own index/value arrays as sort scratch (`matrix_converter.inl:485`). Give the converter its own scratch and `bcoo_A` could be reserved from the *reduced* high-water mark instead — which would remove this step's fitted-grid machinery *and* free ~7x the BCOO storage (case2: 2.5 M triplet slots of 80 B = 200 MB for 286 k triplets). That is a memory-footprint step as much as a speed one, and this box has 8 GB | measure allocation first; `stiff-gipc-case2` and the tumbler |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **Round 4's note that `rigid-wrecking-balls` has "<= 32k unique triplets, > 4M capacity"
  (`spmv.cu`, the `Spmv_rbk_sym_spmv_dot_kernel` comment) is stale.** Measured at this head the rwb
  SpMV grid is **112–185 blocks** (a capacity of 29–47 k triplets), and the fitted grid can only
  shrink it to 64–185 — so the capacity/nnz ratio there is now **~1 to 1.5, not ~100**. Whatever
  changed it is not recorded. The comment is the justification for the
  capacity-grid design and it now overstates its own motivation; worth correcting when someone next
  touches that file.
- **`cube-wall-cloth` is the one scene where removing idle blocks does nothing at all**
  (+0.66 % at scope, −0.21 % end to end), despite a 2.6x capacity/nnz ratio. If a later step wants a
  model of what an idle block costs, that scene is the counterexample it has to explain.

---

## s14 — s12's dual: the contact assembly's join moved from "when the host returns" to "when the first reader asks", with no copy at all — **−1.26 % of `stiff-gipc-case2` (ms/Newton, n=12, p=4e-06)** against a re-measured ceiling of 2.2 %, and the join list is the whole step

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s14/` (`README.md` maps every file onto
the claim it backs; `predictions.txt`, `read_set.txt`, `census_c2_head.txt`, `census_c2_m1.txt`,
`census_c2_m2.txt`, `kmeans_c2.txt`, `verify_runs.log`, `sass_identity.txt`, `results_c2.txt`,
`results_*.txt`, `gate_*.txt`, `tverify_stats.txt`, `bcoo_hash_*.txt`, `ab/`).

**Switch `UIPC_CONTACT_DEFERRED_JOIN`: `0` = today's join (the rollback), `1` = deferred, part 2
stays on the default stream — the default; `2` = deferred and part 2 on a second side stream
(measured, a tie, kept behind the switch).** Verify mode `UIPC_CONTACT_DEFERRED_JOIN_VERIFY=1`.

### The premise, checked first — and it holds at this head

s12 measured this candidate's shape on `48f1c6e3`; s13 has landed since, so the three facts the
step rests on were re-measured on a fresh 60-frame `stiff-gipc-case2` trace at `8ab46206`
(`census_c2_head.txt`):

- **case2 takes the single-receiver fast path**: zero `GlobalDyTopoEffectManager_distribute_*`
  launches, and the converter's kernels appear 329 times — once per Newton iteration for the
  global linear system, never for the dytopo manager. The blocking D2H that blocked s10/s11
  (`_distribute`, `global_dytopo_effect_manager.cu:404` at that head) is not on this scene's path.
- **The ceiling**: contact part 1 is 8 blocks on 40 SMs for 879 µs, part 2 22 blocks for 318 µs,
  and the idle-machine-equivalent inside part 1's windows is **541 µs per Newton iteration**
  (`dur × (1 − fill)` over the launches inside each window; s12's 0.61 ms counted part 2's own
  window as well). Against `ab.py`'s 24.8 ms per Newton iteration that is **2.2 %** — the same
  number as s12's, by a slightly different route.
- **The shadow is empty**: SNH G/H starts a mean **378 µs after part 1 ends** (min 253); 0.0 % of
  part 1's window is covered by any kernel other than part 2. That gap is the join at
  `ipc_simplex_normal_contact.cu:1233` plus the fills and the kinetic G/H that were queued behind it.

### The read set, derived from the code (`read_set.txt`)

The side stream writes exactly the PT/EE gradient and Hessian sub-views that
`SimplexNormalContact::do_assemble` carves out of the manager's `collected_dytopo_effect_*`
buffers (`simplex_normal_contact.cu:99-160`), and nothing else. Every consumer of those buffers,
and where the join now sits in front of it:

| # | consumer | first device read | join |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `FEMLinearSubsystem::Impl::report_extent` | none — host counts only (`fem_linear_subsystem.cu:290-296`) | no join needed: now `gradient_count()` / `hessian_count()` |
| 2 | `FEMLinearSubsystem::Impl::_assemble_dytopo_effect` | `gradients()` / `hessians()` (`:530-559`) | **the accessor joins** — the first read on case2 / mas-bunny, after the FEM kinetic, SNH, bending and strain-limiting launches |
| 3 | `ABDLinearSubsystem::Impl::report_extent` | none (`abd_linear_subsystem.cu:898`) | `hessian_count()` |
| 4 | `ABDLinearSubsystem::Impl::_prepare_dytopo_pairs` | `hessians()` row/col indices (`:1245`) | the accessor joins — inside `_update_subsystem_extent`, i.e. before any ABD assembly work is issued |
| 5 | `ABDLinearSubsystem::Impl::_assemble_dytopo_effect` | `gradients()` / `hessians()` (`:1330-1442`) | the accessor joins (already taken by 4) |
| 6 | the manager's converter (multi-receiver path: cube-wall-cloth, tumbler) | `matrix_converter.convert(collected_*)` | `join_assemble()` immediately before `_convert_matrix()` (`global_dytopo_effect_manager.cu:241`) — **today's join point, to the microsecond** |
| 7 | `_distribute` and the classified receiver views | the *sorted* buffers written by 6 on the default stream | stream order after 6 |
| 8 | the two coupling receivers (`abd_fem_linear_subsystem.cu:116-140`) | `hessians()` | the accessor joins (idempotent; taken by 6) |
| 9 | the next `_assemble`'s `loose_resize_entries` (may `cudaFree` the buffers) | — | `join_assemble()` at the top of `_assemble` (`:223`) |
| 10 | the next `do_assemble`'s `partition_ee` (rewrites `m_ee_perm`, an input of part 1) | — | `do_join_assemble()` at the top of `do_assemble` (`ipc_simplex_normal_contact.cu:1229`) |
| 11 | the contact exporters (`copy_to` on the legacy default stream, which a `cudaStreamNonBlocking` side stream is not ordered against) | `PT_gradients().copy_to` etc. | `join_assemble()` in `ContactExporterManager::get_contact_gradient/hessian` |
| 12 | the line search / energy | positions and pair lists only — never the assembled G/H; and they run after `solve()`, whose assembly joins | — |

The inputs part 1 reads and somebody later writes — positions (the line-search step), the pair
lists (the trajectory filter), `m_ee_perm` (the next `partition_ee`), the collected buffers
themselves (the next resize) — are all ordered after `solve()`'s assembly (rows 2/4/5) or after the
two top-of-function joins (rows 9/10). The AL pipeline uses the AL contact models, not
`IPCSimplexNormalContact` (`require<IPCPipelineFlag>()`), so nothing is ever deferred there.

**The structural guarantee, which is the design choice the brief asked for.** The join is not
"deleted and re-added at the places I found"; it is moved *into the accessor*. A
`DyTopoEffectReceiver` hands out a device view of the received data only through `gradients()` /
`hessians()`, and those now call `join_assemble()` — `DyTopoEffectReceiver::join_assemble()` →
`GlobalDyTopoEffectManager::join_assemble()` → every reporter's `do_join_assemble()` (a new virtual
with an empty default; `IPCSimplexNormalContact` overrides it with a `cudaStreamWaitEvent` on its
join event(s), guarded by a `m_join_pending` flag so that it is idempotent). The only accessors
that do *not* join are the two new host-count ones, by name. A consumer somebody adds later
cannot read the data without joining; the failure mode of a *missed* call site is an early join
(the overlap is lost), not a race.

**The non-fast path keeps today's semantics by construction.** `compute_dytopo_effect()` calls
`join_assemble()` right before `_convert_matrix()` — the first instruction after `_assemble()`
returns, which is where the host used to be when the old join was taken. The verify run on
cube-wall-cloth proves the fallback is taken: 41 deferred joins in 40 frames, all 41 found
"pending" at host time (the host had returned from `do_assemble` microseconds earlier), and the
scene reads **+0.02 % ms/Newton (p = 0.97)** end to end.

### What changed, in one paragraph

`IPCSimplexNormalContact::do_assemble` records the join event as before and, under
`UIPC_CONTACT_DEFERRED_JOIN=1`, sets `m_join_pending` instead of waiting on it; the wait moves to
`do_join_assemble()`. Mode 2 additionally launches part 2 on a second `cudaStreamNonBlocking`
stream forked from the same event, with its own join event. `UIPC_GRID_SPREAD_VERIFY=1` forces
mode 0 (the s10 lesson: the spread verifiers stage on the default stream). Host code only: the
eight touched device TUs compile to **byte-identical SASS** for every pre-existing function; the
contact TU gains exactly one function, the 32-instruction verify-only `dj_compare_words_kernel`,
as a single added hunk with 0 removed lines (`sass_identity.txt`: 829 512 → 829 544 instructions,
74 → 75 functions; the other seven TUs 6640 / 38 016 / 31 128 / 544 / 544 / 544 / 1320
instructions, identical).

### Numerics: bit-identical, proved on device — and a measurement of how load-bearing the join is

The kernels, their arguments, their launch geometry and their atomic order are unchanged; the
only thing that moves is *when the default stream waits*. `UIPC_CONTACT_DEFERRED_JOIN_VERIFY=1`
takes two snapshots of the PT/EE (mode 2: + PE/PP) output regions per assemble:
`snap_side`, copied **on the side stream right after the contact kernels** (so it is the kernels'
output by stream order), and `snap_stale`, copied **on the default stream at the deferred-join
point before the wait** — what a reader at that position would have seen without the join. After
the wait, a device kernel counts 32-bit words where `live != snap_side` (must be 0: nothing
overwrote the buffers between the kernels and the reader) and where `snap_stale != snap_side`
(the words that were still unwritten at the reader's GPU position). `verify_runs.log`:

| scene, mode | deferred joins | pending at host time | words compared | mismatching after join | stale without join |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| case2, 1 | 187 | 183 (97.9 %) | 49 830 540 | **0** | 0 words, 0 calls |
| case2, 2 | 187 | 186 (99.5 %) | 230 169 051 | **0** | 0 |
| cube-wall-cloth, 1 (fallback) | 41 | 41 (100 %) | 18 789 936 | **0** | 0 |
| rigid-wrecking-balls, 1 | 110 | 109 (99.1 %) | 170 123 112 | **0** | 0 |
| mas-bunny, 1 | 21 | 18 (85.7 %) | 361 608 | **0** | 0 |
| tumbler, 2 (fallback) | 200 | 199 (99.5 %) | 1 005 786 795 | **0** | 0 |

**1.475 × 10⁹ words compared, 0 mismatching.** The "stale" column corrects prediction P7: I
predicted that on case2 the contact kernels would still be running when the reader's copy
executed, in a large fraction of the iterations. They never are. The host reaches the join point
while the event is pending 98 % of the time (the host runs 3–4 ms ahead), but by the time the
default stream *reaches* that point on the GPU it has executed the fills, the kinetic G/H and SNH —
3.4 ms of work against a 1.04 ms part 1 — so the wait is always already satisfied. That is the
mechanism of the win stated as a measurement: the join costs nothing because it is never taken
late, and it is never taken late because the reader is 3 ms of GPU work behind the launch.

End-to-end determinism agrees: `mas-bunny` reports **Newton 465 and line search 465 in all 16
runs** of its sweep, PCG within 0.01 %. Prediction P6 also promised an identical `UIPC_BCOO_HASH`
first-conversion hash across arms; **that instrument turned out to be dead at this head** — two
runs of the *same* arm (`=0`, `=0`) give different first-conversion hashes on `mas-bunny`
(`bcoo_hash_repeat.txt`), so the hash cannot distinguish a race from the tree's own
non-determinism. Its comment in `global_linear_system.cu` ("the first conversion of a run depends
only on the deterministic initial state") is stale; reported under "found outside". The
device-side comparison above is the proof; the hash is not.

### Mechanism, from the trace of the changed binary (`census_c2_m1.txt`, `kmeans_c2.txt`)

Predicted (P3): SNH starts before part 1 ends in > 90 % of iterations, part 1 slows by 0–5 %,
SNH by 2–4 %. Measured on the same 60-frame window:

| | head (`=0`) | mode 1 | mode 2 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| SNH start − part 1 end, median | +324 µs | **−248 µs** | −244 µs |
| iterations where SNH starts before part 1 ends | 0.0 % | **76.2 %** | 75.1 % |
| part 1 window covered by kernels other than part 2 | 0.0 % | **61.3 %** (SNH 29.4 %) | 60.7 % |
| contact part 1 µs/launch | 878.7 | **1038.6 (+18 %)** | 1030.5 |
| contact part 2 µs/launch (stream) | 317.7 (7) | 313.5 (7) | 316.2 (15) |
| SNH G/H µs/launch | 2829.6 | 2842.2 (+0.4 %) | 2828.1 |
| strain-limiting shell µs/launch | 144.7 | 156.5 (+8 %) | 155.7 |
| half-plane assemble µs/launch | 46.1 | 52.9 (+15 %) | 51.3 |
| fills µs/launch | 7.2 | 8.1 | 8.1 |

Two predictions were wrong in the informative direction. SNH starts early in 76 % of iterations,
not 90 % — the remaining quarter are iterations where part 1 is short enough to finish inside the
fills-and-kinetic gap. And **part 1 slows by 18 %, not 0–5 %**: its 8 blocks are resident before
SNH is issued and cannot share an SM with SNH's 21 504-register blocks, so this is not SM
contention but memory-system contention with 1249 SNH blocks on the other 32 SMs — and it does
not matter, because part 1 is off the critical path now: the reader's wait is never taken late
(above). The kernels that *are* on the critical path — SNH, bending, the dytopo copy — move by
+0.4 %, +0.3 % and 0.0 %; the small default-stream kernels that now share the machine with part 1
(strain limiting, the half-plane assemble, the fills) pay 8–15 % of their own short durations.
The census says the same thing at the machine level: idle-machine-equivalent inside part 1's
windows goes 541 → 761 µs per iteration — the window got *longer* (part 1 is slower) while the
fraction of it that is filled went from 0 to 61 %.

### End to end — predicted first, then measured (`results_c2.txt`)

Predicted (P4): mode 1 −0.8 to −1.6 % ms/Newton on case2, mode 2 −1.2 to −2.2 %, mode 2 ≥ mode 1.

`stiff-gipc-case2`, **four arms interleaved in one build, n = 12 per arm** (s11's `abn.py`:
rotated + reversed order per rep, one discarded warm-up per arm), the fourth arm a bit-identical
null (`=00`, which `atoi` reads as 0):

| | off (`=0`) | **mode 1** | mode 2 | null |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| mean ms/frame | 165.065 | **162.921 −1.30 %, p = 4.0e-05** | 162.616 −1.48 %, DISJOINT, p = 2.0e-07 | 168.361 (+2.00 %, p = 0.31; see below) |
| **ms per Newton** | 24.825 | **24.512 −1.26 %, t = 6.25, p = 4.1e-06** | 24.511 −1.27 %, p = 1.6e-07 | 25.314 |
| ms per PCG | 0.6432 | 0.6352 −1.24 %, p = 3.3e-06 | 0.6329 −1.61 %, p = 2.8e-06 | 0.6310 |
| Newton | 1662.3 | 1661.7 −0.04 % | 1658.6 −0.22 % (p = 0.03) | 1662.8 |
| PCG | 64 158 | 64 122 −0.06 % | 64 240 +0.13 % | 67 375 (+5.0 %) |
| line search | 1782.8 | 1790.8 +0.44 % | 1775.9 −0.39 % | 1792.1 |

**Mode 1 lands inside its prediction (−1.26 % against −0.8 to −1.6); mode 2 lands at the bottom
edge of its (−1.27 % against −1.2 to −2.2), and the two are a tie: m1 vs m2 ms/Newton
Welch p = 0.996, mean p = 0.44.** Mode 2's disjoint *mean* is partly a −0.22 % Newton drift in its
favour; per Newton iteration there is nothing between them. Mode 1 ships because it is the
simpler mechanism (one stream, one event) for the same number. Taking part 2 off the default
stream buys nothing because part 2 (318 µs, 22 blocks) was already 97 % hidden inside part 1
(s09) and the default stream's next kernels are queued behind nothing else once the join is gone.

The counts are flat to 0.06 % against a 1.26 % wall change in mode 1, so the harness's guard is
silent and the number is readable — for the stronger reason that not one computed quantity can
differ (above). **Against the ceiling: 1.26 of 2.2 % = 57 % of the idle machine inside part 1's
window is now used**, with part 1 itself 18 % slower and the small co-resident kernels 8–15 %
slower absorbing the rest.

**The null arm carries the round's second solver-stall sighting, on the shipped code.** Run
`null_r11` reads 201.95 ms/frame with **PCG 101 830 against 63 000–65 500 in the other 47 runs of
the sweep, at the same Newton count (1661)**: frame 149 alone took 36 640 PCG iterations in 7
Newton iterations against 325 in its sibling run — the linear solver ran to its iteration cap on
that frame's trajectory. The null arm executes the `=0` code path bit for bit, so this is the
scene's own non-determinism landing on a bad trajectory once in 48 runs, not this step; excluding
that run the null reads +0.15 % mean / +0.11 % ms/Newton (p = 0.52 / 0.55), the scene's envelope.
Reported under "found outside".

### The untargeted scenes — and one of them is not the control it was supposed to be

| scene | n | ms/Newton, mode 1 vs off | counts | reading |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| `mas-bunny` | 8 | **−0.25 %, p = 0.0025** (mean −0.25 %, p = 0.0025) | **Newton 465 / line search 465 in all 16 runs**, PCG −0.01 % | real, and explained: see below |
| `cube-wall-cloth` | 8 | **+0.02 %, p = 0.97** | Newton −0.71 % (guard fired on the mean; ms/Newton is the statistic) | **flat — the fallback path, as predicted** |
| `rigid-wrecking-balls` | 8, then 16 | −1.54 %, p = 0.020 at n = 8 with the PCG guard firing; **re-measured at n = 16: −0.67 %, p = 0.088** (mean +0.23 %, p = 0.71) | Newton +0.91 %, PCG +0.51 % — guard fired both times | **flat, as predicted**; the n = 8 reading was the 1-in-20 accident the doctrine warns about |
| `tumbler-garments` | 5 | +0.92 %, p = 0.76; median −2.58 % | PCG +4.0 %, guard fired | unresolved — fallback path, scene floor ~4 % |

`mas-bunny` was nominated as a vacuous control ("21 contact-assembly launches", s09). It is not
vacuous for *this* step: the verify run counts **21 deferred joins per run**, 18 of them pending
at host time, and each one hides a ~0.6–0.9 ms part 1 behind SNH on a scene where it used to be
serial — 21 × ~0.8 ms = ~17 ms of a 6 675 ms run = **0.25 %**, which is the measured number to the
digit. It is the cleanest end-to-end statement of the mechanism in the suite, because its counts
are identical in every run.

`rigid-wrecking-balls` was predicted flat (P5) because the ABD subsystem reads the contact Hessian
in `report_extent` (`_prepare_dytopo_pairs`), before any assembly work is issued. That prediction
missed a reader that is *not* the ABD subsystem: after the simplex contact's `do_assemble`
returns, the manager's `_assemble` loop continues with the **frictional** and half-plane
reporters on the default stream — under the old join those waited for part 1; now they run in its
shadow. On rwb that reader is worth nothing: the scene has **no friction kernels at all** and its
half-plane assemble is 30 µs, so the full-run traces (`census_rwb.txt`) show part 1's window
covered **54.2 % (off) → 55.7 % (mode 1)**, all of it the s10/s11 ABD prepass on stream 15, in
both arms. The n = 8 reading of −1.54 % (p = 0.02, guard firing) reversed to **−0.67 %, p = 0.088 at
n = 16** with the guard still firing — flat, and exactly the s11 pattern: a sub-1 % effect at n = 8
that a scene whose null envelope is ±0.9 % cannot resolve. The prediction stands, for the reason
given plus one the prediction missed (the friction reporter is a reader that would matter on a
scene that has friction and a single receiver; none of the five does).

### Gates

- `gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, **identical counts in all three arms** (`=1` default, `=0`,
  `=2`): 11/3, 1112/36, 2730/46, 100/3, 4/1, 448/23, 14213/95, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped
  (`gate_m1.txt`, `gate_off.txt`, `gate_m2.txt`).
- Tumbler `--verify`, n = 6 per arm (`tverify_stats.txt`): `verify_ok` / `all_finite` / `contained` true in all 12; 0 non-converged, 0 Newton-limit, 0 line-search-limit frames in either arm; every observable overlapping (`cc_min_dist` 2.524 → 2.469 mm p = 0.10, `bore_gap_min` 0.783 → 0.821 mm p = 0.37, `area_ratio_max` 1.524 → 1.484 p = 0.88, Newton 1458 → 1466 p = 0.80). The tumbler takes the fallback path, so this audits the plumbing (the manager-side join, the accessor change), not the overlap.
- **Env-switch audit**: `=0` restores the wait at the end of `do_assemble` (the timeline shows SNH
  starting 324 µs *after* part 1 ends, 0 % coverage — `census_c2_head.txt`); `=2` is visible in
  the timeline as part 2 on stream 15 instead of 7; `=1` leaves part 2 on 7 and moves only the
  wait. `atoi` parses it; `=00` is the null arm.

### Transfer prediction

**Grid-size against machine size, and it grows** — the same category as s10/s11, with the same
caveat. The prize is idle SMs during contact part 1: 8 blocks on 40 SMs here (80 % idle), 8 on
