170 there (95 % idle), so a bigger part has *more* shadow to fill and the fraction of SNH that
lands in it can only rise. Two things pull the other way. Part 1's own duration is 10 563 FP64
instructions per thread at 1/32 rate here and 1/64 on GB202 (s12), so the window it opens is
shorter in absolute terms on a 5090 — but so is SNH, in the same ratio. And the +18 % slowdown of
part 1 under memory contention is a property of this box's 448 GB/s; on a 1.8 TB/s part it should
be smaller, which only helps. Best estimate for a rented 5090: **−1 to −2 % on case2, same sign,
same mechanism**, with `mas-bunny` still showing its 21-iteration signature.

### Limits, stated plainly

- **One scene carries the win.** case2 is the only FEM-only scene with a contact assembly worth
  hiding; `mas-bunny`'s −0.25 % is the same mechanism 21 times instead of 1661. The two ABD+FEM
  scenes take the fallback path by construction and cannot gain from this step at all.
- **The census is naive about concurrency**: `dur × (1 − fill)` per launch inside a window ignores
  that two launches may share the window, so the "761 µs idle" of mode 1 over-counts. It is used
  only as a within-run ratio (0 → 61 % coverage), never as a level.
- **Trace numbers are single 60-frame windows per arm**, used for mechanism only (s11 doctrine);
  every cross-arm level above is from the un-instrumented `abn.py` / `ab.py` sweeps.
- **The verify mode perturbs what it measures**: its scratch buffers grew 1–8 times per run
  (`reallocs`), and each growth is a `cudaFree` that synchronises the device. The "stale = 0"
  reading is therefore a lower bound on how often the reader would have been late; the 98 %
  "pending at host time" is unaffected.
- Mode 2 is a tie on case2 and was not measured on the other scenes.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **The remaining 43 % of the shadow is the 24 % of iterations where SNH never reaches part 1, and the fills + kinetic G/H that sit in front of it** | `census_c2_m1.txt`: SNH starts before part 1 ends in 76.2 % of iterations, median 248 µs early; in the other quarter the gap in front of SNH (the `-1` index fills at 8 µs × 7, the kinetic G/H at 22 µs, the strain-limiting shell at 157 µs, host launch latency) outlasts part 1. s26 already removed the value fill; the two index fills over the *whole* `triplet_A` (1.95 M triplets) remain and are the first thing on the default stream after the join point | case2; move the index fills to a side stream forked before the contact phase (they read nothing), or issue SNH first and the fills after |
| **Part 1 slows 18 % under SNH — memory-system contention, and it says where part 1's time goes** | `kmeans_c2.txt`: 878.7 → 1038.6 µs/launch with 8 resident blocks that no SNH block can share an SM with. The 8 blocks are latency-bound (one 255-register warp per SM); a 1249-block FP64 kernel on the other 32 SMs costs them 18 % through L2/DRAM alone. s08's static census has part 1 at LDL/STL 2 627 / 2 919 per thread; that local traffic is what SNH is competing with | a stack-frame reduction on part 1 (s08 took it 10 456 → 9 496 B; the 255-register wall is s09's open item) now has a *second* payoff: it shortens the window and removes the contention |
| **The friction reporter is a reader nobody has measured** | `read_set.txt`, row 12 of the consumer table: after `IPCSimplexNormalContact::do_assemble` returns, the manager's loop runs the frictional and half-plane reporters on the default stream; with the join deferred they now run inside part 1's shadow. None of the five scenes has both friction and a single receiver (rwb has no friction kernels at all, `census_rwb.txt`; the tumbler has friction but four receivers). A friction-heavy FEM-only scene would gain a second term this step could not measure | needs a scene; the cheapest is `cube-wall-cloth` with its ABD removed |
| **The multi-receiver path is where the tumbler and cube-wall-cloth live, and it still joins at the old point** | `compute_dytopo_effect` joins before the converter because `_convert_matrix` + `_distribute` read the collected buffers immediately (`global_dytopo_effect_manager.cu:241`), then does the blocking D2H s11 found (`:404` at that head). Both could be deferred the same way — run the converter on the side stream, and take the readback off the host path — and then the ABD+FEM scenes would see this step's mechanism too | s11's candidate 2 (the D2H removal) is the prerequisite; measure the gap between part 1's end and the converter's first kernel in an existing cwc trace first |
| **`mas-bunny` is a 0.25 % instrument for contact-assembly placement** | its 21 deferred joins per run reproduce the mechanism at a scale its ±0.1 % determinism resolves (−0.25 %, p = 0.0025, Newton 465 in all 16 runs). Round 5 and s09 called it vacuous for contact assembly; it is vacuous for *kernel-cost* changes to that assembly and a fine instrument for *placement* changes | use it as the second scene for any further contact-overlap step |
| **s12's and s13's open candidates are untouched** | `fast_segmental_reduce_matrix` (4.7 % of case2, 1157 µs/launch, `kmeans_c2.txt`), the MAS preconditioner family (26 % of `mas-bunny`), the capacity/nnz ratio of `bcoo_A` | as they wrote them |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`UIPC_BCOO_HASH` no longer measures what its comment says.** `global_linear_system.cu`
  documents the first conversion's hash as "a direct byte-level A/B of the whole assemble+convert
  pipeline between two runs" because the first conversion "depends only on the deterministic
  initial state". At this head two identical `mas-bunny` runs give **different first-conversion
  hashes** (`bcoo_hash_repeat.txt`, `nnz` identical). Something on the first assembly's path is
  order-dependent now — the round-4/5 converter changes or the s13 grid are the suspects — and any
  future step that reaches for this switch as a bit-identity proof will read a false negative.
- **A one-frame linear-solver stall on the shipped `stiff-gipc-case2` path, 1 run in 48.** The
  bit-identical null arm's run 11 spent **36 640 PCG iterations on frame 149** (7 Newton
  iterations; the sibling run's frame 149 took 325) and 440 on frame 111 (vs 245), for a run-level
  PCG of 101 830 against 63 000–65 500, at an unchanged Newton count. The solver appears to have run
  to its iteration cap several times on that frame. It is the scene's own non-determinism (the null
  arm executes today's code); the round has not seen it before because no earlier case2 sweep had
  48 runs. Worth a `UIPC_PCG_MAX_ITER`-style guard audit and a look at what frame 149 is.
- **`Timer` scopes are the only host work between the contact launch and the FEM assembly on the
  fast path** — `Timer{"Distribute Dytopo Effect"}`, `Timer{"Build Linear System"}`,
  `Timer{"Assemble Subsystems"}`; all no-ops unless `WB_TIMER=1`, when each one drains the device
  (s11). Under `WB_TIMER=1` this step is invisible by construction, as s10/s11 are.
- **`clang-format` is still not installed on this box** (s04–s13 recorded the same).

---

## s15 — the MAS local solve was not at a DRAM floor, it was at a memory-level-parallelism floor: ptxas unrolls its 16-column loop x2, and fully unrolling it is **−13.3 % / −11.2 % of the kernel** on `mas-bunny` / `stiff-gipc-case2`, bit-identical — **−1.94 % of `mas-bunny` end to end (n=10, disjoint, p=1.4e-12) and −1.14 % of `stiff-gipc-case2` (second sweep, n=8, disjoint, p=5.6e-4)**, both predicted from the share before they were measured

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s15/`.

**Ships default ON as `UIPC_MAS_ROWDOT2=16`; `UIPC_MAS_ROWDOT2=0` is the rollback and the A/B's old
arm, and it launches the round-4 s12 kernel whose SASS is byte-identical to main's.**

### The brief's premise, checked first — and the "never profiled" half is wrong

The brief says six rounds never profiled this family. Round 4's record says otherwise, in detail:
`2026-09-12-perf-round4.md` carries **four accepted steps on `mas_preconditioner_engine.cu`** (s08
warp-aggregated scatter, −85 % on that kernel; s09 register-column sweep inverse, −70 %; s12 the
atomic-free row-dot local solve itself, −14.6 %; s13 the R-tail fill fold) and **three recorded
rejections on exactly the kernel this brief nominates**: three relayouts of `schwarz_local_solve`
(staged / row-dot / row-dot-from-shared, all 28–29 µs), a "smaller inverse representation" (void —
the inverse is already the upper triangle, 136 blocks x 36 B = 4 896 B per cluster = the measured
6.27 MB), and the three-kernel fusion (needs two grid barriers; 686 blocks exceed the cooperative
cap). Round 4's closing note is that the kernel "sits at a ~28 µs floor that neither a traffic nor
a reduction lever moved", with sector accounting putting it at "~87 % of the DRAM roof".

Two of s13's (round 6) shapes were also already answered there. The **wave-quantisation** hypothesis
(686 blocks x 3 warps = 1.7 waves) is refuted by round 4's own clusters-per-block sweep — cpb 1 / 2 /
4 give 2.1 / 1.7 / 1.7 waves and read **28.30 / 27.94 / 27.91 µs**, i.e. the wave count does not
move the kernel. And `launch_spread` cannot apply: the grid is 686 x 96 on 40 SMs, not small
relative to the device, and `build_multi_level_R` / `collect_final_Z` are parallelism-limited
(80–81 blocks x 8 warps = 640 warps, exactly as s13 said).

What round 4 did *not* check is **what the compiler did with the loop** — and that is where the
floor was.

### The family, re-ranked at this head (`e0729b0a`; nsys `cuda_gpu_kern_sum`, full runs, fresh prefix)

| kernel | `mas-bunny` (100 f) | `stiff-gipc-case2` (250 f) |
|---|---|---|
| `Spmv_rbk_sym_spmv_dot_chunked` | 28.19 %, 51.75 µs x 35 210 | 16.57 %, 108.15 µs x 62 845 |
| **`MAS_schwarz_local_solve_rowdot`** | **15.43 %, 27.95 µs x 35 675**, grid 686x96 @ 39 reg | **8.39 %, 53.38 µs x 64 498**, 1522x96 |
| `StableNeoHookean3D` G/H | 10.54 %, 1 464 µs x 465 | 11.50 %, 2 855 µs x 1 653 |
| `MAS_build_multi_level_R` | 7.44 %, 13.47 µs, 80x256 @ 42 reg | 3.58 %, 22.78 µs, 178x256 |
| `MAS_collect_final_Z` | 3.20 %, 5.80 µs, 81x256 | 1.57 %, 9.96 µs, 180x256 |
| `MAS_invert_cluster_matrices_sweep` | 1.81 %, 251 µs x 465 | 2.13 %, 529 µs x 1 653 |
| `MAS_scatter_hessian_to_clusters_k1_agg` + `k2` | 1.37 + 0.99 % | 1.61 + 1.16 % |

MAS apply (the three kernels inside every PCG iteration) is **26.1 % of `mas-bunny` and 13.5 % of
case2**; the once-per-Newton assembly (scatter + invert) is another 4.2 % / 4.9 %. s13's figures
reproduce to 0.1 pp. On `rigid-wrecking-balls` the engine never runs (no FEM subsystem); on the
tumbler `rowdot` is 2.42 % (s00).

### What the SASS says the s12 kernel does — the finding

`cuobjdump -sass` of the shipped `schwarz_local_solve_rowdot_kernel` (`rowdot_s12_head.sass`): **120
instructions, 19 LDG, 4 FMUL + 8 FFMA + 2 FADD, one backward branch.** The 16-column loop is
**unrolled x2 by ptxas** (`IADD3 R11, R11, 0x2` / `ISETP.NE ... 0xf`), and inside each trip the
source's `if(c >= local_row)` — which is *divergent* within every warp, because the 32 lanes hold
~11 different rows — is compiled as two predicated arms: **12 matrix loads issued per trip for 6
live ones**, plus 6 residual loads. Per column the arithmetic is `FMUL, FFMA, FFMA` (the three-term
product) and one `FADD` into the accumulator.

So each thread waits on its loads eight times per launch, with at most ~12 loads in flight. The
"~87 % of the DRAM roof" reading was sector-based; the L2-resident probe below shows the kernel was
nowhere near a bandwidth floor.

### The sweep: one templated kernel, three shapes and a probe, measured per launch on `mas-bunny`

`rowdot2<UNROLL, STAGE, PROBE>` computes exactly the s12 expression per column and stores the same
scalar; what changes is the fetch. `UIPC_MAS_ROWDOT2=<unroll> + 100*stage + 1000*probe`.
Full 100-frame runs, one nsys session per mode (`rowdot_sweep_nsys.md`):

| shape | regs | µs/launch | vs s12 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| s12 kernel (head, `=0`) | 39 | **27.93** | — |
| u2, branch-free addressing (`=2`) | 32 | 27.76 | −0.6 % |
| u4 (`=4`) | 43 | 26.08 | −6.6 % |
| u8 (`=8`) | 63 | 24.36 | −12.8 % |
| **u16, fully unrolled (`=16`, ships)** | 72 | **24.23** | **−13.3 %** |
| u2 + residual staged in shared (`=102`) | 33 | 27.48 | −1.6 % |
| u4 + residual staged (`=104`) | 48 | 26.21 | −6.2 % |
| u8 / u16 + residual staged (`=108` / `=116`) | 72 / 78 | 24.18 / 24.30 | −13.4 / −13.0 % |
| matrix staged in shared (2 x 4 896 B, coalesced float4), u4 / u8 / u16 | 46 / 64 / 64 | 26.47 / 26.68 / 26.70 | −5 % (rejected) |
| the same with a load->store staging loop (7 serialised DRAM latencies per block) | 44–64 | 29.1–29.3 | **+4.5 %** (rejected) |
| **probe**: u2 reading an L2-resident matrix (`cluster_id & 63`) | 32 | **18.68** | timing only |
| **probe**: u4-staged reading an L2-resident matrix | 48 | 17.21 | timing only |

Read across: **branch-freedom alone is worth nothing (−0.6 %), staging the residual is worth
nothing, staging the matrix is a loss, and the unroll factor is the whole effect** — 27.9 → 26.1 →
24.4 → 24.2 µs for x2 → x4 → x8 → x16, saturating at x8. With all 48 matrix loads and 48 residual
loads of a thread issued up front (u16: 97 LDG, 2 branches, 72 registers) the kernel moves 6.27 MB in
24.2 µs = 259 GB/s. The probe puts the non-DRAM floor of the *u2* shape at 18.7 µs, so at u2 DRAM
accounted for only ~9 of 27.8 µs; the rest was exposed latency. The residual gap between u16
(24.2) and the DRAM floor (6.27 MB / ~365 GB/s ≈ 17 µs) is what is left, and the staged-matrix
result says a coalesced fetch does not buy it back on this part: the 10.3 KB of shared memory per
96-thread block caps residency (9 blocks/SM) and the barrier costs more than the ~700 → 77
wavefronts per block save.

The same on case2, full 250-frame runs: **53.35 → 47.76 (u8) / 47.37 µs (u16), −11.2 %**, grid
1522 x 96 unchanged.

Block count, block size and the `(blockIdx, threadIdx) -> output scalar` map are unchanged in every
row, so the s13 per-block profiler over-credit does not apply here.

### Numerics: bit-identical, proved on device against the shipped kernel on the same R

- **Arithmetic**: every instantiation compiles the column body to the s12 pattern — u16:
  16 FMUL + 32 FFMA + 16 FADD for 16 columns, i.e. `fma(m2, r2, fma(m1, r1, m0*r0))` then
  `acc + t`, in column order 0..15 (`sass_census.txt`, `rowdot2_u16.sass`).
- **Device comparison**: new probe mode `UIPC_MAS_APPLY_VERIFY=4` snapshots R and Z of the
  production apply, restores the *same* `multi_level_R` (the coarse tail has been zeroed by
  `collect_final_Z` since round 4's s13b — mode 3, which re-runs the restriction, measures that
  kernel's atomic noise, not the solve's; mode 4 exists because of that), re-runs the **s12 kernel**
  into Z and reports max|diff| over all 3 x clusters floats on device. Result, `NO_GRAPH=1`:
  **max|diff| = 0.000000e+00 on all 1 011 applies** — `mas-bunny` 490 (10 frames), case2 496 (5),
  cube-wall-cloth 12 (5), `tumbler-garments` 13 (3) — with R reported identical in the same probe
  (`verify/mode4_*.txt`).
- **Untouched kernels**: normalised SASS of `schwarz_local_solve_rowdot_kernel` (120 instrs),
  `build_multi_level_R_kernel` (1 200), `collect_final_Z_kernel` (216) and the old atomic
  `schwarz_local_solve_kernel` (448) in the final object is **identical** to the head object, so the
  `=0` arm is main's binary (the s01 lesson).
- **Counts**: `mas-bunny` Newton 465 in every run of every sweep and A/B below; PCG inside the
  35 185–35 240 baseline envelope in both arms (the SpMV's atomics make PCG non-reproducible in
  *either* arm; the preconditioner output itself is bit-identical, so it cannot be the cause).

### Performance, end to end — predicted first, then measured

Pre-registered in `predictions.txt` from the scope share: **`mas-bunny` −1.9…−2.0 %** (−3.70 µs x
35 675 launches = −2.05 % of kernel time on a GPU-bound scene), **case2 −0.9 %** (−5.98 µs x 65 887
= −0.96 % of kernel time), cube-wall-cloth −0.2…−0.3 % (rowdot ~1.8 % of its kernel time; inside the
scene's ±0.54 % null envelope, so "flat" is the expected reading).

`ab.py`, one build, ABBA, one discarded warm-up per arm, `UIPC_MAS_ROWDOT2=0` vs `=16`:

| scene | n/arm | mean ms/frame old -> u16 | delta | ms per Newton | ms per PCG it | Newton | PCG |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| **`mas-bunny`** | 10 | 66.5663 -> **65.2742** | **−1.94 %, t=17.4, p=1.4e-12, DISJOINT** | **−1.94 %, DISJOINT** | −1.93 %, DISJOINT | **465 in all 20 runs** | −0.01 % (35 180–35 235, inside the baseline envelope) |
| `stiff-gipc-case2`, sweep 1 | 8 | 163.0085 -> 162.2008 | −0.50 %, p=0.61, guard fired (PCG +1.68 %) | −0.46 % | −1.95 % | −0.04 % | one u16 run at **PCG 75 760** (all other 31 runs: 62 680–65 420) |
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`, sweep 2** (independent, same protocol) | 8 | 162.7126 -> **160.8657** | **−1.14 %, t=4.54, p=5.6e-4, DISJOINT** | **−1.14 %, t=5.07, p=2.8e-4, DISJOINT** | −0.59 %, p=0.14 | +0.01 % | −0.56 % |
| `stiff-gipc-case2`, pooled, all 32 runs | 16 | 162.8605 -> 161.5332 | −0.82 %, p=0.11 | −0.80 %, p=0.098 | −1.27 %, p=0.059 | −0.02 % | +0.56 % |
| `stiff-gipc-case2`, pooled without the PCG-excursion run | 16 / 15 | 162.8605 -> 160.8006 | **−1.26 %, t=6.98, p=1.3e-7, DISJOINT** | −1.23 %, p=1.2e-8 | −0.67 %, p=0.003 | −0.03 % | −0.60 % |
| `cube-wall-cloth` (control, rowdot ~1.8 %) | 6 | 58.3325 -> 57.3940 | −1.61 %, p=0.36, guard fired | **−0.49 %, p=0.14** | −0.57 %, p=0.59 | −1.12 % | −1.02 % |

(`ab_mb.txt`, `ab_c2.txt`, `ab_c2b.txt`, `pooled_c2.txt`, `ab_cwc.txt`; per-run values in `ab/*.json` and `pooled_c2.txt`.)

**`mas-bunny` lands on the prediction: −1.94 % against a predicted −1.9…−2.0 %, with the counts
that make the scene the round's precision instrument (Newton and line search 465 in every run,
PCG inside the baseline's own 35 185–35 240 envelope in both arms).** The scope delta carried to
the wall in full, which is what an unchanged block count should give (s13's per-block over-credit
does not arise here).

**case2 needed two sweeps, and the reason is recorded rather than tidied away.** The first sweep's
new arm contains one run whose PCG count is **75 760** where the other 31 runs of both arms sit in
62 680–65 420; it alone moves that arm's mean by +1.4 ms/frame and fires the harness's guard. The
preconditioner it ran is bit-identical to the old arm's (above), so it cannot have caused the
excursion; the SpMV's block-level `atomicAdd` into the dot makes this scene's PCG trajectory
non-reproducible in *either* arm (round 4's s12 row noted "its trajectory wanders run to run"), and
this is the excursion landing in the arm under test. An independent second sweep at the same n
reads **−1.14 % on both the mean and ms/Newton, disjoint, with Newton +0.01 % and PCG −0.56 %** —
the reading the prediction (−0.9 %) expected. Pooled over all 32 runs the mean is −0.82 % (p=0.11);
pooled without the excursion run it is −1.26 % (p=1.3e-7). The claim for case2 is **about −1 %**,
carried by the second sweep, not by the exclusion.

**`cube-wall-cloth` is flat, as predicted**: ms/Newton −0.49 % (p=0.14) against a predicted
−0.2…−0.3 % inside the scene's ±0.54 % envelope; its raw mean moved −1.6 % on a Newton count that
moved −1.1 %, which a bit-identical change cannot do — trajectory scatter, reported as unreadable by
the harness and as "flat" here.

`rigid-wrecking-balls` was not run as a control: the MAS engine launches nothing there (no FEM
subsystem; s12 in round 4 and s13 here both note it), so it would be the vacuous control §2.2 warns
about. `tumbler-garments` was not benchmarked: rowdot is 2.42 % of its kernel time (s00), the
predicted effect is −0.3 % against a scene that resolves ~4 %, and the identity proof already covers
its cluster population (13 applies at max|diff| = 0). A `--verify` audit has nothing to find that a
bit-identical preconditioner does not already settle — stated as a limit, as s13 did.

### Gates

- `gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, default arm: **identical counts** — common 11/3, core 1112/36,
  geometry 2730/46, sanity_check 100/3, regression 4/1, backend_cuda 448/23, sim_case **14213/95**,
  pytest 48 passed 1 skipped (`gate_default.txt`). The `=0` arm is the head binary for every device
  function it executes (SASS identity above), so its gate is s14's.
- `pgrep -af` clean at the end of the step.

### Transfer prediction

Category (PERF_METHOD §6): **algorithmic / latency — more loads in flight per thread, same bytes,
same launch geometry.** It is not wave quantisation (the block and wave counts are unchanged, and
round 4's cpb sweep shows the kernel is insensitive to them), not spill/stack (0 B stack in both),
and not an occupancy bound (no `__launch_bounds__`; registers 39 → 72 at 96-thread blocks, 10 → 9
resident blocks/SM, and u8 at 63 registers reads the same). What the change buys is proportional to
the latency x bandwidth product the kernel has to cover; a 5090 has roughly 4x the bandwidth and
similar DRAM latency, so the *absolute* µs saved per launch should shrink with the launch and the
*fraction* should stay of the same order. **Sign transfers; size is unpredictable from this box.**
One caveat: on a 96 MB-L2 part the 6.27 MB inverse is L2-resident across PCG iterations, which puts
the kernel in the probe's regime (18.7 → ? µs) where the unroll's share may be *larger*, not
smaller — the probe rows above are the number a rented run should compare against.

### Limits, stated plainly

- **The unroll floor is not the DRAM floor.** 24.2 µs against ~17 µs; the staged-matrix shape was
  the one coalescing attempt and it lost on residency. A shape that keeps 10+ blocks/SM *and*
  coalesces the `c >= row` arm would need a second copy of the inverse (round 4's s13 already priced
  that at 2x memory), so this is where the kernel stops on this part.
- **`build_multi_level_R` (7.4 %) and `collect_final_Z` (3.2 %) are untouched** — see candidates.
- Scope numbers are single sessions per mode; every cross-arm wall number is from the `ab.py` sweeps.
- The u8/u16 difference (24.36 vs 24.23; 47.76 vs 47.37) is inside single-session scatter; u16 ships
  because it is the shape with nothing left to schedule, not because it measured better.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **Fold the fine-level restriction into the local solve** (`build_multi_level_R` 13.5 µs = 7.4 % of `mas-bunny`, 3.6 % of case2) | Round 4's s13 measured that **54 % of `build_multi_level_R` is the fine-level part** (the FP64 gather of R through `part_to_real`, the `multi_lr` store, the 16-lane `cub::WarpReduce`) and that the kernel moves 0.77 MB in 13.3 µs — **latency-bound, ~2 µs of DRAM**. Its SASS here: 1 200 instructions, 31 LDG, 24 `ATOMG.ADD.F32` and **3 `ATOMS.CAST.SPIN`** — the shared-memory float `atomicAdd` on the elected lane is a compare-and-swap spin loop, serialised across the lanes of a bank. The fine restriction's inputs (r, `part_to_real`) are frozen before the apply and its fine output (`multi_lr`) is read only by the local solve, so a rowdot block can gather r for its own 16 nodes directly (the same `static_cast<float>` of the same doubles = the same R bits), stage them in shared memory (the `STAGE` variant above is the hook — a barrier the kernel can now afford), issue the coarse `going_next` atomics from one lane per cluster, and solve — hiding the restriction's dependent-load chain under the 6.27 MB stream. Round 4 rejected *the three-kernel fusion* on the grid-barrier argument; this is not that: the coarse clusters (81 of 1 281 on the bunny, 6 %) run as a second small launch of the same kernel with a cluster offset, so no grid barrier. Rounding-level on the coarse tail only (the shared atomics' order is already arbitrary; the existing mode-2 probe measures that noise at R 2.1e-6). Prize: up to ~10 of the 43 µs the three apply kernels cost per PCG iteration on the bunny | `mas-bunny` per launch + n>=10 wall; `UIPC_MAS_APPLY_VERIFY` modes 2/3 for the rounding level, with PCG counts |
| **`collect_final_Z` into `fused_dot`** (5.8 µs = 3.2 % of `mas-bunny`) | 81 blocks x 256 = 640 warps, 26 LDG per thread, latency-bound like the restriction. `fused_dot` reads z and r right after it; it could gather `multi_lz` + the coarse `LevelTable` itself and write z as a side effect. Crosses into `linear_system/` and couples the generic PCG to the MAS engine, so it is a design question first | `mas-bunny` |
| **`fast_segmental_reduce_matrix`** | still 3.5 % of `mas-bunny` (490 µs x 465) and 4.8 % of case2 (1 180 µs x 1 653); nominated by s12 and s13, still untouched | case2 |
| ~~`schwarz_local_solve_rowdot` unroll / fetch shape~~ | **done — this step.** What is left in it is the 24 → 17 µs gap to the DRAM floor, and the one coalescing shape tried lost on residency | — |
| ~~`launch_spread` on the MAS grids~~ | **refuted at this head**: rowdot is 686/1 522 blocks on 40 SMs (not small), `build_multi_level_R` / `collect_final_Z` are 640 warps at 8 per block (parallelism-limited, as s13 said). No call site to wire | — |
| **The `*converged` read at the top of every apply kernel** | every one of the three MAS kernels and the SpMV starts with a dependent global load of the device flag before any work — one L2 round trip (~0.5 µs) x 4 per PCG iteration on a ~120 µs iteration. Only removable by restructuring the captured graph's early-exit (a conditional node, or host-side check every k iterations); measure the round-trip first with a probe that hoists the read | `mas-bunny` |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`build_multi_level_R`'s else-branch has a latent intra-warp race**, and it is the one V1's
  `racecheck` flagged 50 times: lanes zero `sum_residual(threadIdx.x)`, `atomicAdd` into the elected
  lane's slot, and the elected lane reads its slot back — with no `__syncwarp` between the three,
  under independent thread scheduling. In situ it is benign (the s12/s18 probes read the path's
  noise at R 2.1e-6, the same as its atomic-order noise), but the candidate above replaces the
  shared atomics with a `__match_any_sync` peer reduction and would remove it.
- **The two verify probes (`UIPC_MAS_APPLY_VERIFY` modes 1–3) cannot prove the local solve alone**,
  because they re-run the restriction and inherit its coarse-tail atomic noise; mode 4 is the one
  to use for any future local-solve change.
- **`clang-format` is still not installed on this box** (s04–s14 recorded the same).

## s16 — the fine-level restriction folded into the local solve: the three MAS apply kernels go from **43.5 to 36.6 µs per PCG iteration** on `mas-bunny` (**−15.9 %**) and 80.1 → 66.7 on case2, the fine Z bit-identical and the coarse tail at the old path's own noise — **−4.29 % of `mas-bunny` end to end (n=10, disjoint, p=1.3e-23, Newton 465 in every run) and −2.36 % ms/Newton of `stiff-gipc-case2` (n=8, disjoint, p=5.5e-8)**, both above the prediction; and the shape round 4 rejected is not the shape that shipped

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s16/`.

**Ships default ON as `UIPC_MAS_FUSED_R=2`; `=0` is the rollback and the A/B's old arm (it launches
`build_multi_level_R` + `rowdot2<16>`, both SASS-identical to main). `=1` and `=3` are the two
measured-and-rejected orderings, kept as probes.**

### Why this is not round 4's rejected fusion — checked before anything was built

Round 4's s13 rejected fusing `build_multi_level_R` + `schwarz_local_solve` + `collect_final_Z` into
**one** kernel: the restriction must finish for *all* fine nodes before any coarse cluster is solved,
and all levels must be solved before any fine node is collected — two grid-wide barriers, a
cooperative launch capped at ≤ 640 resident blocks on this part against a 686-block (bunny) /
1 522-block (case2) local solve, and a prize s13 sized at ~1.3 µs of removed round-trips. Every one
of those premises still holds at this head and none of them applies to this step:

- **No grid barrier.** The only dependency the fine restriction has is *forward*: its fine output
  (`multi_lr[0, map_nodes)`) has exactly one reader, the fine clusters' own local solve, which sits in
  the same block; and its coarse output (`multi_lr[map_nodes, total)`) is an atomic accumulator that
  only the *coarse* clusters read. So the fine clusters (1 280 of 1 372 on the bunny, 93 %) restrict
  and solve in one launch, and the coarse clusters (92) are a second launch of the same kernel with a
  scalar offset. The launch boundary is the barrier. `collect_final_Z` is untouched.
- **Same launch count** (3 per apply), so s11's 0.16–0.22 µs inter-node gaps are neutral, and no
  cooperative-launch cap enters.
- **A different prize.** s13 counted only the removed `multi_level_R` round-trip (~0.5 µs). The prize
  here is the *kernel* that produced it: round 4's s12 measured `build_multi_level_R` at 13.3 µs for
  0.77 MB — a ~4x gap to its DRAM floor on 640 warps — with a probe that deletes its coarse loop at
  7.2 µs. Folding the fine part into a 2 058-warp, DRAM-streaming kernel hides a latency-bound
  kernel under a bandwidth-bound one; s15's candidate sized it at "up to ~10 of the 43 µs".

The brief's second premise — that the fine `multi_lr` entries are read only by the local solve — was
checked by grep before building: `multi_level_R` is written by `build_multi_level_R`, read by the three
local-solve kernels, tail-zeroed by `collect_final_Z` (s13b) and `set_preconditioner`, and snapshotted
by the verify probe. Nothing else. `part_map_size` is a sum of bank-padded partition sizes
(`fem_mas_preconditioner.cu:333`), so the fine region is 16-aligned and level 1 begins at
`bank_align(max(padded_N, total_nodes))` ≥ `map_nodes` — the coarse launch covers exactly the
`[map_nodes, total)` range the old kernel solved with zero R, unchanged.

### What the fused kernel does (`fused_R_kernel<RESTRICT, ORDER>`, 96 threads = 2 clusters)

1. Thread `(row, comp)` gathers `float(R[3 * part_to_real(node) + comp])` — the same double, the
   same cast, the same bits as the old kernel — into a `float4 s_rv[32]`, and thread `(row, 0)`
   parks the real id in `s_idx[32]`.
2. Every thread issues its 48 matrix loads into registers **before** the barrier, so their DRAM
   latency covers the restriction.
3. `__syncthreads()`; all live threads run the rowdot2<16> column body against `s_rv` (the s15
   `STAGE` variant's hook — the barrier the kernel can now afford) and store their scalar.
4. **Warp 0** (lane `t` = fine node `t` of the block, 2 banks of 16) applies the old aggregation
   rule — a bank whose `prefix_orig` is 1 is one aggregate pushed by lane 0; otherwise each connected
   component (`fine_conn`, transitively closed and self-inclusive by construction in
   `prepare_prefix_sum_L0`) is pushed by its lowest lane — with **16 × 3 `__shfl_sync` in a fixed
   increasing-lane order** instead of shared-memory float atomics, then walks the `going_next` chain
   and issues the 3 × (levels − 1) global `atomicAdd`s. No shared atomics anywhere: the SASS has
   0 `ATOMS`, 48 `SHFL`, 1 `BAR`.

The coarse launch (`RESTRICT = false`) is the rowdot2<16> body with an offset: 456 instructions,
97 LDG, 72 registers — the same census as the shipped rowdot2<16>.

### The ordering sweep, measured per launch on `mas-bunny` (`nsys/apply_sweep.md`)

| `UIPC_MAS_FUSED_R` | fine launch (640 × 96) | regs | coarse (46 × 96) | collect | apply total | vs old |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `=0` old: `build_R` 13.45 + `rowdot2` 24.23 | — | 42 / 72 | — | 5.84 | **43.51** | — |
| `=1` push before the row-dots | 28.66 | 118 | 4.10 | 5.95 | 38.71 | −11.0 % |
| **`=2` push after the row-dots (ships)** | **26.42** | **78** | 4.17 | 6.02 | **36.61** | **−15.9 %** |
| `=3` chain walked into shared memory before the barrier, push after | 31.62 | 92 | 4.13 | 5.85 | 41.60 | −4.4 % |
| (`=2`, first build, before the lambda refactor) | 27.04 | 72 | 4.13 | 6.15 | 37.31 | −14.2 % |

case2 (250 f): old 80.10 (22.79 + 47.39 + 9.93); `=1` 69.38; **`=2` 66.71 (fine 50.91, coarse 5.83,
−16.7 %)**; `=3` 73.66.

Two readings. **Register pressure across the barrier, not the chain's latency, is the lever**: keeping
the 48 matrix operands live while warp 0 walks the chain (`=1`, 118 registers, 5 resident blocks/SM)
costs 2.2 µs against pushing after the FMAs when they are dead (`=2`). And **the prefetch shape is
refuted**: moving the chain in front of the barrier makes warps 1–2 wait on warp 0's five dependent
loads before *any* FMA can start, and its 92 registers drop another resident block; it is the worst
of the three despite doing the least after the solve. Whole-run kernel time: bunny 6 318 → 6 052 ms
(−4.2 %), case2 41 191 → 40 191 ms (−2.4 %) at flat PCG counts.

**Where the "up to ~10 µs" went**: 6.9 µs realised. The fine launch costs 2.2 µs more than rowdot2
(the gather, the barrier, the push), the coarse launch is a 46-block kernel at its ~4 µs latency floor
(two dependent loads and the `*converged` read, for 450 KB of matrix), and `collect_final_Z` reads
+0.2 µs after a short kernel instead of a long one.

### Numerics: fine Z bit-identical on device, coarse tail at the old path's own noise (`verify/`)

New probe mode **`UIPC_MAS_APPLY_VERIFY=5`**: snapshot the production apply's R and Z between the solve
and `collect_final_Z` (the s15 hook), then re-run **the two-kernel path** (`build_multi_level_R` + the
s12 row-dot kernel) on a zeroed R, and compare **per region** — the fused path never writes the fine
R entries, so the comparison is `Z_fine`, `Z_coarse`, `R_coarse`. Mode 2 (same path re-run) now
reports the same split when the fused path is on. Modes 1/3/4 force the two-kernel path (they compare
a local solve against the production R), so s15's mode-4 instrument still reads 0 under the new
default (`v4_mb_default.txt`, 490 applies, 0 fused launches).

| run (`NO_GRAPH=1`) | applies | `Z_fine` max&#124;diff&#124; | `Z_coarse` rel | `R_coarse` rel |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **mode 5, bunny, `=2`** (fused vs old) | 490 | **0** | 8.7e-6 | 2.6e-6 |
| mode 5, bunny, `=1` / `=3` | 490 / 490 | **0** / **0** | 6.4e-6 / 9.0e-6 | 3.1e-6 / 2.2e-6 |
| mode 5, case2 / cube-wall-cloth / tumbler | 497 / 12 / 13 | **0** / **0** / **0** | 4.6e-6 / 3.2e-7 / 1.1e-5 | 1.8e-6 / 6.1e-7 / 7.3e-7 |
| mode 2, **old path** vs itself (its own atomic-order noise) | 490 | — (whole Z 4.5e-6) | — | whole R 2.9e-6 |
| mode 2, fused `=2` / `=3` vs itself | 490 / 490 | **0** / **0** | 4.4e-6 / 4.9e-6 | 1.4e-6 / 1.8e-6 |

So: **1 992 applies on four scenes with `Z_fine` exactly zero**, and the fused-vs-old coarse
difference (R 2.6e-6, Z 8.7e-6) sits at the old path's own re-run noise (R 2.9e-6, Z 4.5e-6) — the
coarse tail is an atomic accumulator in both, and this step also fixes the in-bank sum's order
(increasing lane) where the old shared atomics had none. The arithmetic evidence behind the zero: the
fused kernel's column body is 16 FMUL + 32 FFMA + 16 FADD, rowdot2<16>'s census (`sass_census.txt`);
the 48 extra FADD are the predicated shuffle sums.

**Untouched kernels**: normalised SASS of `build_multi_level_R_kernel` (1 200), `rowdot2<16>` (456),
`rowdot` (120), `collect_final_Z_kernel` (216) and the old atomic `schwarz_local_solve_kernel` (448) in
the final object is **identical** to the head object — after one false start: an alias line added at
the top of the file shifted the old kernel's `UIPC_KERNEL_ASSERT` `__LINE__` literal (0x519 → 0x51a),
caught by the diff and undone. The `=0` arm is main's binary.

**Counts**: bunny Newton 465 and line search 465 in all 20 A/B runs, PCG 35 175–35 240 (old) /
35 185–35 225 (new), inside the baseline envelope; case2 Newton −0.02 %, PCG −0.52 % (63 255–65 195, no
excursion this time), line search −0.01 %.

### Performance, end to end — predicted first (`predictions.txt`), then measured

Predicted from the scope share, with s13's caution stated in advance — the block count per apply
changes (80 + 686 → 640 + 46) and CUPTI charges per block, so the `kern_sum` delta (−3.9 % of kernel
time on the bunny, −2.15 % on case2) was written down as a **ceiling**: bunny −3.0…−3.9 %, case2
−1.7…−2.2 % ms/Newton, cube-wall-cloth flat (−0.2…−0.5 %, inside its ±0.54 % envelope).

`ab.py`, one build, ABBA, one discarded warm-up per arm, `UIPC_MAS_FUSED_R=0` vs `=2`:

| scene | n/arm | mean ms/frame old → fused | delta | ms per Newton | ms per PCG it | Newton | PCG |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| **`mas-bunny`** | 10 | 65.2289 → **62.4313** | **−4.29 %, t=79.3, p=1.3e-23, DISJOINT** | **−4.29 %, DISJOINT** | −4.30 %, DISJOINT | **465 in all 20 runs** | +0.01 % |
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`** | 8 | 161.1814 → **157.3440** | **−2.38 %, t=10.3, p=6.9e-8, DISJOINT** | **−2.36 %, t=10.5, p=5.5e-8, DISJOINT** | −1.87 %, p=8.8e-6 | −0.02 % | −0.52 % |
| `cube-wall-cloth` (control, MAS apply ~2.9 %) | 6 | 56.4760 → 56.3046 | −0.30 %, p=0.83 | **−0.36 %, p=0.52** | +0.09 % | +0.07 % | −0.40 % |

(`ab_mb.txt`, `ab_c2.txt`, `ab_cwc.txt`; per-run values in `ab/*/`.)

**Both targeted scenes land above the prediction, not below it.** The `kern_sum` ceiling was
−3.9 % on the bunny and the wall reads −4.29 %; the per-session whole-run kernel sums (−4.2 %) agree
with the wall better than the per-apply arithmetic does, i.e. the block-count caution did not bite
here — the small kernel that was removed (80 blocks) was not the one CUPTI was over-crediting, the
one it charges per block is the 640/686-block solve whose count barely moved. The reading of the
guard is the strong one: with the fine preconditioner output bit-identical and the coarse tail at the
old path's own noise, the identical Newton / line-search counts and the flat PCG counts are exactly
what a change that computes nothing differently should give.

`cube-wall-cloth` is flat as predicted (−0.36 % ms/Newton, p=0.52). `rigid-wrecking-balls` was not run:
the MAS engine launches nothing there (vacuous control, as s15 and round 4's s12 note). The tumbler was
not benchmarked: the MAS apply is 2.4 % of its kernel time, the predicted effect (~−0.4 %) is an order
of magnitude below what the scene resolves, and its cluster population is covered by the mode-5
identity (13 applies, `Z_fine` = 0). No `--verify` audit was run for the same reason s13 and s15 gave.

### Gates

- `gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, default arm (`=2`): **identical counts** — common 11/3, core
  1112/36, geometry 2730/46, sanity_check 100/3, regression 4/1, backend_cuda 448/23, sim_case
  **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped (`gate_default.txt`, also `/workspace/output/round6/gate_after_s16.txt`).
  The `=0` arm executes the head binary's device functions (SASS identity above) and the untouched
  host branch, so its gate is s15's.
- `pgrep -af` clean at the end of the step; tree returned to `main`, `build-perf` rebuilt from main.

### The race: gone from the shipped path, still present in the `=0` arm

s15 found that `build_multi_level_R`'s else-branch zeroes `sum_residual(threadIdx.x)`, shared-`atomicAdd`s
into the elected lane's slot and reads it back with no `__syncwarp` between the three — the race V1's
`racecheck` flagged 50 times. **The default path no longer runs that code**: the fused kernel's in-bank
aggregation is a shuffle reduction over registers (0 `ATOMS` in its SASS), so under `UIPC_MAS_FUSED_R=2`
the racy kernel is never launched. It is *not* fixed in `build_multi_level_R_kernel` itself, deliberately:
that kernel is this step's A/B arm and its probe reference, and it had to stay byte-identical to main.
The fix there is two `__syncwarp()` calls (after the zeroing stores, after the atomics) — a separate
minimal commit for the validation pass or the next agent, once the arm is no longer needed as a
reference; it changes nothing for the shipped path.

### Transfer prediction

Category (PERF_METHOD §6): **algorithmic / latency — a latency-bound kernel (13.4 µs for 0.77 MB on
640 warps) absorbed into a bandwidth-bound stream (6.27 MB on 2 058 warps), plus one 46-block launch
at a ~4 µs latency floor.** Not wave quantisation (the fine launch is 640 blocks of the same 96-thread
shape rowdot2 had; round 4's cpb sweep shows the kernel insensitive to it), not spill (0 B local in
every instantiation; the 112 B stack is the assert's printf frame, as in the old kernel), and the
occupancy component is measured and adverse-to-none (78 vs 72 registers cost nothing: 26.4 vs 27.0 µs).
**Sign transfers.** Size: the win is `build_R(target) − coarse_launch(target) − fine_overhead`, and
the coarse launch is a fixed latency cost of ~3–4 µs that does not shrink with bandwidth; on a part
where `rowdot2` runs at ~7 µs and `build_R` at ~7 µs the step is still positive but a smaller
fraction, and if `build_R` there were below ~5 µs it would be a wash. The number a rented run should
compare is the three-kernel apply total per PCG iteration in both arms — `nsys/apply_sweep.md` is
the reference table. A 96 MB-L2 part also makes the local solve L2-resident (s15's probe regime),
which makes it *more* latency-bound and the fold correspondingly more, not less, useful.

### Limits, stated plainly

- Scope numbers are single sessions per mode; the cross-arm claim rests on the `ab.py` sweeps.
- The coarse launch is 4.2 µs of the 36.6 — a latency floor for 46 blocks. A "last fine block
  solves the coarse clusters" shape (threadfence + counter, no grid sync) would remove the launch but
  serialise 450 KB of matrix through one block; not attempted, sized as a loss (see candidates).
- The `=3` result says the chain's latency is not on the block's critical path; what the fine
  launch's remaining +2.2 µs over rowdot2 is made of (gather, barrier, shuffle sum, push) was not
  attributed separately.
- `clang-format` is still not installed on this box; the style was kept by hand.

Crosses into `linear_system/` — s15's design caveat stands | `mas-bunny` |
| **The coarse launch's 4.2 µs** (46 blocks × 96 on the bunny, 101 on case2) | a 450 KB kernel at a latency floor: `*converged` read → 48 loads → 16 FMAs → store. Removing the `*converged` read alone (s15's candidate, one L2 round trip per kernel) is worth ~0.5 µs here and in each of the other two apply kernels; folding the coarse solve into `collect_final_Z`'s launch is a design question (collect needs coarse Z) | `mas-bunny` per launch |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **`m_active_level_num` is honoured by `collect_final_Z` but not by the restriction** — both
  `build_multi_level_R` and the fused kernel push to all `m_level_num − 1` coarse levels regardless.
  Harmless today (nothing sets it below the full hierarchy; the un-collected coarse Z is simply
  unused work), but a level cap would not save the restriction's atomics.
- **The fine entries of `multi_level_R` are dead storage on the default path** (`map_nodes` × 12 B,
  245 KB on the bunny); they are kept because the `=0` arm and probe modes 1/3/4 write and read them.
- The `ab.py` harness worked unchanged: no excursion run this time on case2 (all 16 runs inside
  63 255–65 195), so the s15 pooled-with-and-without reporting was not needed.

## s17 — the converter's segmented reduce ran five warp-tree levels for every warp when its segments average 6.5 elements: stopping at the first level no lane needs is **−22.4 % / −24.8 % of the kernel** on `stiff-gipc-case2` / `mas-bunny`, bit-identical for every slot the kernel stores — **−0.95 % of `mas-bunny` end to end (n=10, disjoint, p=2.5e-10, Newton 465 in every run) and **−1.10 % ms/Newton (n=16 over two independent sweeps, t=8.5, p=2.2e-9; each sweep −1.1 %, the second disjoint)** of `stiff-gipc-case2`**, both predicted from the share; and the probes put a **5.9 % ceiling** on everything a re-summation could still take from this kernel

Evidence: `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-s17/`.

**Ships default ON as `UIPC_SEG_REDUCE2=1`; `UIPC_SEG_REDUCE2=0` launches the round-4 (s10) kernel, whose
SASS is byte-identical to main's — the A/B arm and the rollback.** Diagnostics (all default off):
`UIPC_SEG_VERIFY=1` (device-side bit comparison against the old kernel, per slot class),
`UIPC_SEG_PROBE=1` (two timing stubs into scratch), `UIPC_SEG_HIST=k` (per-warp tree-level histogram).

### The brief's two premises, checked first — one holds, one is not what the code does

- **"4.8 % of case2, ~3.5 % of `mas-bunny`, untouched"** — holds at this head (`4181f28b`, nsys
  `cuda_gpu_kern_sum`, full runs, fresh prefix): `fast_segmental_reduce_matrix_kernel` is **4.88 % of
  case2** (1960 ms, 1664 launches x 1177.9 µs, 14 699–15 706 blocks x 128 at 46 registers, the
  fifth-largest kernel in the scene) and **3.76 % of `mas-bunny`** (489.2 µs x 465). The converter chain
  around it, re-ranked: the CUB radix sort **2.94 % / 2.68 %** (709 µs per convert on case2 — larger than
  every other converter kernel combined), `fused_compact_keys_k2` 0.61 / 0.46 %, the sorted-key unpack
  0.35 / 0.26 %, `zero_cross_warp` 0.27 / 0.14 %, `fused_flag_upper_k1` 0.25 / 0.18 %, scans + RLE ~0.45 /
  0.3 %; the chain totals **~10.2 % of case2 and ~8.0 % of `mas-bunny`** (`scope.md`, `nsys/head_*.csv`).
- **"s13 found the converter uses the destination arrays as sort scratch (`matrix_converter.inl:485`)"** —
  not what the line does. At s13's head line 485 is `to.resize_triplets_discard(from.triplet_count())` in
  the non-fused `convert()`, and the default fused `convert_sym` does the same at its step 5
  (`to.resize_triplets_discard(m)`): the destination is *resized* to the raw upper-triangular count before
  the RLE shrinks it to `h_count`, and **nothing writes `m` entries into it** — every sort buffer
  (`ij_hash*`, `sort_index*`, `ij_pairs`, `unique_*`, `sorted_partition_*`) is the converter's own. So
  `bcoo_A`'s capacity is coupled to the raw count by a *resize ordering*, not by a scratch dependency, and
  the decoupling is two lines (resize after the RLE; reserve `bcoo_A` from a reduced high-water mark),
  not a new scratch allocation. **Decided by measurement, not built**: `run_benchmark.py` records case2's
  peak at **1968 MiB of 8192** (`baseline-runs/stiff-gipc-case2.json`, `gpuMemory.peakTotalMiB`), so the
  ~180 MB it would free (2.65 M slots x 80 B against 286 k x 1.5) is not binding on this box, and it has
  no kernel-time effect at all now that s13's fitted grid already removes the idle SpMV blocks. It stays a
  footprint candidate (below), and the kernel was the better target.

### The finding, from the SASS and a histogram, before any code

`cuobjdump -sass` of the shipped kernel (`sass/census_gls.txt`): **392 instructions, 50 DADD, 95 SHFL, 13 LDG**,
2 branches. The nine `cub::WarpReduce::HeadSegmentedReduce` calls each run **five fixed shuffle levels**
(offsets 1, 2, 4, 8, 16) and at each level every lane executes `if(lane + offset <= last_lane) v = other + v`
— the add is *predicated per lane*, so a level at which no lane of the warp qualifies still costs the warp
one FP64 instruction per matrix entry, 9 x ~16 clocks on a 1/32-rate part. The predicate is monotone in
the offset (a lane that fails at 2^d fails at 2^(d+1)), so the useful trip count of a warp is
`ceil(log2(longest in-warp run))`, and the sorted input's runs average **6.5 elements** (case2: in = 1.86 M
per launch, out = 286 k; bunny 819 k / 129 k).

`UIPC_SEG_HIST` counts the warps of a launch by that trip count (`verify/new_vs_old_*.txt`):

| scene | in / out per launch | warps | levels 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 | mean levels | FP64 removable, bit-identically |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|
| `stiff-gipc-case2` | 1 856 397 / 286 419 | 58 016 | 0 / 0 / 0 / 18 381 / 23 014 / 16 621 | **3.97** | 20.6 % |
| `mas-bunny` | 818 543 / 128 736 | 25 580 | 0 / 0 / 0 / 8 463 / 10 458 / 6 659 | **3.93** | 21.4 % |
| `tumbler-garments` | 390 652 / 55 350 | 12 208 | 0 / 0 / 0 / 2 629 / 6 247 / 3 332 | 4.06 | 18.8 % |
| `cube-wall-cloth` | 194 177 / 48 001 | 6 072 | 600 / 0 / 0 / 1 612 / 1 622 / 2 238 | 3.71 | 25.8 % |
| `rigid-wrecking-balls` | 5 740 / 5 740 | 180 | **179** / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 1 | 0.03 | 99 % — **no duplicate triplets at all** (the ABD pair pre-reduction of round 4's s03 leaves nothing to sum) |

### The change (`fast_segmental_reduce_matrix_k2_kernel<..., Probe>`)

Same prologue as the old kernel (three key reads, the 72-byte gather through the permuted value functor,
the head / cross-warp flags, the int flag reduce), then `last_lane` computed exactly as cub's
`SegmentedReduce<HEAD_SEGMENTED>` computes it (ballot of the head flags, shifted to tail flags, masked to
lanes >= the caller, the warp's last lane forced in, `__clz(__brev(...))`), and the tree as an unrolled
five-step loop that **breaks at the first step where `__any_sync(lane + offset <= last_lane)` is false**.
The per-lane add is `value = op(other, value)` with `other = __shfl_down_sync(full, value, offset)` — the
generic-op path cub takes for `::cuda::std::plus<double>` (its `add.f64` asm specialisation is for
`cub::Sum` only), so every lane executes the same adds on the same operands in the same order as before and
simply stops issuing the ones cub would have predicated off. Stores and atomics are unchanged. The old
kernel is untouched (not templated over, not moved); the k2 kernel is dispatched beside it, and
`FastSegmentalReduce<64, 32>` / the scalar reduce keep the old path (the tree assumes a full physical warp).

Binary: k2 is 1 359 static instructions (the unrolled loop's five exit paths duplicate code: 94 static
DADD for the same 45 per-lane maximum), **48 registers vs 46 — the same 8-register allocation granule at
128-thread blocks, so the same occupancy**, 0 B stack in both. **All 57 / 63 / 38 pre-existing functions in
the three TUs that instantiate this header (`global_linear_system.cu`, `global_dytopo_effect_manager.cu`,
`external_articulation_constraint.cu`) are SASS-identical to the head object** (`sass_identity.txt`,
normalised per function; 29 head-only functions are the k2 / probe / verify / histogram kernels). The
`=0` arm is main's binary.

### Numerics: bit-identical where the old kernel stores, at the old path's own noise where it accumulates — proved on device

`UIPC_SEG_VERIFY=1` runs, after every production launch, the **old** kernel into a zeroed scratch copy and
compares every 64-bit word, splitting the slots into two classes computed from the keys: a segment that
spans **>= 3 warps** takes >= 3 atomic operands in *both* kernels and its result depends on arrival order
(2-warp segments are `a + b`, commutative, exact); everything else must match bit for bit.

| run (short, all scenes) | calls | words compared | mismatching words | of which in >= 3-warp slots | **elsewhere** | multi-warp slots | max rel |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **new vs old, `stiff-gipc-case2`** | 14 | 36 093 312 | 8 443 | 8 443 | **0** | 6 390 | 2.1e-14 |
| **new vs old, `mas-bunny`** | 12 | 13 903 488 | 3 198 | 3 198 | **0** | 2 352 | 3.8e-14 |
| new vs old, `cube-wall-cloth` / rwb / tumbler | 9 / 7 / 8 | 3 888 081 / 361 620 / 3 985 200 | **0 / 0 / 0** | — | **0** | 0 | 0 |
| *control:* **old vs old**, case2 (`UIPC_SEG_REDUCE2=0`) | 14 | 36 093 312 | 8 109 | 8 109 | **0** | 6 390 | 4.1e-13 |
| *control:* old vs old, `mas-bunny` | 12 | 13 903 488 | 2 962 | 2 962 | **0** | 2 352 | 5.1e-14 |

**0 mismatching words outside the arrival-order class over 58 M compared words on five scenes, and inside
it the new-vs-old count (8 443 / 3 198) is the old-vs-old count (8 109 / 2 962)** — the same class, the
same size, the same magnitude (round 4's s10 verify measured the same class at 22 466 words per 12 case2
frames). The kernel computes the same values as main's; the matrix's residual non-determinism is the
pre-existing cross-warp atomic order, present in both arms.

Counts: `mas-bunny` Newton 465 and line search 465 in all 20 A/B runs, PCG 35 185–35 245 (inside the
baseline's 35 185–35 240 envelope in both arms); case2 Newton −0.16 % / PCG −0.18 % (sweep 1),
Newton −0.36 % / PCG −0.19 % (sweep 2), no PCG excursion in either sweep.

### Scope: one build, env switch, full runs, identical launch geometry (`scope.md`)

| scene | old (cub tree) | **k2** | delta | k2 Probe=1, no tree | k2 Probe=2, no gather |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`** (250 f, 1665 launches) | 1179.3 µs | **915.0 µs** | **−22.4 %** | 861.8 | 803.7 |
| **`mas-bunny`** (100 f, 465) | 488.4 µs | **367.4 µs** | **−24.8 %** | 361.2 | 318.7 |

Block count, block size and the `(blockIdx, threadIdx) -> element` map are unchanged, so s13's per-block
CUPTI over-credit does not apply; the untouched SpMV / SNH / MAS kernels read within 0.3 % between the
sessions (`scope.md`). Whole-run kernel time −1.24 % (case2) / −1.11 % (bunny) at flat counts.

**The probes bracket what is left, and it is not much.** With the *entire* remaining tree deleted (Probe=1)
the kernel reads 861.8 / 361.2 µs against the production 916.0 / 369.9 — **54 µs = 5.9 % on case2, 9 µs
= 2.4 % on the bunny** — and with the gather deleted instead (Probe=2) 803.7 / 318.7. Read together: the
tree (~700 µs) and the random 72-byte gather (~760 µs) now cost about the same and overlap almost
completely; the old kernel's five-level tree (~880 µs) was the one that stuck out. **That 5.9 % is the
ceiling for any rounding-level re-summation of this kernel** (K elements per thread with in-thread
pre-adds, a serial per-output loop, ...), which is why none was built: the bit-identical stop took ~83 %
of what the tree can give, and the remainder is worth ~0.3 % of case2 at the cost of a changed summation
order on the system matrix. The kernel is now gather-bound.

### Performance, end to end — predicted first (`predictions.txt`), then measured

Predicted from the share before the sweeps: **`mas-bunny` −0.8…−1.0 %** (−0.93 % of kernel time on a
GPU-bound scene), **case2 −1.0…−1.3 % ms/Newton** (−1.10 % of kernel time), cube-wall-cloth flat (the
launch is a tenth of case2's, well under 1 % of that scene), rwb 0 (the kernel is ~5 µs there — but it
*does* execute, tree skipped, so it is a real control), tumbler not run (2.50 % share per s00, −0.55 %
predicted against a ~4 % resolution).

`ab.py`, one build, ABBA, one discarded warm-up per arm, `UIPC_SEG_REDUCE2=0` vs `=1`
(`ab/ab_*.txt`, per-run json in `ab/<scene>/`):

| scene | n/arm | mean ms/frame old → new | delta | ms per Newton | ms per PCG | Newton | PCG |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| **`mas-bunny`** | 10 | 62.4494 → **61.8565** | **−0.95 %, t=12.6, p=2.5e-10, DISJOINT** | **−0.95 %, DISJOINT** | −0.96 %, DISJOINT | **465 in all 20 runs** | +0.01 % (35 185–35 245) |
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`, sweep 1** | 8 | 157.1009 → 155.1061 | −1.27 %, t=5.05, p=1.8e-4, overlapping | **−1.11 %, t=5.9, p=4.1e-5** | −1.10 %, p=1.4e-3 | −0.16 % | −0.18 % |
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`, sweep 2** (independent, same protocol) | 8 | 157.2027 → **154.9423** | **−1.44 %, t=6.21, p=2.3e-5, DISJOINT** | **−1.08 %, t=5.67, p=6.3e-5, DISJOINT** | −1.25 %, p=9.7e-3 | −0.36 % | −0.19 % |
| **`stiff-gipc-case2`, pooled** | **16** | 157.1518 → **155.0242** | **−1.35 %, t=8.16, p=4.1e-9** | **−1.10 %, t=8.47, p=2.2e-9** | −1.17 %, p=3.8e-5 | −0.26 % (p=0.002) | −0.19 % (p=0.64) |
| `cube-wall-cloth` (control) | 6 | 55.8396 → 55.7468 | −0.17 %, p=0.92 | **+0.05 %, p=0.93** | −0.63 %, p=0.11 | −0.23 % | +0.46 % |
| `rigid-wrecking-balls` (control) | 6 | 25.4171 → 25.4194 | +0.01 %, p=0.99 | +0.68 %, p=0.18, **guard fired** (Newton −0.67 %, PCG −0.84 %) | +0.85 % | −0.67 % | −0.84 % |

Both targeted scenes land on their predictions, `mas-bunny` disjoint at n=10 with every count identical,
case2 at −1.10 % ms/Newton pooled over 16 runs per arm (t=8.5), with the second sweep disjoint on both the mean and ms/Newton; its pooled Newton count drifts −0.26 % (p=0.002), a quarter of the effect and — since the kernel computes the same values as main's for every slot whose value is defined — the scene's own trajectory scatter, so ms/Newton is the statistic to read (the raw mean, −1.35 %, carries some of that drift). Peak GPU memory is unchanged in both arms (`gpuMemory.peakTotalMiB` case2 1888–1918 old vs 1895–1914 new, `mas-bunny` 1253 in all 20 runs): the default path allocates nothing new The two controls are flat: cube-wall-cloth's ms/Newton is +0.05 % (p=0.93)
inside its ±0.54 % envelope, and rwb's wall is unreadable by the harness's own guard (its counts moved
0.7–0.8 % against a 0.01 % wall change — trajectory scatter on a scene where the kernel is ~5 µs per
launch and 179 of its 180 warps skip the tree), reported as flat, not as a win. **No scene regresses.**

### Gates

- `gate.sh` vs `baseline_tests.txt`, default arm (`=1`): **identical counts** — common 11/3, core 1112/36, geometry 2730/46, sanity_check 100/3, regression 4/1, backend_cuda 448/23, sim_case **14213/95**, pytest 48 passed 1 skipped (`gate_default.txt`, also `/workspace/output/round6/gate_after_s17.txt`); the only textual difference from the baseline file is pytest's wall time. The `=0` arm executes main's device
  functions (SASS identity above) and the untouched host branch, so its gate is s16's.
- `pgrep -af` clean at the end of the step; tree returned to `main`, `build-perf` rebuilt from main.
- Tumbler `--verify` not run: the kernel computes the same values as main's for every slot whose value is
  defined (proved above on the tumbler's own population, 3 985 200 words, 0 mismatches), so a trajectory
  audit has nothing to find; the tumbler was also not benchmarked (predicted −0.55 % against a ~4 %
  resolution). Stated as a limit.

### Transfer prediction

Category (PERF_METHOD §6): **algorithmic — fewer FP64 warp instructions for the same bytes, same launch
geometry, same values.** Not wave quantisation (grid unchanged), not spill (0 B stack), not an occupancy
effect (same register granule; measured 46 → 48). Two things move the *size* on another part: (1) FP64 rate
— a 1/32-rate part over-rewards FP64 removal, and a 1/64-rate consumer Blackwell would reward it *more* per
instruction while its ~4x bandwidth shrinks the gather side, so the kernel there is more tree-bound than
here and the fraction should be at least as large; (2) the level histogram is a property of the mesh, not
the GPU (3.97 / 3.93 mean levels), so the 21 % of FP64 removed is the same everywhere. **Sign transfers;
the fraction is expected to hold or grow.** The number a rented run should compare is the per-launch pair
in `scope.md` plus the Probe=1 floor, which says how far from gather-bound the kernel is on that part.

### Limits, stated plainly

- Scope numbers are single sessions per arm; every cross-arm wall claim is from the `ab.py` sweeps.
- The dytopo manager's non-fused `convert()` and `external_articulation_constraint` reach the same host
  function and therefore the k2 kernel, but neither runs on the fast path of any suite scene (case2 has
  zero `distribute_*` launches, s14); they are covered by the SASS identity and the shared code, not by a
  timing.
- The 48-register k2 kernel is 1 359 static instructions from the unrolled early-exit; a `#pragma unroll 1`
  shape was not tried (it would trade the duplicated tails for a loop-carried offset).
- `clang-format` is still not installed on this box; the style was kept by hand.

## Candidates for the next step

| candidate | measured evidence | where to gate it |
|---|---|---|
| **The CUB radix sort behind the reduce: 2.94 % of case2 / 2.68 % of `mas-bunny`, and its key is twice as wide as it needs to be** | `nsys/head_c2.csv`: the onesweep passes total **1180 ms / 1664 converts = 709 µs per convert** on case2, more than every other converter kernel combined. The key is `uint64 row * cols + col` sorted over `bit_width(rows * cols − 1)` bits (`matrix_converter.inl`, `matrix_converter_key_bits`), with `SortPairs<uint64, int>`; for every suite scene `rows * cols < 2^32` (case2's blocked DoF count is ~40 k), so a **32-bit key** halves the key bytes each pass moves (12 → 8 B per pair per pass) at the same 4 passes. Bit-identical by construction (a permutation). Beyond that, round 4's s10 rejection records the *stable-prefix* cache (case2 92–96 % of the pattern unchanged between iterations) as still open — it would remove the sort, not shrink it | case2 (2.9 %) + `mas-bunny` |
| **The reduce is gather-bound now: 862 µs of its 915 are the random 72-byte gather through `perm`** | Probe=1 vs Probe=2 above. Each lane's 9 x 8 B block at 8-byte alignment always spans **3 sectors (96 B for 72 B, 33 % waste)**, and the source order is the assembly's element order, so locality is the mesh's. Two shapes, both bit-identical: (a) gather in the *sorted* order once per Newton iteration only where the pattern is stable (the same s10 prefix finding), (b) an element/vertex ordering pass on load (RCM-style) so that consecutive sorted keys read nearby source blocks — a scene-preprocessing candidate whose ceiling is the 862 → ~500 µs DRAM floor | case2 per launch; needs a locality probe first |
| **`bcoo_A` reserved from the raw triplet count x 1.5 (~180 MB on case2) by a resize ordering, not a scratch dependency** | this step's premise check: `convert_sym` step 5 resizes `to` to `m` before the RLE; moving that after the RLE and reserving `bcoo_A` from a reduced high-water mark is a footprint cleanup, **not a speed step** (case2 peaks at 1968 MiB of 8192; s13's fitted grid already removes the idle SpMV blocks, and would still be needed at a 1.5x reduced reservation) | memory only; `run_benchmark.py`'s `gpuMemory.peakTotalMiB` |
| **rwb has no duplicate triplets: the whole sort + RLE + reduce there is a permutation of 5 740 entries** | `verify/new_vs_old_rwb.txt`: in = out = 5 740, 179 of 180 warps skip the tree. The chain is ~0.3 % of rwb, so this is a note, not a target — unless a future ABD scene is large | — |
| ~~the reduce's warp tree~~ | **done — this step.** Ceiling for anything further on the tree: 5.9 % of the kernel (Probe=1) | — |

### Found outside this step's area (reported, not fixed)

- **The brief's `matrix_converter.inl:485` reading is a resize, not a scratch use** (above); s13's candidate 4
  should be re-worded before anyone builds "its own sort scratch" — the converter already has one.
- **`FastSegmentalReduce`'s ragged tail does one pointless atomic per launch** whenever `in_size % 32 != 0`
  (the s32 comment already records it); harmless, unchanged here, and the k2 kernel reproduces it exactly
  because it must.
- `clang-format` still not installed on this box (s04–s16 recorded the same).

## V3 — the round's final evaluation: base `60af65e6` against head `6b1e21cb`, two builds on one box, interleaved — **tumbler-garments −27.1 % (n=20, disjoint, p=8e-20), stiff-gipc-case2 −16.7 %, cube-wall-cloth −18.4 %, rigid-wrecking-balls −11.1 %, mas-bunny −7.9 %**, all five disjoint at n=5, Newton counts within +1.5 % / −2.7 % of base, the gate identical to `baseline_tests.txt`, and every one of the thirteen switches proved live on the binary

Optimization stopped at s17. This pass measures the composition of the twelve accepted steps
(s02, s04, s06, s07/V2, s08, s10, s11, s13, s14, s15, s16, s17) as one number per scene, base
tree against head tree, and re-checks what the round's own instruments say about the head. Evidence
under `agent_docs/performance/data/2026-09-14-round6-final/`; scratch under
`/workspace/output/round6/v3/`.

### The base tree, built the way the head was built — and checked before it was trusted

Every A/B in this round was an env switch inside one binary. A base-vs-head comparison needs a
second binary, and PERF_METHOD §6 is explicit that it must be built the same way. The recipe is the
showcase's upstream comparison, mirrored:

- **worktree** `/workspace/output/round6/base/wt` at tag `perf-round6-base` = `60af65e6`, submodules
  at the tag's pointers (`libuipc-samples` `ecad2ca1`, `SymEigen` `a12c56e3`), tree clean;
- **build** `build-base/` configured by `configure_base.sh`, a copy of `configure_perf.sh` with only
  the source tree, the build directory and the python changed: Release, `UIPC_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=75`,
  tests ON, examples/benchmarks OFF, `UIPC_DEV_MODE=ON`, same nvcc (`cuda-12.8`), same host compiler
  (`/usr/bin/c++`), same ninja. The **CMakeCache flags diff against `build-perf` is empty** except
  `UIPC_PYTHON_EXECUTABLE_PATH` (`cache_perf.txt` / `cache_base.txt`): `-O3 -DNDEBUG` for both C++ and
  CUDA, architecture 75, same toolchain file;
- **vcpkg tree**: libuipc's `UIPC_USING_LOCAL_VCPKG` logic forces `VCPKG_INSTALLED_DIR` to
  `<build>/vcpkg_installed` and re-runs `vcpkg install` on a fresh build directory, which fails in
  this container (the known `No such file or directory` in `commands.install.cpp`, `configure.log`).
  As the showcase does, `build-perf/vcpkg_installed` was copied into `build-base/vcpkg_installed`
  — **every file md5-identical** (`VCPKG-TREE-MD5-IDENTICAL`), the generated `vcpkg.json` and
  `vcpkg-configuration.json` byte-identical — and the second configure saw `VCPKG_MANIFEST_CHANGED=0`.
  So both binaries link the same Eigen, fmt, spdlog, cpptrace, TBB objects. (`build-perf`'s own
  cache also points at `build-perf/vcpkg_installed`, not `build-dahl`'s as `configure_perf.sh` asks
  for — the project overrides the flag; the trees are identical anyway.)
- **venv** `/workspace/output/round6/base/venv`, `/usr/bin/python3.11` like `uipc-perf-env`, holding
  numpy 2.4.6 / pybind11 3.1.0 / pybind11-stubgen 2.5.5 and the base build's `pyuipc` (installed by
  the build's own `after_build_pyuipc.py` step). Full build: 721 steps, 20 jobs, ~11 min.

Two checks before a single comparison run (brief rule 2, §2.8 in the other direction):

| check | result |
|---|---|
| the two binaries differ where the round says they should (`nm -C libuipc_backend_cuda.so`) | `fused_R_kernel` 0 / 4, `rowdot2_kernel` 0 / 6, `reduce_matrix_k2_kernel` 0 / 12, `abd_gh_prepass_verify` 0 / 5, `do_assemble_kernel` instantiations 52 / 88, `filter_toi_k1_kernel` 8 / 14 (base / head) |
| the base build reproduces the coordinator's base-tag runs (`base-sanity/`, one run per scene, base venv) | `mas-bunny` **67.32 vs 67.31 ms** (+0.01 %, Newton 465 both, peak 864 MiB both — the deterministic scene, so this is the sharp check); `stiff-gipc-case2` 186.58 vs 186.78 (−0.11 %, Newton 1661 vs 1659); `cube-wall-cloth` 69.41 vs 69.67 (−0.38 %); `rigid-wrecking-balls` 27.80 vs 28.88 (−3.75 %, a 4-second scene with a ±2 % null and a cold first run on the coordinator's side); `tumbler-garments` 148.97 vs s00's n=5 150.94 (−1.3 %, inside the scene's ±3–5 %), peak 1780 vs 1793 MiB |

Nothing differs that should not, and the binaries are the ones the round describes. `build-perf`,
`uipc-perf-env`, `build-dahl` and `uipc-dahl-env` were not touched; `build-perf` at the end of the
pass is `ninja: no work to do` from `main`.

### The harness: `ab2.py`, the two-build equivalent of `ab.py`

`ab.py` varies an env switch inside one build; `ab2.py` (archived) varies the **python interpreter**
— each arm is a venv holding one build's `pyuipc` — and both arms run the **same scene code from the
same checkout** (`main`'s `libuipc-samples` at `6764ad33`, which differs from the base tag's pointer
only by the tumbler scene itself and an opt-in `UIPC_CWC_TIGHT` that no benchmark sets) through
`scripts/run_benchmark.py`, so the workload is identical and only the engine differs. It keeps
`ab.py`'s statistics and ordering (one discarded warm-up per arm, then **ABBA** blocks; Welch's t;
disjointness; the iteration-count guard) and its instrument checks — `returnCode` 0 and the
manifest's frame count on every run, the driver checkout not moving — and adds three of its own:
the metadata's `python` must be the arm's interpreter; the md5 of every `.so` in each arm's
`_native/` directory is taken before and after the sweep and must not change; and the two arms'
libraries must differ from each other (a null would otherwise pass silently). All checks passed on
all five sweeps; the fingerprints are in each `*_summary.json`. The Mann–Whitney U p-value is
reported beside Welch's t because the tumbler's per-run distribution is not normal.

official scenes at n=5 per arm; manifest frame counts throughout. 80 measured runs plus 10 warm-ups,
in one sitting (16:27–16:57), nothing else on the GPU (`pgrep` checked; the s00 poller is gone).

Reading it, scene by scene:

  −1.0 % (p = 0.17, inside its run-to-run spread), so the −16.7 % is throughput. The product of the
  step claims (s02 −6.43, s04 −1.54, s06 −2.02, s08 −0.4, s13 −0.93, s14 −1.26, s15 −1.14, s16
  −2.36, s17 −1.10) is **−16.0 %**; measured −16.7 %. Peak memory −160 MiB, disjoint.
- **`cube-wall-cloth`**: −18.4 % mean / **−19.7 % ms/Newton**; Newton +1.5 % (p = 0.23) — the
  direction V1 measured for s02's Gauss-Newton Hessian (+3.2 % on the tumbler; smaller here), and
  the head arm carries the scene's Newton tail this time (max 528 vs base 509; s07 and V2 saw the
  same tail land on every arm including the exact one). Claims product (s02 −11.83, s07/V2 −3.81,
  s08 −0.98, s11 −2.13): **−17.8 %**; measured −18.4 %. The wider head range
  [54.4–59.7] against base [68.6–70.4] is this scene's known Newton-driven scatter.
- **`tumbler-garments`**, the round's target: **−27.1 % mean, −27.9 % median, −28.0 % ms/Newton,
  −29.1 % ms/PCG**, disjoint at n = 20 with the worst head run (126.9) 6 % faster than the best base
  run (135.4). Newton **+1.21 %** (p = 0.135) and PCG **+2.84 %** (p = 0.224), overlapping: the same
  sign and size V1 measured for the Gauss-Newton direction (+3.2 % Newton / +5.5 % PCG at n = 10)
  and V2's "nothing systematic" at n = 20; the two algorithmic approximations (s02's hinge, s07/V2's
  PE+PP contact Hessian) cost ~1–3 % more iterations and that is inside the −27 %, not hidden by it.
  The product of the individual tumbler claims (s02 −19.2, s04 −3.86, s06 −2.06, s07 −4.37, s10
  −2.33, most of them individually "overlapping" on this scene) is −28.9 %; measured −27.1 %, with
  the +1.2 % Newton drift accounting for most of the gap. **Peak GPU memory** 1631 → 1205 MiB on
  the mean (−26 %), but this statistic is a 0.2-s `nvidia-smi` sample of a transient — the base arm
  ranges 1218–2336 MiB and the head arm 1076–1460 across identical runs — so it is the *envelope*
  that moved, not a fixed number: the head's worst run (1460 MiB) is below the base's median
  (1541). On an 8 GB card that is the deliverable s02 promised.
- **`rigid-wrecking-balls`** is the one scene where the composition is **larger than the steps
  claimed**: −11.1 % mean, −8.2 % median, **−8.6 % ms/Newton**, against a claims product of −5.5 %
  (s10/s11 −4.82, s14 −0.67). Two things make up the difference and both are stated rather than
  smoothed over. First, **the head runs fewer iterations on this scene**: Newton −2.72 % (p = 0.019)
  and PCG −2.62 % (p = 0.005) at n = 5, line search −2.2 %. s07 reported rwb Newton −1.09 % (p =
  0.046) at n = 15 for the contact projection change and V2 shipped it; the round's measured rwb
  Newton null is ±1.5 % (s07); so a −2.7 % count shift with p = 0.02 at n = 5 is at the edge of what this scene
  resolves, in the direction the round already recorded, and it is why the mean (−11.1 %) moves
  more than ms/Newton (−8.6 %). Second, ms/Newton itself is 3 pp beyond the claims: the round
  measured rwb at n = 5–8 on six steps that touch it (s04, s06, s07, s08, s13, s17) and called each
  "unresolvable" or "flat" against the scene's ±2 % null — six sub-resolution effects of the same
  sign add up to something a base-vs-head comparison can see. The disjoint −7.1 % peak-memory
  (745 → 692 MiB, tight ranges) is s06's compacted candidate array.

**Sum of the parts vs the whole.** Four of five scenes land within 1 pp of the product of the
steps' own end-to-end claims (mas-bunny −8.2 → −7.9, case2 −16.0 → −16.7, cwc −17.8 → −18.4,
tumbler −28.9 → −27.1); rwb lands 3 pp better than claimed for the reasons above. The round's
per-step numbers compose, which is the check §5 asks for and which nothing before this pass had
made.
